Separately, `src/context/ThemeProvider.jsx` declared its own `LIGHT_THEME`/`DARK_THEME` palette that contradicted the tokens (`background: #050510`/`#F8F9FA` vs `#03071B`/`#F6F8FC`), and that palette is **dead**: no screen consumes `colors` from `useTheme()` (all 8 destructuring sites use a local `colors` object) — the only live consumer is `GlassCard`'s `colors.shadow.card`, which is the same string in both palettes. Fixed: every divergent `rgba()` is folded onto the brand violet `rgba(139,30,243)` (the same value the config's `shadow-arvdoul-glow` now uses), `GlassButton` uses the `bg-arvdoul-gradient` class, the palettes moved to `src/context/themePalettes.js` and derive their surfaces/text from the design tokens, and the token `shadow.glow`/`glowStrong` (which pointed at an unrelated `#8B5CF6`) now point at the brand violet. `designTokenDriftGuard.test.js` was extended to match the rgba form, ban the rogue gradient and assert the palettes stay token-derived; `designTokens.test.js`/`contextContract.test.js` stay green. (ThemeProvider still exports a superset of `useTheme` keys; retiring the dead palette is left as a separate, API-shape change.) |
| N113 every declared feature flag was inert — 13 "features" the admin console could flip with no effect | ✅ **now closed** | `src/shared/featureFlagRegistry.cjs` declared 13 flags, the admin console rendered them, `setFeatureFlagOverride` audited every change, and `featureFlagService` was wired at bootstrap — but **no product surface ever read one**: `isEnabled(` had zero call sites in `src`/`functions` (only its own definition), and there was no `useFeatureFlag` hook. `featureFlagService.test.js` stayed green because it only exercised the service's own read API, never that a screen consumed a flag (the same "impressive-looking but not wired" pattern as N104). Wired every flag that gates a real feature to real code: `feed.ml_ranking` → the `ml_personalized` feed branch (`feedService.getSmartFeed`), `feed.diversity_rerank` → `_applyDiversityOptimized`, `feed.ads` → `_insertMonetizationOptimized`, `messaging.e2ee` → the encrypt/decrypt paths in `messagesService`, `search.vector` → `searchService._vectorSearch` gating, `monetization.pay_per_view` → the PPV unlock gate in `videoService`, `messaging.calls` → the profile call button, `stories.music_library` → the story Music tool, `ai.studio` → the `/ai-studio` route (`FeatureGate`), and `moderation.auto_review` → the server `moderateComment`/`moderateCommentOnUpdate` triggers via a new server-side `isFlagEnabled()` in `functions/featureFlags.js` (override > registry default, fail-closed). Added `src/hooks/useFeatureFlag.js` (reactive) and `src/components/Shared/FeatureGate.jsx`. Removed the four flags whose feature **does not exist** (`live.recording` — no `MediaRecorder` anywhere; `ai.streaming` — the gateway is non-streaming; `admin.analytics_beta` — no dashboard; and their tests) instead of shipping a false claim. Also fixed the profile **call button**, which navigated to `/call/${profileUid}` while `CallScreen`/`callService` expect a `conversationId` (it resolved "Conversation not found" for every call): the button now resolves the direct conversation first, and the dead duplicate `startCall`/`acceptCall`/`endCall` in `messagesService` (callService is canonical) were removed. New `featureFlagConsumption.test.js` (hard-failing `guards` CI job) fails if any declared flag has no `isEnabled`/`isFlagEnabled`/`useFeatureFlag`/`FeatureGate` consumer, so a flag can only be added alongside the code it gates. |
| N105 a sent chat message rendered twice (optimistic bubble never reconciled) | ✅ **now closed** | `ChatScreen` inserted an optimistic bubble, then `reconcileSentMessage` was never called: the pending map entry was `{ conversationId, data }` (no `localId`), so the merge built the bubble with `id: p.localId` → **`undefined`**. `sendMessage` was only awaited. The realtime `subscribeToConversation` emits the sender's own message (`!hasPendingWrites` after commit), and the debounced reload replaced the list with the server page — so the optimistic bubble was dropped, but if the realtime snapshot arrived before the debounced reload, the sent message rendered twice (once optimistic, once server). The optimistic id is now the value used as the Firestore doc id (idempotency key), so `reconcileSentMessage` marks the bubble confirmed in place (same React key), the merge filters out any pending id already present in the server page, and the handler rolls back on failure. Guarded by `reelsAndMessagesWiring.test.js` (fails on `id: p.localId`). |
| N106 `getMessages` filtered by `ttlExpiresAt > now`, hiding any message missing the field | ✅ **now closed** | `_fetchMessagesFromShards` applied `where('ttlExpiresAt', '>', now)`. Every client- and server-written message sets the field, but a message document created by any other path (migration, admin, a future server writer) without it would be permanently invisible while still counting toward the unread badge — a silent "message vanished" trap. The filter is removed; the 365-day TTL is enforced by the existing expiry cleanup, and expired messages are already filtered in-memory where the TTL is meaningful. Guarded by `reelsAndMessagesWiring.test.js`. |
| N107 chat rendered newest-first (message order inverted) | ✅ **now closed** | `_fetchMessagesFromShards` queries `orderBy('createdAt', 'desc')` for backward paging, and `getMessages` returned that order verbatim, but `ChatScreen` (and `messagingStore`) render in array order and prepend older pages. Result: the newest message showed at the TOP, "Load earlier messages" sat above the list and prepended older messages into an already-newest-first array — the whole thread was upside-down. `getMessages` now sorts ascending once (`_messageTime`), so every consumer gets chronological order. Guarded by `reelsAndMessagesWiring.test.js`. |
| N108 the reels Messages badge was a hardcoded "8" | ✅ **now closed** | `ReelsScreen` hardcoded the unread badge as `8`, a fabricated count that never matched the real unread total. The fix had a wrong premise — it called reels "an immersive route *outside* `MainLayout`" — but reels **is** inside `MainLayout` (`isImmersiveVideo` only hides the TopAppBar; `hideBottomNav` is false for reels), so ReelsScreen's inline bar was a *second* bottom nav stacked on the canonical `BottomNav`. N114 removes the inline bar entirely and the badge now lives in `BottomNav` (`useNavBadges`), the single nav surface. Guarded by `reelsAndMessagesWiring.test.js`. |
| N109 the reels header showed a "Following" pill it never resolved | ✅ **now closed** | The header rendered a clickable "Following" chip with an emerald dot and a `navigate('/friends')` action — implying a mutual-friend relationship with the current reel's author that no code ever computed. Removed and replaced with a non-claiming "Swipe up for more" hint. Guarded by `reelsAndMessagesWiring.test.js` (the string and the `ChevronRight` import must not return). |
| N110 reels/videos feeds stopped at one page (pagination cursor ignored) | ✅ **now closed** | `ReelsScreen` fetched `limit: 10` and never paginated, so the feed ended after ten reels. `VideosScreen` DID pass `cursor: nextCursor`, but `videoService.getVideoFeed` destructured only `lastDocSnapshot` and ignored `cursor` — so "load more" silently re-fetched page 1 (the same class of bug as N085). `getVideoFeed` now resolves the opaque cursor id to a document snapshot and `applyCursor`, and `ReelsScreen` prefetches the next page when the viewer nears the end (deduped by id). Guarded by `reelsAndMessagesWiring.test.js`. |
| N111 Tailwind opacity steps 8/12 (and 98) generate no CSS — invisible glass | ✅ **now closed** | `tailwind.config.js` never declared the `8` and `12` opacity steps, and Tailwind only emits a `bg-white/8` / `border-white/12` rule when the step exists in `theme.opacity`. The classes were therefore silent no-ops: **19 glass surfaces rendered fully transparent** — `SearchResults`, `TrendingSection`, `CreatorCarousel`, `videoUtils` glass classes and the ThemeProvider `glass` presets (`bg-white/8 border-white/12`, `bg-white/12`) — with a green build, a passing lint and no warning. `bg-gray-900/98` in `VideoBottomSheet` was the same defect, and `bg-gray-750` in six Admin screens (`AdminEconomyScreen`, `AdminSupportTicketsScreen`, `AdminVerificationScreen`, `AdminAuditLogsScreen`, `AdminCommunityManagementScreen`, `AdminSystemHealthScreen`) was a non-existent gray shade — 11 table-row/panel backgrounds rendered with no fill. Fixed by declaring `opacity: { 8, 12 }` in the config and dropping `/98` to `/95`; the new `tailwindUtilities.test.js` (guards job) scans every color/opacity utility in `src` and every palette shade, failing if either is not declared, so the next invalid step/shade fails CI instead of shipping invisible UI. The same guard found three more silent no-op families: `shadow-arvdoul-glow` (10 FAB/CTA glows — the shadow was never declared), `animate-in`/`fade-in`/`zoom-in-95`/`slide-in-from-bottom-*` (13 modal/message-bubble/error-boundary entrances using the `tailwindcss-animate` plugin API, but the plugin was never installed) and `animate-fade-in`/`animate-fade-in-out`/`animate-slide-up`/`animate-spin-slow` (undefined keyframes). Fixed by declaring the glow shadow, the missing keyframes/animations and a native `animate-in` implementation in `src/styles/tailwind.css` (no new dependency); the guard now also validates every `animate-*` utility and every entrance class. |
| N114 1:1 calls could never connect (two peers, two call docs) | ✅ **now closed** | `callService.createCall` used `addDoc`, so when both peers opened the call screen each created its **own** `calls/{id}` document, subscribed to its own doc, and never saw the other's offer/answer — the call could never connect. Nothing listened for an incoming call either, so a callee only rang if they were already on `/call/:conversationId`. Now `deriveCallId(uidA, uidB)` sorts the two uids so both sides address the same document; `ensureCall` reuses an existing ringing doc (a late joiner must not clobber the in-flight offer) and resets only an ended one; `watchIncomingCalls` + a global `IncomingCallBanner` (mounted in `AppBootstrap`) ring the callee with Accept/Decline. The ring is a bounded `getDocs` poll, not an `onSnapshot`, because the cost guard forbids net-new listeners; the query is `participants array-contains selfUid` so the read rule is provable (a bare `calleeId` filter is an unsafe list query) and `calleeId` is filtered in code. `CallScreen` also now honours `?type=audio` (audio-only `getUserMedia`, no PiP). Guarded by `callFlowWiring.test.js`. |
| N115 the reels screen drew a second bottom nav (stacked on the canonical one) | ✅ **now closed** | The user report "reels doesn't look Arvdoul level" had a structural cause: `ReelsScreen` rendered its own inline `<nav>` (Home/Friends/+/Messages/Profile) while `MainLayout` still mounted the canonical `BottomNav` on `/reels`, so the screen showed **two** stacked bottom bars. The inline bar also hardcoded `/friends` and `/create-story` and carried a duplicate unread badge. The inline nav is removed; `BottomNav` is the single nav surface and owns the live badge (`useNavBadges`). Guarded by `reelsAndMessagesWiring.test.js`. |
| N116 the reels root used `h-screen` inside MainLayout's full-screen flex container | ✅ **now closed** | `ReelsScreen`'s root was `h-screen` while `MainLayout` already gives it a full-height `flex-1` box, so the screen was `100vh` tall inside a shorter flex area — the bottom controls overflowed past the viewport and were clipped. Now `h-full`, matching the sibling `/videos` immersive feed (`relative w-full h-full bg-black overflow-hidden`). |
| N117 the reels accents were an off-brand Tailwind ramp | ✅ **now closed** | Every reel accent used stock Tailwind ramps (`violet-600`/`pink-500`/`purple-600`/`indigo-600`/`cyan-400`) instead of the signature brand family, so the screen did not read as Arvdoul. Folded onto `#8B1EF3 → #4431F7 → #055BFB` (plus brand pink `#C82BFF` and cyan `#0088FF`). |
| N136 /edit/:postId never read (edit created duplicates) | ✅ **now closed** | `PostOptionsDrawer` "Edit" navigates to `/edit/:postId`, but `EditPostLoader` only read `?edit=` and the reducer's `editPostId` was stored and never consumed, so editing a post opened an empty composer and published a *duplicate*. `EditPostLoader` now reads the route param and `publishPost` branches on `editPostId` to call `firestoreService.updatePost` and return to `/post/:id`. |
| N137 /event/:eventId/edit never read (edit created duplicates) | ✅ **now closed** | `EventDetailScreen` "Edit" navigates to `/event/:eventId/edit`, but `CreateEventScreen` ignored `eventId` and always created a new event. It now reads the param, prefills from `eventService.getEvent`, and calls `updateEvent` instead of `createEvent`. Guarded in `routeParamWiringGuard.test.js`. |
| N135 community compose ignored ?community= | ✅ **now closed** | `CommunityDetailScreen` links to `/create-post?community=<id>`, but `CreatePost` never read the param: a post composed from a community was written straight to `posts` via `firestoreService.createPost` with the composer's own visibility, landed in the public home feed, and the server-authoritative `createCommunityPost` callable (membership + content-approval + `stats.postCount`/`activityScore`) was never invoked — so `communityService.createCommunityPost` had no client caller at all. The provider now reads the param, forces `visibility: 'community'`, publishes through `createCommunityPost`, returns to `/community/<id>`, bakes the id into offline-queue records for replay, and HomeScreen/feedService keep optimistic community posts out of the public feed. Guarded in `highlightsWiringGuard.test.js`. |
| N133 dead props passed but never consumed | ✅ **now closed** | `PostOptionsDrawer` received `navigate` it never destructured (builds its own via `useNavigate`), `TrendingSection`/`CreatorCarousel` received `isDark` (both read `useTheme()`), and `VideoCreatorInfo` received `theme` (reads `useTheme()`); the callers looked wired while the value was silently dropped. Removed the dead props and added `deadPropsGuard.test.js` — an import-resolving scan that compares every JSX attribute against the callee's destructured params — to the hard-failing `guards` CI job. |
| N134 "Hide this ad" hid nothing | ✅ **now closed** | `SponsoredPostCard.handleHideAd` called `onAdHidden`, but no caller passes it, so the menu item dismissed only the popover and the ad stayed put (and reloaded unchanged). The card now records the hidden creative id per account in localStorage, filters it on load, and clears itself on hide — a real, persistent hide. |
| N132 highlight links opened the generic story grid, never the highlight | ✅ **now closed** | `HighlightsScreen` navigated to `/highlight/{id}` and `ProfileHighlightsSection` to `/stories?highlight={id}`, both routed to `StoriesScreen` — but `StoriesScreen` read neither the `:highlightId` route param nor the `highlight` query param, so every highlight link dropped the viewer on the full story grid (the highlight's own stories were never shown, and the deep link silently degraded to a generic feed). `StoriesScreen` now resolves both forms, loads the ordered stories via `storyService.getHighlightStories`, builds the viewer group and opens the viewer on it (prepended into the filtered list). Guarded by `highlightsWiringGuard.test.js`. |
| N118 the profile screen crashed with "something went wrong" (render-time TDZ) | ✅ **now closed** | A flag-wiring edit moved `handleStartCall`'s `useCallback` **above** the `const profileUid = …` it depends on, so the dependency array referenced `profileUid` while it was still in its temporal dead zone — every render threw `ReferenceError: Cannot access 'profileUid' before initialization` and the profile route hit the error boundary. `profileUid` is now declared before the callback. A `no-use-before-define` sweep of `src` found no other render-time instance (the rest are deferred callbacks). |
| N081 post-embedded poll voting was client-authoritative (forgeable counts, no expiry, broken multi-vote) | ✅ **now closed** | `firestoreService.voteOnPoll` mutated `posts/{id}.poll` counters inside a client transaction, but the posts update rule lets ANY signed-in user change `poll` — so vote counts were forgeable, there was no server-side expiry check (`closesAt` ignored), and the `isMultiple` branch incremented an option count without recording a vote (unlimited inflation on re-tap). The composer also wrote `rewardCoins` but nothing ever credited it, while `PollCard` toasted `+N coins!` anyway (a false claim). Voting now goes through the `functions/polls.js` `votePostPoll` callable (exported + merged, `VOTE_POST_POLL`): it validates eligibility/expiry/option, records one vote per (viewer, option) — keyed on `uid` for single-choice, `uid_optionId` for multi — credits the reward exactly once per viewer via a server-only marker doc, awards `civic_vote_cast` XP, and writes the reward through the double-entry ledger. `poll_votes` writes are now denied to clients entirely; the reward toast fires only on the first vote. Guarded by `integrationWiring.test.js`. |
| N082 CreatorPayoutScreen fabricated the earnings split and lifetime total | ✅ **now closed** | The breakdown cards rendered `(balance * 0.7)` and `(balance * 0.3)` as "Gifts & Tips" / "Ad Revenue Share" and a hardcoded `$0.00` "Lifetime Withdrawn" — a fixed ratio and a fixed zero that no server ever produced (the coin ledger has no 70/30 split; the real reasons are `gift` and `ad_reward`). The cards now sum real `coin_transactions` by reason via `getCoinEarningHistory`/`getCoinSpendingHistory` (`sumCredits`), and the lifetime total converts actual `withdrawal_*` debits through the shared `formatCoinsAsUsd`. Guarded by `noFabricatedData.test.js`. |
| N083 the composer offered visibility scopes the rules could not honour | ✅ **now closed** | `VISIBILITY_OPTIONS` offered `close_friends`, `subscribers` and `custom_list`, but there is no close-friend list, no subscriber→creator edge and no custom-list collection anywhere (no management UI, no rules, no data model) — so a post set to one of those scopes was readable by NOBODY (the strict get rule admits only public/followers/friends/community/author), a silent "published but invisible" trap. `friends` was also honored only as a plain follow edge (a mutual-follow friend is the app's real model). The rule now admits `friends` only on a mutual follow (`followEdgeExists` both ways), and the composer offers only the scopes the rule can honour (public / followers / friends / only_me); the unsupported three are withheld until a backing model exists. Guarded by `postVisibilityEnforcement.test.js` (asserts every offered scope appears in the rule and the three unsupported ones are gone). |
| N084 mildly-flagged comments vanished from the thread | ✅ **now closed** | `commentService.getCommentsByPost` filtered `moderationStatus in ['approved', 'pending']`. A newly created comment starts `pending`; the async `moderateComment` trigger then writes `pending_manual_review` (with `isHidden=false`) for a low toxicity score — a state the query did NOT include, so a slightly-flagged comment silently disappeared from the thread the moment Perspective scanned it (the comment *count* still included it, so the thread header disagreed with the list). The visible set is now the shared `VISIBLE_COMMENT_MODERATION_STATUSES = ['approved','pending','pending_manual_review']` (only `rejected`/`removed` hide, and those are always written with `isHidden=true`). Guarded by `integrationWiring.test.js`. |
| N085 "load more replies" silently re-fetched page 1 | ✅ **now closed** | `CommentsDrawer.loadReplies` passes the pagination cursor as `startAfter`, but `commentService.getReplies` read only `options.cursor` — so every "load more replies" click re-queried the first page (deduped against the already-loaded ids, so nothing new ever appeared and replies beyond the first page were unreachable). `getReplies` now accepts `startAfter || cursor`, matching `getPostsByUser`. Guarded by `integrationWiring.test.js`. |
| N086 SearchScreen creator "Follow" was a `console.log` no-op | ✅ **now closed** | `CreatorCarousel` renders a real Follow button and calls `onFollow(creatorId)`, and `SearchScreen` passed `handleFollow`, but that handler was `console.log('Follow creator:', creatorId)` — following a recommended creator did nothing (and no state changed). Every other surface (`ReelsScreen`, `PostCard`, `ConnectionsScreen`, `PostOptionsDrawer`, `NotificationsScreen`) calls `userService.followUser`. `handleFollow` now calls `followUser`/`unfollowUser` with an optimistic carousel update and rollback on failure, and the dead `default: console.log('Navigate to:')` branch in `handleResultClick` was removed. This required exposing `setTrending` from `useSearch()` — caught by `hookContract.test.js`, which fails when a screen destructures a key its hook does not return. Guarded by `integrationWiring.test.js` + `hookContract.test.js`. |
| N138 the entire posts `list` rule was invalid (`request.query.get()` is not a Firestore API) — every public feed, profile grid and community feed query was denied | ✅ **now closed** | `postQueryConstrainsPublic`/`postQueryConstrainsOwn`/`postQueryConstrainsViewableCommunity` gated `allow list` on `request.query.get(field, sentinel)`, but Cloud Firestore's `request.query` exposes only `limit`, `offset` and `orderBy` — there is no `request.query.get()`. The expression therefore always evaluated against the sentinel (`'__none__'`), so `postListableByViewer()` was false for every non-admin and **every** feed `list` was rejected with `PERMISSION_DENIED` (the home feed's `_publicPostFilters()` query, the profile grid, the community feed, search). `postVisibilityEnforcement.test.js` stayed green because it only asserted the helper *text* existed, never that a query succeeded against the emulator. Fixed: the list rule now authorises per candidate document (`resource.data.authorId == uid() || postListableShape()`), which is the correct "rules are not filters" contract — an author reads their own posts, everyone else only a published/non-deleted/public/non-private (or public-community) post, and an unconstrained query is denied wholesale. Verified against the Firestore emulator for all five real query shapes (public feed, own drafts, viewer-of-public-profile, community feed, following `in`-query) plus the deny cases; live coverage added to `firestoreRulesCompile.test.js`. |
| N139 group invite links were uncreatable (rule nested under the wrong collection) | ✅ **now closed** | `group_invites` had a `match` block nested under `conversations/{id}`, but `messagesService.createInviteLink`/`listInviteLinks` read and write the **top-level** `group_invites` collection, so every create fell through to the deny-all catch-all and "create invite link" silently failed (no error surfaced to the admin). The rule is now top-level: `allow get` for any signed-in user (the unguessable id is the bearer token in the link), `allow list` gated on the document's own `conversationId` admin, admin-only create/update/delete. Added the `joinGroupViaInvite` callable (a client transaction cannot add a non-admin to `participants`), `JoinGroupScreen`, and the `/join-group?invite=` route. Guarded by the emulator test in `firestoreRulesCompile.test.js`; the coverage guard was also fixed to count only true top-level `match` blocks. |
| N140 the `group_invites` list rule read the query filter via `request.query.get()` (same invalid API as N138) | ✅ **now closed** | The `allow list` clause I added initially used `request.query.get('conversationId', '__none__')`, so the admin management view (`listInviteLinks`) was denied. Rewritten to authorise on `resource.data.conversationId`, so only invites of conversations the caller administers are returned and the collection still cannot be enumerated. Verified in the emulator (admin list succeeds, non-admin list denied). |
| N141 the `stories` read rule could never satisfy a `list` query (`resource.data` is only the query-constrained subset on `list`) | ✅ **now closed** | `stories` used one combined `allow read: if … vibeIsActive(resource.data) && vibeVisibleToViewer(resource.data, uid()) && vibeNotBlocked(resource.data, uid())`. On a `list` (query) evaluation, `resource.data` exposes **only the fields the query constrains**, so any field the rule reads but the query omits is `undefined` and the read throws (`Property isDeleted is undefined on object`), denying the whole list. The only reachable client list is the owner's own grid (`getUserStories` → `where('userId','==',me).where('isDeleted','==',false)`, used by `HighlightsScreen`); it was denied even for the owner. Fixed by splitting `allow get` (full per-document decision, unchanged) from `allow list: if resource.data.userId == uid()` (owner grid only). A public-discovery list is intentionally not granted — it would need the full constraint set and the only callers (`searchStories`, `getSponsoredStories`) are dead. Verified in the emulator (owner grid succeeds, stranger grid denied, point `get` of a public story still succeeds); live coverage added to `firestoreRulesCompile.test.js`. |
| N142 silent write failures — data-layer writes swallowed by an empty `catch` | ✅ **now closed** | Six data-layer writes were wrapped in `try { … } catch (e) {}` (or a comment-only catch) with no log and no rethrow, so a rules/quota/offline failure was indistinguishable from success: `feedService.updateUserInterestVector` (interest vector never stored), `feedService._saveSessionState` (seen/hidden posts never remembered), `firestoreService.updateUserPostCount` (denormalised count drifted silently), `messagesService._recordRateLimit` (send throttle silently unenforced), `userService.updateLastActive` (presence stale), `monetizationService` outbox backup (durable backup lost). `commentService._checkSpamRate`'s advisory counter also failed closed to "allow" with no trace. Each now logs through the shared `Logger`/service child logger before returning its best-effort fallback, so the failure is observable. Guarded by `silentWriteFailureGuard.test.js` (scans `src/services|utils|store|offline|firebase`; fails on any write-path catch that is empty or has no log/rethrow), added to the hard-failing `guards` CI job. |
| N143 push token acquisition registered a non-existent service worker (`sw-notifications.js`) | ✅ **now closed** | `notificationsService.requestPushPermission` registered `${base}sw-notifications.js`, which does not exist in `public/` (only `sw.js` and `firebase-messaging-sw.js` do). The second `register()` rejected and an empty `catch (err) {}` hid it, so the flow proceeded to `getToken()` against a worker that was never installed — and the failure was invisible. Removed the phantom registration (the real FCM worker `firebase-messaging-sw.js` carries the `push`/`notificationclick` handlers and is what `getToken()` binds to) and the failure is now logged through `logger.warn`. Guarded by the SW-registration assertions in `noFabricatedData.test.js`. |
| N144 `notification_counters` read rule keyed on the wrong document-id shape — the owner's own unread counter was always denied | ✅ **now closed** | The collection stores one document per shard with id `{userId}_shard_{n}` (`notificationsService._getTotalUnreadCount` / `getNotificationStats` / `subscribeToNotificationCount` all build `${userId}_shard_${i}`), but the rule was `allow read: if isOwner(userId)` — i.e. `uid() == "<uid>_shard_3"`, which is never true. Firestore rules are not filters, so every client read of the counter was denied (and swallowed by the `onSnapshot` error callback / the `getDoc` loop), leaving the Alerts badge and the stats call silently reading zero. The rule now authorises on the id prefix (`counterId.matches(uid() + '_shard_[0-9]+')`; `matches()` requires a full-string match, so the trailing `[0-9]+` is required and `startsWith` was avoided because its availability in this ruleset is unverified) and denies all client writes (the callables own the counters, as before). Guarded by `notificationCounterRuleShape.test.js` (static contract always; owner-allowed / stranger-denied adversarial check when an emulator is reachable), added to the hard-failing `guards` CI job. |
| N145 screens rendered a misleading **empty** state when a data read *failed* — a catch reset the list to `[]` (often with no log) and the UI then said “No conversations yet” / “No transactions yet” with no error and no retry | ✅ **now closed** | The read path for a list is `load()` → `catch { setList([]) }` → render `list.length === 0 ? <EmptyState/> : …`. A failed read (rules denial, offline, timeout) collapsed into the same branch as a genuinely empty result, so the user was told the platform held no data and was given no way to retry. Repaired on every affected surface: `MessagingScreen` (conversations + message requests), `StoriesScreen`, `ReelsScreen`, `NewConversationScreen` (friends + search), `VideoAnalyticsScreen`, `Profile/HighlightsScreen`, `CoinsScreen` (transactions), `Help/HelpCenterScreen` (tickets), `Admin/AdminEconomyScreen` (payout queue + ledger), `GroupInfoScreen` (member search), `CreateStory` (music catalog), `CreatePost` (balance loading-vs-failed), `VideoGiftModal` (balance). Each now keeps a distinct error state, renders an `ErrorState`/inline error, and offers retry (or distinguishes “failed” from “still loading” where the value is a scalar). Best-effort reads that legitimately fall back to empty (`InFeedStoriesModule`, `CreatePost` geocoding, `LiveScreen` gift config, `CoinsScreen`/`HomeScreen` ad fetch, `ConflictResolutionScreen`, `GIFPicker`) now log the failure so it is observable rather than silent. Guarded by `silentReadFailureGuard.test.js` (static: fails on any screen/component catch that empties a list with no log/error/toast/rethrow; plus pins for each fixed site), added to the hard-failing `guards` CI job. |
| N146 `feedService.getSmartFeed` reported a *failed* generation as `success: true` with an empty feed, and the per-source reads swallowed their errors | ✅ **now closed** | When generation threw, `getSmartFeed` returned `{ success: true, feed: <fallback>, metadata: { isFallback: true } }`. If the fallback was also empty the caller saw a successful, empty feed and `HomeScreen` rendered the “no posts yet” empty state — a failed read presented as a legitimate empty one, with no error and no retry. `success` is now `posts.length > 0` (the fallback posts are still served and still marked `isFallback`), so an empty fallback propagates as a failure the UI already handles (`result.success === false` → error + retry). The per-source readers (`_getFollowingFeedEfficient`, `_getLatestFeed`, `_getDiscoverFeedPaginated`, `_getSponsoredPosts`, `_getAdOptimized`) returned `[]`/`null` from a bare `catch`; each now logs through the shared `Logger` so a degraded source is diagnosable. Same treatment for the silent `null`/`[]` read returns in `searchService` (sponsored result, profile lookup), `monetizationService` (sponsored search result) and `messagesService` (conversation-list ad). |
| N147 best-effort side effects failed silently — a bare `.catch(() => {})` on a server-owned write hid a failure | ✅ **now closed** | A fire-and-forget mutation whose failure is user-visible (a server-owned view/share counter, `markConversationAsRead`, story completion, `daily_login`/`live_minute`/`follow_received` XP, the `active_day` ledger, a comment/friend notification, the profile-complete flag) was fired with a bare `.catch(() => {})`, so the side effect simply never happened with no trace: the count stayed frozen, the badge stayed stale, the recipient was never notified. Every such call now logs (view/share counters in `VideoDetailScreen`, `VideoFeed`, `ReelsScreen`, `ProfileFeedGrid`, `PostOptionsDrawer`; `markConversationAsRead` in `ChatScreen`; story completion in `StoriesScreen`; XP/ledger/notifications in `AuthContext`, `userService`, `liveService`, `firestoreService`, `storyService`; the comment notification in `CommentsDrawer`; the paid-boost spend in `CreatePost`). `GIFPicker` also now surfaces a GIF-search outage instead of an empty grid that reads as “no matches”. The `silentWriteFailureGuard` (data layer) and `silentReadFailureGuard` (list reads) already cover their halves; this sweep closes the fire-and-forget call site half. |
| N148 silent catches survived on the **server** and in profile/auth sync paths — a failure was indistinguishable from success | ✅ **now closed** | The N142/N147 sweeps covered the client; the `functions/` runtime and several client sync paths still swallowed failures with no log. Fixed: `auth.isAdmin` (a failed `admins/{uid}` read failed closed with no trace — an admin locked out by a transient error was undiagnosable), `auth.getUserEmail` (both read failures), `moderation` reportCount increment (a genuine write failure was indistinguishable from a deleted target), `stories` rejected/explicit/expired media-delete failures (orphaned Storage objects), `user.deleteUserData` push-token cleanup, `monetization` ad-targeting profile read, `authService` server rate-limit outage + failed-attempt persist + phone-signup onboarding flag + Google profile update, `authService.verifyEmail` profile lookup + pending-profile creation (a verified account with no profile document), `userService` profile-store sync after create/update, `feedService` interest-vector/session-state/preferences/single-post/ML-source/ad-prefetch reads, `analyticsService` identity + top-posts reads (silently reported as zero), `marketplaceService`/`pollService`/`aiStudioService` localforage failures, `searchService` skipped candidate, `ProfilePublicScreen` view tracking, `NotificationsScreen` follow-request hydration, `videoService.shareVideo` counter, and `userService.unfollow` follower snapshot. Guarded on both sides now: the client sweep is frozen by `srcSilentCatchGuard.test.js` and the server by `functionsSilentCatchGuard.test.js` (both in the hard-failing `guards` job) — each fails on an empty catch that carries no explanatory comment, so a silent failure cannot be reintroduced; `sideEffectFailureGuard.test.js` additionally fails when a known server-owned side-effect call is followed by a bare no-op `.catch(() => {})`. |
| N149 the composer fabricated a "passed moderation" verdict when the check could not run | ✅ **now closed** | `CreatePost.moderateContent` caught a failed `moderatePost` call and dispatched `{ approved: true, flags: [], fallback: true }`, so an unreachable moderation provider rendered "✅ Content passed moderation" — a safety claim the platform never verified (the server itself returns `unknown: true` when the provider is unconfigured, precisely so the client does not claim a pass). The catch now dispatches the same `unknown` state, `ModerationStatus` renders an explicit "Moderation check unavailable — content was not screened" state, and the approval copy is gated on a real `approved` flag. Guarded by `noFabricatedData.test.js`. |
| N150 the admin content list read non-existent top-level `views`/`likes`, and admin rank titles fabricated a level | ✅ **now closed** | Posts store engagement under `stats` (`firestoreService` writes `stats: { likes, views, … }`), but `AdminContentManagementScreen` rendered `item.views`/`item.likes` — fields no writer ever creates — so every row showed "0 views 0 likes" and the operator saw fabricated zeros as if measured. It now reads `item.stats?.views`/`item.stats?.likes` through a `formatStat` helper that renders `—` for an unknown count. `AdminEconomyScreen`/`AdminVerificationScreen` rendered `getRankTitle(level || 0)`, which silently labels a missing level as the lowest rank ("Arrival"); both now render `—` for a non-finite level. Guarded by `noFabricatedData.test.js`. |
| N151 the admin dashboard and bottom-nav fabricated `0` for unmeasured values | ✅ **now closed** | `adminService.count` swallowed a failed aggregate query and returned `0`, so the dashboard rendered "0 users / 0 posts" while the count had actually thrown — an unmeasured collection presented as an empty one. It now returns `null` (unknown); the dashboard's stat cards render `—`, the pending-report sum stays `null` if any component count is unknown, and "Platform Revenue" reads `Unavailable` instead of a fabricated `$0`. `BottomNav` rendered `effectiveUser?.coins ?? 0`, claiming "0" coins before the profile document loaded; an absent balance now renders `—`. Guarded by `noFabricatedData.test.js`. |
| N152 `MenuScreen`/`RankingsScreen` fabricated a "Citizen" identity and an empty wallet | ✅ **now closed** | `MenuScreen` rendered `activeUser?.displayName || "Citizen"` / `|| "citizen"` and `RankingsScreen` rendered `item.user?.displayName || item.community?.name || 'Citizen'` — a fabricated identity that bypassed the placeholder-rejection the read model owns (`pickDisplayName`/`pickHandle`/`deriveHandle`). Both now resolve identity through `profileReadModel`. `MenuScreen` also coerced an absent balance to `0` (`"0 Coins"`, claiming an empty wallet before the profile document loaded); an unknown balance now renders `—` and the nav badge falls back to the plain "Coins" label. Guarded by `noFabricatedData.test.js`. |
| N153 economy surfaces fabricated an empty wallet on a failed read | ✅ **now closed** | `walletService.getTransactions` swallowed a failed ledger read and returned `[]`, so `WalletScreen` rendered "No ledger transactions recorded yet." for a read that never happened; `WalletScreen`'s balance card printed `(wallet?.availableCoins \|\| user?.coins \|\| 0)` and had no error state; `CoinsScreen` coerced an unknown balance to `0`; `ProfileMetricsGrid` fabricated `0` coins; `VideoAnalyticsScreen` rendered a failed wallet read as a `$0.00` pending payout. All now treat an absent/failed value as *unknown* (`—` / retry state) and the service fails closed. Guarded by `noFabricatedData.test.js`. |
| N154 analytics surfaces fabricated a zeroed dashboard on a failed read | ✅ **now closed** | `analyticsStore.loadAnalytics` caught a failed read and installed an all-zero `fallbackAnalytics` (`totalViews: 0, totalReach: 0, totalEngagement: 0, coinsEarned: 0`) while clearing `state.error`, so the creator dashboard rendered "0 views / 0 reach / 0 engagement / 0 coins earned" as if measured and its error branch was unreachable; `ProfileCreatorDashboard` then did `Number(analytics?.totalViews) || 0` on top. The store now surfaces the failure (`state.analytics = null`, `state.error` set) and the dashboard renders an absent metric as `—`. Guarded by `noFabricatedData.test.js`. |
| N155 profile/progression surfaces fabricated `0` for unmeasured counts and balances | ✅ **now closed** | `ProfileMetricsGrid`'s `formatNumber` returned `'0'` for a null count and defaulted posts/followers/following/views/likes to `0`; `RankingsScreen` rendered `item.score?.toLocaleString() \|\| 0`; `ProgressScreen` rendered `(user?.experience \|\| 0)` and `user?.coins \|\| 0`; `walletService.getWalletOverview` coerced `Number(userData.coins \|\| userData.coinBalance \|\| 0)` (and `availableCoins` from it) so an unmeasured balance read as an empty wallet; `PostOptionsDrawer` derived a fabricated "Creator Score" from `followerCount / 1000`; `WalletScreen` compared the withdrawal amount against `availableCoins \|\| 0`. All now treat an absent value as *unknown* (`—` / `null`), and the withdrawal form refuses to submit against an unverified balance. Guarded by `noFabricatedData.test.js`. |
| N156 media/video surfaces fabricated a `0` stat, and four private `formatCount` copies had drifted from the canonical one | ✅ **now closed** | `videoUtils.formatViewCount` returned `'0'` for a non-number, and `VideoCard`/`VideoDetailScreen`/`VideosScreen`/`ReelsScreen`/`VideoActionRail`/`videoService` all coerced an absent `views`/`likes`/`comments`/`shares`/`saves`/`gifts` to `0` before rendering, so an unmeasured video read as "0 views 0 likes". `ReelsScreen` shifted `Math.max(0, undefined + 1)` and `VideoFeed.handleLike` invented a like total of `1` for a video whose real count was never loaded. An absent stat now stays `null` and renders `—` (an optimistic like on an unknown total stays unknown). `formatCount` existed in four private copies (`searchUtils`, `QuickAccessPanel`, `CommentsDrawer`, `PostCard`), three of which printed `num.toString()` for a missing value; they now all import the one canonical formatter. `AuthContext` also mirrored `coins`/`postCount`/`videoCount`/`savedCount`/`followerCount`/`followingCount` as `|| 0`; they are now `null` when the profile does not carry them. `ProfileFeedGrid`'s like/comment counts and `PerksRewardsScreen`'s treasury render unknown as `—`. Guarded by `noFabricatedData.test.js`. |
| N157 post-analytics read non-existent top-level counters, and several engagement surfaces fabricated a `0` | ✅ **now closed** | `PostAnalyticsScreen` derived every metric from `postDoc.viewsCount \|\| postDoc.views \|\| 0` / `postDoc.likesCount \|\| postDoc.likes?.length \|\| 0`, but `firestoreService` writes engagement under `stats` — so the screen rendered a measured "0 views / 0 likes / 0 comments" for every post, plus a fabricated `0%` engagement rate. It now reads `postDoc.stats.*` and renders an unknown metric as `—`. `postService.getPost` returned the `{ success, post }` envelope from `firestoreService` instead of the post, so its callers (`PostCard.promotePost`, `PostAnalyticsScreen`) read `undefined` for every field; it now unwraps and throws on failure. `PostCard`'s engagement reducer seeded `likeCount: post.stats?.likes \|\| 0` and shifted it from a fake zero (a like on an unmeasured post invented a total of 1) — an unknown total now stays null; its inline analytics panel, `InlineComments` "View all N comments", `CommentsDrawer`'s "N total", `PollCard`'s vote total, `StoriesScreen`'s view count and `LiveScreen`'s viewer count all render unknown as `—`. `PerksRewardsScreen` treated an unknown coin balance as an empty treasury (and marked every gift unaffordable). `CreatePost`'s analytics preview rendered `?? 0` metrics and invented a "+12% this week" growth rate when no delta existed. Guarded by `noFabricatedData.test.js`. |
| N158 creator/economy surfaces fabricated zeroed standing from absent data | ✅ **now closed** | `CreatorDashboardScreen` seeded every metric with `analytics?.totalX \|\| 0` and its local `formatNumber` returned `'0'` for anything non-numeric, so an unmeasured creator saw a measured "0 views / 0 reach / 0 engagement / 0 coins" and a fabricated `0%` change. It now passes `null` through and renders `—`, and a change badge only renders for a real numeric delta. `CreatorPayoutScreen` mapped a `null` balance (the documented "unknown" return of `monetizationService.getBalance`) to `0`, which claimed an empty wallet and blocked every payout with "exceeds your current balance"; an unknown balance is now unknown and the request path refuses with an honest "balance unavailable" message. Its "Double-entry verified ledger · 100% secure payouts" claim was a security assertion the client cannot back — replaced with the verifiable "Server-verified ledger · payouts reviewed before settlement". `ProfileSystemRoadmapModal` turned absent XP into `0` (rendering "Start posting & engaging to earn XP" and a "Max" next-level badge for an unmeasured profile); absent XP now reads "XP not yet recorded". `ProfileFeaturedSection`/`ProfilePinnedPosts` read the never-written top-level `likesCount` and fabricated `0`; they now fall back to `stats.likes`. Community/event member/post/attendee counts and the admin community table render `—` for a missing stat. `LiveScreen` viewer counts are `null` until polled, not a frozen `0`. Guarded by `noFabricatedData.test.js` (batch-4 block) and `liveWiringGuard.test.js`. |
| N159 royal-eligibility checklist and video comment count fabricated zeros | ✅ **now closed** | `ProfileSystemRoadmapModal`'s `RoyalEligibilityChecklist` coerced every dimension (`activeDays`, `reputation`, `contribution`, `influence`, `achievements`) through `Number(x \|\| 0)`, so an unmeasured profile showed a measured "0/20 Active Days" and the checklist rows rendered `0/<target>`. Unknown dimensions now stay `null` and render `—/<target>` (unmet), and `level={currentLevel ?? 0}` passed a fabricated `0` into the checklist (and into `getRoyalEligibility`, whose fail-closed `0` still denied but for the wrong reason) — it now passes the real `null`. `VideoCard`'s comment-count badge read the never-written top-level `video.commentsCount` while `videoService` exposes `video.comments` (mapped from `stats.comments`), so every video showed `—` for a real comment count; it now reads `comments ?? commentsCount`. Guarded by `noFabricatedData.test.js`. |
| N160 `analyticsService.getUserAnalytics` cached a fabricated all-zero fallback on failure | ✅ **now closed** | On any read failure the service logged a warning and returned (then *cached*) `{ totalViews: 0, totalReach: 0, totalEngagement: 0, coinsEarned: 0, ... }`, so a creator's dashboard rendered "0 views / 0 reach / 0 coins" as measured data, the `analyticsStore` error branch was unreachable, and the poisoned cache meant later reads returned the fabricated zeros without retrying. It now logs at error level and rethrows; the callers already render an error state (`analyticsStore` sets `analytics = null` + `error`; `VideoAnalyticsScreen` sets `loadError`; `CreatePost` treats analytics as advisory). Guarded by `noFabricatedData.test.js`. |
| N161 collections offered a Public/Private control the rules cannot honour | ✅ **now closed** | `CollectionsScreen` collected a "Private Collection" toggle and rendered a Public/Private badge from `c.isPrivate`, but `collectionsService.createCollection` never wrote the flag and `firestore.rules` scopes every `collections` read/update/delete to `resource.data.userId == uid()` — so a "Public" folder was readable by nobody and the badge was theatre. (Persisting the flag would have been the *wrong* fix: it would have stored a visibility the rules still deny.) The toggle, badge and "private or public folders" copy are replaced with an honest "Only you" badge and a note that folders are private to the account; `shareCollection`'s `sharedWith` field is equally unreadable by the shared-with user under the same rule, which is documented for follow-up. Guarded by `noFabricatedData.test.js`. |
| N162 collaboration dashboard fabricated a member count and a dead avatar row | ✅ **now closed** | `collaborationService.getStats` mapped `members: typeof data.members === 'number' ? data.members : 0` and `team: Array.isArray(data.team) ? data.team : []` from the project document, but `functions/collaboration.js` never writes a `members` number or a `team` array — the roster lives in the `team/{uid}` subcollection. Every project card therefore showed a fabricated "0 members" and a permanently empty avatar row (`project.team.slice(0,3)`), and the `+N` overflow chip could never render. The server now maintains a denormalized `memberCount` on the project document (`1` at create, `FieldValue.increment(±1)` in `addTeamMember` / `removeCollaborationTeamMember`); `getStats` maps the real `memberCount` (or `null` when absent, rendered "Members unavailable"); the dashboard shows a real `N member(s)` line instead of dead avatars. `getTeam` now resolves real names/avatars through the canonical `socialGraphService.hydrateProfiles` batch, so the detail-screen roster no longer shows bare uids. Guarded by `noFabricatedData.test.js`. |
| N163 "New Project" button in the collaboration dashboard was a dead control | ✅ **now closed** | `ProjectDashboardScreen`'s primary "New Project" `motion.button` had no `onClick`, so the only way to create a collaboration project — `collaborationService.createProject` (a real `createCollaborationProject` callable) — was unreachable from the UI. The button now opens an accessible `Dialog` (name, description, content type) that calls `createProject`, surfaces callable errors inline, prevents duplicate submits, and navigates to the new project on success. |
| N164 collaboration team management (invite / role change / removal) was unreachable from the UI | ✅ **now closed** | `collaborationService.createInvite`, `updateMemberRole` and `removeTeamMember` (all backed by real server callables with role checks) had **no caller in `src/`** — the team tab only rendered a read-only roster, so a project owner could never actually build a team. The team tab now exposes an "Invite member" dialog (email + role) for callers with `team.invite`/`team.manage`, an inline role selector and a remove action per non-owner member, all gated by `canManageTeam(myRole)` and the server's own permission check. |
| N165 feed EventCard fabricated attendee counts and read a never-written cover field | ✅ **now closed** | `src/screens/PostCard/EventCard.jsx` (the in-feed event card) read `event.stats.going`, `event.stats.interested` and `event.previewAttendees`, none of which any writer produces — the real counter is `stats.attendeeCount` (`functions/events.js`). Every event therefore showed "0 going" and a permanently empty avatar row, and the "gravity"/trending chip was computed from a fabricated zero. It also read `event.banner`, but the cover is stored under `coverImage` (CreatePost + `eventService`), so the real cover never rendered. Now: the count comes from `stats.attendeeCount` (absent → `—`, never `0`), the dead avatar row is gone, and the cover reads `banner || coverImage`. The host line no longer fabricates the placeholder name "Arvdoul User"; the real author name is passed from `PostCard` (`hostName`/`hostUsername`/`hostVerified`). Guarded by `noFabricatedData.test.js`. |
| N166 non-interpolated `${}` inside a static `className` silently dropped styling | ✅ **now closed** | A `${...}` in a **double-quoted** JSX attribute is not interpolation — the literal text is emitted — and an escaped `\${...}` inside a template literal is emitted verbatim too. Both shipped: `EventDetailScreen` and `EventDiscoveryScreen` rendered their status badge with the literal class `${statusBadge.className}` (no colour), and `FilterTool`/`CropTool`/`TextTool` had 19 escaped `\${isDark ? … }` classes so every dark/light surface, text and placeholder class on those controls was missing (the input rendered with no background). All 19 escapes stripped, both static classNames converted to template literals, and a new hard-failing `classNameInterpolationGuard.test.js` (in the `guards` CI job) fails if either pattern reappears. |
| N167 `CreatorPayoutScreen` fabricated a zero earnings breakdown on ledger-read failure | ✅ **now closed** | The gifts/ads/lifetime-withdrawn figures are sums over the coin ledger, but a **failed** `getCoinEarningHistory`/`getCoinSpendingHistory` read collapsed to `[]` and the screen rendered a confident "0 Coins" / "$0.00 withdrawn" — telling a creator they had earned nothing. Earnings now start `null` and only update when the read is fulfilled; unknown renders "—". Guarded by `noFabricatedData.test.js`. |
| N168 `AdminVerificationScreen` fabricated "0 strikes ✓" for an unknown strike count | ✅ **now closed** | The screen read `Number(applicant.strikesCount ?? 0)`, but `strikesCount` is written **only on `communities`** (`functions/admin.js`), never on a verification applicant. So the strike gate always saw a clean `0`, passed `meetsStrikes`, and could contribute to "Meets every badge requirement". An absent count is now `null` → the gate fails closed and the row renders "—". Guarded by `noFabricatedData.test.js`. |
| N169 analytics earnings (`coinsEarned`) fabricated as 0 when the wallet read failed or the field was never written | ✅ **now closed** | No server path increments `profile_analytics.coinsEarned`; `analyticsService` derives it from the `users` wallet balance. Both the exists-branch (`data.coinsEarned \|\| 0`) and the new-creator fallback (`coinsEarned = … ?? 0`) collapsed an absent/failed read to a confident "$0.00" on the creator dashboards, `VideoAnalyticsScreen` revenue card, `CreatorDashboardScreen` and `ProfileCreatorDashboard`. Now `null` (rendered "—") unless a numeric value exists; the coins change-% is likewise null rather than a fake 0. Also fixed `VideoAnalyticsScreen`'s revenue header, which labelled a USD figure as "Total Coins Earned". Guarded by `noFabricatedData.test.js`. |
| N170 `storage.rules` namespace/shape did not match the paths the client actually writes — nearly every media upload was denied `storage/unauthorized` | ✅ **now closed** | `storage.rules` declared `avatars/{userId}/{fileName}`, `posts/{userId}/{postId}/{fileName}`, `messages/{conv}/{messageId}/{fileName}`, `sounds/{userId}/{fileName}` …, but the client wrote `avatars/{uid}` (a 2-segment path that cannot match a 3-segment rule), `posts/{uid}/{file}`, `chat_media/{uid}/{file}`, `comments/voice/{uid}/{file}`, and namespaces with **no rule at all** (`chat_media`, `comments`, `events`, `group-photos`, `communities/avatars`, `audio_exports`). Verified against the Storage emulator with `@firebase/rules-unit-testing`: avatar uploads, post media, story media, chat media, voice comments, event covers, group photos, community media, audio exports and generic `users/...` uploads were all DENIED. Fix: one canonical owner-scoped layout `{namespace}/{ownerUid}/...` for every client write (message media is participant-scoped at `messages/{conversationId}/{messageId}.{ext}` and gated by a cross-service `firestore.get(conversations/…).data.participants`), plus a rewritten `storage.rules` with `{fileName=**}` (matches the zero-segment `avatars/{uid}` form) and an explicit owner `allow delete` (a delete has no `request.resource`). Client paths updated in `userService`, `SetupProfile`, `CreatePost`, `CreateImage`, `CreateVideo`, `CreateEvent`, `CreateEventScreen`, `CreateCommunityScreen`, `NewConversationScreen`, `CommentsDrawer`, `messagesService`; anonymous fallbacks now fail closed. Guarded by `storageRulesPathAlignment.test.js` (static + live emulator) and the updated `storageRulesDeploy.test.js`/`noFabricatedData.test.js`. |
| N174 creator "Your Ranking" fabricated a "Top 1%" percentile by ordering an all-zero field | ✅ **now closed** | `analyticsService.getCreatorRanking` ranked creators with `orderBy('coinsEarned')` over `profile_analytics` — but no server path ever increments `coinsEarned` (earnings live on the wallet; see N169). Every creator therefore sat in an arbitrary order against all-zero data, and the top doc received a confident **"Top 1%"** / "Top 5%" / "Rising Creator" label, shown on `CreatorDashboardScreen` ("Your Ranking") and in `ProfileCreatorDashboard`'s standing card. The read-failure branch also returned the fabricated label `'New Creator'`. Fix: a creator is ranked only when a positive, maintained score exists (otherwise `position`/`percentile`/`label` stay `null`), the failure branch returns `label: null`, and the dashboard renders the ranking card only when a real rank exists (an absent rank is not dressed up as "New Creator"). Guarded by `noFabricatedData.test.js`. |
| N178 ranking pagination passed a raw number to `startAfter()` and coerced an unmeasured trend to 0 | ✅ **now closed** | All six `rankingService` list queries (creators/wealth/reputation/communities/trending/rising) passed `startAfter(offset)` to the modular SDK, which treats a raw value as a **field value** compared against `orderBy('score')` — so `offset` was compared to a document's `score`, not its position. "Load more" therefore silently dropped/duplicated rows instead of paging. Fix: bounded offset paging (`limit(offset + PAGE_SIZE)` + `snapshot.docs.slice(offset)`), since the modular SDK exposes no `offset()`. Also, `trend: data.trend \|\| 0` coerced an absent trend to a measured "0 / no change"; it is now `?? null` and `TrendIndicator` renders an em-dash for an unmeasured trend. Guarded by `noFabricatedData.test.js`. |
| N177 `storyService.getABBucket`/`setABOverride` was an abandoned experiment with a random assignment and no consumer | ✅ **now closed** | The service assigned each user to an A/B bucket via `Math.random() < 0.5` (persisted to `localStorage['ab_bucket_<uid>']`) and exported `getABBucket`/`setABOverride` — but no surface anywhere reads the bucket and no code ever calls either method (grep across `src`, `functions` and `__tests__` finds only the definition/export). A dead experiment that randomly "assigns" users is dead code with a fabricated assignment; removed both methods and their service-facade exports. |
| N176 `getFollowerGrowth` fabricated an invented straight-line follower trend when no snapshots existed | ✅ **now closed** | With no `user_daily_stats` follower-count snapshots the service fell back to `estimatedDailyGrowth = followerCount / days` and emitted a synthetic daily series (`followers: currentFollowers − estimatedDailyGrowth × daysFromNow`, `newFollowers: estimatedDailyGrowth`) — an invented trend presented as measured growth. The method has no caller in `src`, so nothing rendered it yet, but it was a live fabrication trap for the next consumer. Fix: it now returns `[]` until real snapshots exist (`userService._recordFollowerSnapshotIfDue` writes them on follow/unfollow). Guarded by `noFabricatedData.test.js`. |
| N175 the "Rising" ranking tab and every other `rankings/*` list read a collection no writer populates | 🔴 **CONFIRMED — BLOCKED (needs a scheduled ranking writer)** | `RankingsScreen`'s "Rising" tab calls `rankingService.getRisingCreators`, which queries `rankings/rising/creators`; the creator/wealth/reputation/community tabs likewise read `rankings/{type}/{category}`. No Cloud Function, scheduled job or client path ever writes to the `rankings` collection (`firestore.rules` even declares `allow write: if false; // scheduled functions compute rankings`, but no such function exists). Every tab is therefore permanently empty and shows "No rankings yet". `getRisingCreators` also orders by `growthRate`, a field with no writer. The feature is reachable and honest (it renders an empty state, not fabricated rows), so this is a missing-backend gap rather than fabricated data. Closing it requires a scheduled ranking-computation function (write `rankings/{type}/{category}` with `score`, `trend`, `timeRange`) plus its indexes; not implemented here because the ranking formula is a product decision. |
| N173 analytics period-over-period "change" badges fabricated a 0% delta when no previous period exists | ✅ **now closed** | `getUserAnalytics` defaulted `changes: { views: 0, reach: 0, engagement: 0, coins: 0 }` and computed `views`/`reach` as `previousTotal > 0 ? … : 0`, so `CreatorDashboardScreen`'s metrics grid and `VideoAnalyticsScreen`'s stat/revenue cards rendered a confident **"0%"** period-over-period badge whenever there was no previous period (a brand-new creator) or the baseline was zero. There is no measured change in either case. Fix: the default `changes` values are `null` and the `: 0` fallbacks are `: null`; `VideoAnalyticsScreen` passes the deltas through only when they are real numbers (its `revenue.change` is no longer a hardcoded `0`). Guarded by `noFabricatedData.test.js`. |
| N172 Creator dashboard fabricated a green "+0.0%" growth rate and derived "Active Days" from the recent-post count | ✅ **now closed** | `analyticsService.getUserAnalytics` initialised `growthRate: 0` and, in the new-creator fallback, `activeDays: topPosts.length > 0 ? Math.min(topPosts.length, days) : 0`. No writer ever computes a growth rate into `profile_analytics` (only `functions/analytics.js` seeds `growthRate: 0` on doc create), so `CreatorDashboardScreen`'s `analytics?.growthRate !== undefined` guard was always true and rendered a permanent green **"Growth Rate +0.0%"** with an up-arrow for every creator, including a stalled one — a fabricated positive trend. The `activeDays` figure was the number of recent public posts dressed up as the canonical active-day streak (`users/{uid}.activeDaysCount`, written by `functions/levelSystem` `recordActiveDay`), so a prolific poster was shown a fabricated "active streak". Fix: `getUserAnalytics` now returns `growthRate: null` / `activeDays: null` (only a real stored number survives), the dashboard renders the growth card only when `typeof analytics?.growthRate === 'number'` (a `null.toFixed()` crash is also avoided) and reads Active Days from the profile (`userProfile?.activeDaysCount ?? storeUser?.activeDaysCount ?? null`, `—` when unknown); the identity mirror in `AuthContext.syncUserWithAppStore` now carries `activeDaysCount`/`activeStreak`; `analyticsStore` keeps `growthRate`/`activeDays` null when unmeasured, `functions/analytics.js` no longer seeds `growthRate: 0`/`activeDays: 1` on the analytics doc, and `PerksRewardsScreen` floors an unknown active-day count at 0 instead of fabricating "1 day". Guarded by `noFabricatedData.test.js`. |
| N171 CreatePost persisted a session-scoped `data:`/`blob:` URL as post media when a Storage upload failed | ✅ **now closed** | The composer's `uploadMedia` caught a failed/timed-out upload (a bare 7 s `Promise.race` timeout that rejected a still-in-flight upload) and fell back to `convertFileToDataURL(file)`, a base64 `data:` URL, or to `item.preview` (a `blob:` object URL). Either was written into `posts/{id}.media[].url` — a URL that renders **only** for the uploader's current tab and is broken for every other viewer and after any reload. The same shape hid behind the background-sync `catch` (`Background media upload fallback`), which swallowed an upload failure and wrote the optimistic `blob:` previews to Firestore. A generated video/image thumbnail is also a `blob:` URL and was persisted as `media[].thumbnail`. Fix: `uploadMedia` no longer fabricates a URL — it uploads, requires a `downloadURL`, uploads the generated thumbnail (or drops it), and **throws** if any item failed; the background sync no longer swallows the error (the outer catch drops the optimistic entry and hands the post to the offline queue); `convertFileToDataURL` and the 7 s timeout are deleted. Guarded by `noFabricatedData.test.js`. |
| N179 `analyticsService.getFollowerGrowth` coerced a missing snapshot `delta`/`value` to a fabricated 0 | ✅ **now closed** | The method mapped stored `user_daily_stats` snapshots with `followers: s.value || 0` / `newFollowers: s.delta || 0`. The snapshot writer (`userService._recordFollowerSnapshotIfDue`) writes only `value`, so `delta` **always** collapsed to a fabricated `0` ("no new followers") for every row, and a stored `value` of a real `0` was indistinguishable from an absent one. Now `typeof s.value === 'number' ? s.value : null` / same for `s.delta`, so an unmeasured delta renders unknown rather than a measured zero. Guarded by `noFabricatedData.test.js`. |
| N180 `analyticsService` carried a dead local `LRUCache` class, an unused `doc`/`getDoc` destructure and a `console.warn` | ✅ **now closed** | The local `LRUCache` class was superseded by `cacheManager.namespace('analytics', …)` and had zero references; `getFollowerGrowth` destructured `doc, getDoc` it never used; `destroy()` logged a listener-unsubscribe failure via `console.warn` instead of the Logger convention. Removed the dead class, trimmed the destructure and routed the log through `logger.warn`. |
| N181 new-creator analytics fallback fabricated a synthetic daily series and an invented `totalReach` | ✅ **now closed** | The `getUserAnalytics` new-creator fallback built a `days`-long `dailyStats` array of all-zero days with a single final `views = totalViews` spike (rendered by the analytics chart and the creator-dashboard sparkline as measured per-day data), and set `totalReach: totalReach || Math.max(totalViews, followerCount)` — an estimate presented as the measured reach counter. `VideoAnalyticsScreen` also mapped `totalViews: data.totalViews || 0`, so an absent total read as a measured `0`. Now the fallback reports the real sharded totals and an **empty** series until real snapshots exist, and the screen maps `totalViews` to `null` (rendered `—`) when it is not a number. Guarded by `noFabricatedData.test.js`. |
| N183 the no-fabrication sweep had a second, untouched class of `x || 0` display coercions | ✅ **now closed** | T1.1 (the money/counts sweep) is complete: the first pass guarded money and the primary counters, but a second set of measurement counts still coerced a missing value to a fabricated `0` — highlights story counts, sound reel counts, collection item counts, community banned-user counts, admin strike counts and progression `xpToNext`/`progress`. All now render `—` through `lib/unknownNumber.js` (or use `unknownNumber(v) ?? 0` for a bar width, where the label carries the unknown state). Guarded by `noFabricatedData.test.js`. |
| N184 stale write-only-`useState`/denied-block duplication in the admin surfaces | ✅ **now closed** | Three admin screens hand-rolled the same 'Access Denied / Admin privileges required' card with three different button styles; they now render the single `design-system/PermissionDenied.jsx` (`role="alert"`, one exit action). `sharedStatePrimitivesGuard.test.js` fails if any UI module re-declares the denial copy inline. Additionally the N184 no-fabricated-counts sweep (round 2) is folded into this row's evidence: see N183. |
| N187 prediction-market wagering was a coin value sink with no settlement engine | ✅ **now closed** | `functions/polls.js` `votePoll` (and the `PollsScreen` wager modal / `pollService.createPoll`) let a user stake coins on a poll option: the wager was debited from the staker through the double-entry `coin_transactions` ledger and `system/coin_supply` was **burned** — but no settlement engine exists to pay out or refund a pool, so every staked coin was destroyed. Removed end-to-end: the `votePoll` callable now records a plain vote only (no ledger debit, no supply burn), `PollsScreen` renders no wager modal / prediction toggle / pool balance, `pollService.createPoll` no longer accepts `isPredictionMarket`/`poolCoins`, the dead `prediction_markets` (and nested `bets`) `firestore.rules` block is deleted, and the "Prediction Markets" copy is gone from `MenuScreen`, `QuickAccessPanel` and the route comment. `noFabricatedData.test.js` fails if a wager surface or the `poll_wager` ledger reason reappears. |
| N188 post-poll voter rewards were an unfunded coin faucet | ✅ **now closed** | The composer let a post author set `poll.rewardCoins` (0–1000, client-side) and `functions/polls.js` `votePostPoll` **minted** that many coins to every first-time voter — incrementing `users/{uid}.coins` and `system/coin_supply` while debiting nobody. A creator could set 1000/voter and mint unlimited coins; the reward was never funded from the author's balance and no escrow existed. Removed end-to-end: `votePostPoll` records the vote only (the `poll_vote_reward` ledger credit, the `__reward` marker doc and the `coin_supply` increment are gone), `PollCard` no longer renders the "+N coins" badge/reward toast or takes an `onReward` prop, `firestoreService.voteOnPoll` drops `rewardCoins` from its return, and `CreatePoll`/`CreatePost` drop the reward input, `SET_POLL_REWARD` reducer case, `pollReward` state and `rewardCoins` payload field. `noFabricatedData.test.js` + `integrationWiring.test.js` guard the removal. |
| N189 marketplace purchases destroyed the buyer's coins and never paid the seller | ✅ **now closed** | `functions/monetization.js` `purchaseMarketplaceItem` debited the buyer's coins, posted a `system:coin_supply` burn and incremented `salesCount`, but **never credited `product.creatorId`** — every sale destroyed the full price and the seller earned nothing, while the marketplace advertises "0% platform fees" (`MarketplaceScreen`, `QuickAccessPanel`). The purchase is now a real transfer buyer → seller (`reason: 'marketplace_sale'` credit, `relatedUserId`, double-entry `users:{buyer} → users:{seller}`, supply unchanged), with explicit guards for a sellerless listing and self-purchase. `noFabricatedData.test.js` fails if the burn returns. |
| N190 `verifyPurchase` fabricated IAP receipt verification and minted coins | ✅ **now closed** | The client-callable `functions/index.js` `verifyPurchase` checked only that `functions.config().appstore.*`/`play.*` keys *existed*, then credited coins via `_addCoins` **without ever validating the receipt** against Apple/Google — the comment even admitted "Real platform-specific validation would call the Apple/Google servers here" with no such call. It had no client caller and was not in the `FUNCTIONS` registry. Removed the export plus the dead entry-point helpers that existed only to serve it (`checkIdempotency`, `createTransactionRecord`, `_addCoins`/`_spendCoins`/`_transferCoins`). Coin purchases go through the real Stripe-verified `monetization.js` `purchaseCoins` path. Guarded by `noFabricatedData.test.js` + `sharedConfigSync.test.js`. |
| N191 the story-quiz update rule let any viewer rewrite the quiz | ✅ **now closed** | `firestore.rules` `/stories/{storyId}/quizzes/{quizId}` had a blanket `allow update: if … vibeIsActive(…) && vibeVisibleToViewer(…)` (unlike the sibling poll rule, which is field-scoped), so any viewer of an active Vibe could rewrite another creator's quiz document — `correctOption`, `coinReward`, anything. Now scoped to `answers`/`userAnswers`/`updatedAt`, requires the viewer to add their own `userAnswers` entry, and forbids rewriting an existing one. Guarded by `firestoreRulesCompile.test.js`. |
| N192 dead world-open `spaces/{id}/signals` match | ✅ **now closed** | Nothing reads or writes `spaces/{spaceId}/signals` — real WebRTC signalling uses `signaling_rooms/{roomId}` (participants-only). The leftover `allow read, write: if isSignedIn()` was removed. |
| N193 `withdrawal_requests` accepted a client-authored create | ✅ **now closed** | The rule `allow create: if isSignedIn() && request.resource.data.userId == uid()` let a client write a withdrawal row directly. The authoritative path is the `requestWithdrawal` callable, which locks the coins and writes the request in ONE transaction; a client row could exist with no coin lock behind it (a forged payout request for the admin queue). No client code writes here, so `allow create: if false;` — the row is server-only, reads (owner/admin) and owner-cancel are unchanged. Guarded by `firestoreRulesCompile.test.js`. |
| N194 stat docs were hijackable via their id | ✅ **now closed** | `user_daily_stats/{statId}` (id `<uid>_<date>`) and `live_viewers/{viewerId}` (id `<streamId>_<uid>`) authorised writes only on the *incoming* `userId` field, ignoring the doc id — so a signed-in user could overwrite another user's snapshot / watch record by targeting its id. Both now also require the doc id to encode the caller (`statId.startsWith(uid() + '_')` / `viewerId.endsWith('_' + uid())`), matching the real id shapes written by `userService._recordFollowerSnapshotIfDue` and `liveService.joinLiveStream`. Guarded by `firestoreRulesCompile.test.js`. |
| N195 the space "Tips" total was a dead client counter | ✅ **now closed** | `spacesService.sendTip` moved the coins via the server `transferCoins` callable but then bumped `spaces/{id}.tipsTotalCoins` from the client. The `spaces` update rule admits only the host (or the roster fields), and a self-tip is rejected, so that write was **always denied** — the "Tips" total on `SpacesScreen` never moved. The counter is now bumped by the same `transferCoins` server transaction (when `reason === 'space_tip'` and `metadata.spaceId` names a real space), and the client write was removed. Guarded by `noFabricatedData.test.js`. |
| N196 live viewers were never recorded (owner-only stream write) | ✅ **now closed** | `liveService.joinLiveStream`/`leaveLiveStream` wrote `live_streams/{id}.stats.totalViewers`/`updatedAt` from the viewer's client, but the `live_streams` update rule is owner-only (`request.auth.uid == resource.data.userId`). For every non-owner viewer the write was denied, which failed the **whole** join/leave transaction — so the `live_viewers` record, the sharded `viewerCount`, and the XP-on-leave all silently no-op'd (`LiveScreen` swallowed the error). Both transactions now write only the viewer-owned `live_viewers` record and the sharded `viewerCount`; the cumulative `stats.totalViewers` is maintained by a new server trigger `functions/live.js#onLiveViewerCreate` (incremented once per viewer-record create, so a re-join is not double-counted), and `getLiveStream`/`getLiveAnalytics` overlay the sharded count on read. Guarded by `liveWiringGuard.test.js`. |
| N197 group membership writes were denied (system messages + self-leave) | ✅ **now closed** | Two independent rule defects made every group-membership mutation fail: (1) `_sendSystemMessage` writes `senderId: 'system'`, but the message-create rule required `senderId == uid()` for EVERY message, so the timeline chip was denied and — because the call was `await`ed — it aborted `leaveGroup`/`addParticipants`/`removeParticipants`/`changeRole`/`transferAdmin`/`approve`/`reject`. (2) the `conversations` update rule required an admin for ANY `participants` change, so a non-admin "leave" (`participants: arrayRemove(uid)`) was denied. The create rule now admits a system message from a conversation admin, or from the member whose own uid is in `eventData` (self-leave / self-transfer), and forbids `text`/`media` so it cannot smuggle content; the update rule admits `old.participants.removeAll([uid()]) == new` (self-removal only). `leaveGroup` sends the notice *before* the removal (the sender must still be a participant) and `_sendSystemMessage` no longer aborts the primary mutation. Verified with emulator-backed adversarial checks in `firestoreRulesCompile.test.js`. |
| N198 `spaces` roster update trusted `hasOnly` (keys, not values) — any signed-in user could rewrite a live space's `participants` | ✅ **now closed** | `firestore.rules` `/spaces/{spaceId}`'s member carve-out was `request.resource.data.diff(resource.data).affectedKeys().hasOnly(['raisedHands','participants','listeners','audienceCount'])`. `hasOnly` compares **key names only**, so any signed-in user could REPLACE the whole `participants` array — add or remove anyone, or wipe the roster. `participants` is exactly what `signaling_rooms/{roomId}` authorises on, so an outsider could insert themselves into an arbitrary live space's roster and read/steal its WebRTC offer/answer (eavesdrop), or empty the roster to lock the real participants out (DoS). The member branch now pins the roster to the caller: `request.resource.data.participants.removeAll([uid()]) == resource.data.participants.removeAll([uid()])` (the only permitted difference is the caller's own membership), `audienceCount` must equal `listeners.size()`, and the listener array may change by at most one entry. The host/admin branch is unchanged. Verified with emulator-backed adversarial checks in `firestoreRulesCompile.test.js` (member join/leave succeed; outsider add/remove/wipe/count-desync fail). |
| N199 the `posts` non-author update carve-out trusted `hasOnly` (keys, not values) — any signed-in user could rewrite a post's `stats`, `poll` and `gifts` | ✅ **now closed** | The non-author branch of `match /posts/{postId}`'s `allow update` was `request.resource.data.diff(resource.data).affectedKeys().hasOnly(['stats', 'gifts', 'poll', 'updatedAt'])`. `hasOnly` compares **key names only**, so any signed-in user could REPLACE the entire `stats` map on anyone's post — forging `likes`, `views`, `comments`, `pollVotes` and the reaction totals — or rewrite the `poll` (`options`/`totalVotes`, i.e. forge a vote tally) or the `gifts` array. The real non-author write is the reaction aggregate only (`stats.reactions.<emoji>`, written by `firestoreService.addReaction`); gifts and poll votes are written server-side (`sendGift`, `votePostPoll`). The carve-out is now the reaction aggregate only: top-level `hasOnly(['stats','updatedAt'])` plus a nested `stats` diff `hasOnly(['reactions'])`. The author branch is unchanged. `firestoreService.incrementCommentCount` (dead, but wrote `stats.comments` directly) now routes through the sharded counter, the shape `countersManager.apply` overlays on read. Verified with emulator-backed adversarial checks in `firestoreRulesCompile.test.js` (non-author reaction bump succeeds; whole-`stats` / `poll` / `gifts` rewrites fail; author content edit succeeds). |
| N200 a participant could replace (not just append) another member's message read receipt | ✅ **now closed** | The message-doc carve-out `allow update ... hasOnly(['readBy', 'deliveredTo'])` matched key names only, so any conversation participant could REPLACE both arrays — forging a read receipt for a member who never opened the message, or wiping the record that someone had read. The real path (`markMessageAsRead`/`markMessageAsDelivered`) appends the CALLER's own uid via `arrayUnion`. The carve-out now pins the caller's uid: `new.removeAll([uid()]) == old.removeAll([uid()])` for both arrays. Verified with emulator-backed adversarial checks in `firestoreRulesCompile.test.js`. |
| N201 the `comments` non-author carve-out trusted `hasOnly` (keys, not values) — a non-author could set `likes: 999999` | ✅ **now closed** | The non-author branch of `match /comments/{commentId}`'s `allow update` was `hasOnly(['likes', 'likesBy', 'dislikes', 'dislikesBy', 'reactionCounts', 'updatedAt', 'lastActivityAt'])`. `hasOnly` compares key NAMES only, so any signed-in user could set the numeric `likes`/`dislikes` tallies on anyone's comment (and the author could inflate their own) — the numbers `CommentsDrawer` renders as `formatCount(comment.likes)`. The numeric tallies are the sharded counter, never a client field: they are seeded to 0 at create and only `countersManager` moves them (`likeComment`/`dislikeComment`/`removeLikeDislike` write only the `likesBy`/`dislikesBy` arrays). `likes`/`dislikes` are now excluded from the non-author set and added to the author branch's immutable list. Guarded statically in `firestoreRulesCompile.test.js`. |
| N202 story `emoji_sliders` viewer update was an unconstrained `isSignedIn()` write | ✅ **now closed** | The `stories/{id}/emoji_sliders/{sliderId}` viewer branch was `allow update: if isSignedIn() && vibeIsActive(...) && vibeVisibleToViewer(...)` — no field constraint, so any viewer could rewrite the aggregate (`sum`/`count`/`average`) on anyone's slider. The sibling `polls`/`quizzes` rules already pin the changed keys to the response fields; the slider now mirrors them (`hasOnly(['sum','count','average','userVotes','updatedAt'])` and the caller's uid must be newly added to `userVotes`). Guarded in `firestoreRulesCompile.test.js`. |
| N203 `support_tickets` owner-update carve-out let a user self-resolve or inject fake agent replies | ✅ **now closed** | The rule allowed the owner to update `hasOnly(['status','userRating','updatedAt','messages'])`. No client path updates a ticket — `supportTicketService` only files them (`addDoc`) and `HelpCenterScreen` only reads them, and `noFabricatedData.test.js` already asserts there is no client `updateDoc` on `support_tickets`. The carve-out therefore had no legitimate writer and only let a user mark their own ticket resolved or append forged `{ sender: 'agent', ... }` entries into `messages[]`.
