Updates/deletes are now admin-only; the real reply/status write is the audit-logged `adminResolveSupportTicket` callable. Guarded in `firestoreRulesCompile.test.js`. |
| N204 `dmRequestFrom()` checked existence, not `status` — a DECLINED message request still authorised the DM | ✅ **now closed** | The `conversations` create rule lets the recipient override their own `allowDMs: 'none'` when `dmRequestFrom(sender, recipient)` holds, which was `exists(message_requests/{recipient}_{sender})`. `respondToMessageRequest` only sets `status` (`declined`/`accepted`) and nothing ever deletes the doc, so a request the recipient had *declined* still returned true and the sender could open the DM anyway — the decline was unenforced. The helper now requires `status == 'pending'`, matching `friendRequestFrom`/`followRequestFrom`. Guarded in `dmPermissionWiring.test.js`. |
| N205 request docs did not pin the deterministic doc id to the payload — a forged `message_requests` id fabricated DM consent | ✅ **now closed** | `dmRequestFrom(sender, recipient)` reads `message_requests/{recipient}_{sender}` **by path** and only inspects `.data.status`; `friendRequestFrom`/`followRequestFrom` likewise trust `${from}_{to}`. The create rules for `message_requests`/`friend_requests`/`follow_requests` validated the payload fields but NOT the id, so a user could file a doc at an arbitrary id and forge the recipient's consent — for a `allowDMs: 'none'` target that let the sender open the DM anyway (the decline/refusal was bypassed). All three creates now require `requestId == <from>_<to>` (and reject self-addressing). Adversarial emulator check in `firestoreRulesCompile.test.js` (N205) + static guard. |
| N206 a private account's approved followers could not read its content — the read rule described a follower branch it did not implement | ✅ **now closed** | `postReadableByViewer`/`postListableShape` hard-required `resource.data.authorIsPrivate == false` with **no follower branch**, but `profileCapabilityEngine.resolveCapabilities` grants `canViewContent` to an approved follower of a private account. The profile grid therefore returned **0** posts for every follower (and the author-scoped query was denied outright), even though the rule's own comment claimed "an approved follower can read a private author's content". Naively adding the follower branch would have opened a **self-follow content leak**: a user could forge `follows/{me}_{privateUser}` (the create rule only checked that `followerId == uid()`, not the target's privacy) and read the private account's content without consent. Fix: a shared `authorContentVisible()` = public author **OR** approved follower (`followEdgeExists(uid(), authorId)`); the `follows` create rule now forbids self-creating an edge to a **private** target (`targetIsPrivate()`) — that edge is written only by the owner on approval. The client grid query omits the `authorIsPrivate` constraint only for an approved-follower view (`getPostsByUser({ constrainAuthorIsPrivate })` from `profileStore`), and a new `posts(authorId, authorIsPrivate, status, visibility, isDeleted, createdAt)` composite index backs the public shape (it was uncovered). Emulator-backed adversarial check in `firestoreRulesCompile.test.js` (N206: follower admitted, stranger denied, self-follow-to-private denied, owner-approval edge allowed) + `profileFeedPagination` / `postVisibilityEnforcement` guards. |
| N207 `counter_shards` accepted a client-written absolute `value` for ANY shard id — a signed-in user could fabricate any count | ✅ **now closed (absolute-mint forgery); residual grind risk documented** | `match /counter_shards/{shardId}` allowed `create`/`update` when the changed keys were `hasOnly(['value'])` with **no constraint on the value**, so any signed-in user could set a counter's `value` to an arbitrary absolute number (`followerCount`, `likes`, `views`, `saves`, `shares`, `comments`, `replies`, `viewerCount`, `totalReach`) on any document in one write. The prior deferral claimed this was "not closable by a rule alone" because "rules cannot distinguish a legit `increment(±1)` from an absolute write". **That claim was wrong and is refuted by the emulator:** `request.resource.data.value` in a Firestore rule is the *resulting* value and `resource.data.value` the *pre-write* value, so a rule CAN bound the relative delta (verified with a throwaway emulator probe: a rule `value - resource.data.value <= 5` allowed `increment(1)` and denied `increment(100)` and an absolute `{value: 999999}`). **Fix:** the rule now requires the single numeric `value` field AND bounds the write to a relative `±100` change (`request.resource.data.value - resource.data.value` in `[-100, 100]` on update; `value` in `[-100, 100]` on create). Every reachable client call site increments by ±1 (`CountersManager` call sites); the ±100 headroom covers the batched comment-delete path (`amount: -count`). A one-shot absolute mint — create or update — is now **denied**. **Residual (documented, not fully closed):** the shard id is still unbound to the writer and no per-user rate limit applies to this collection, so a signed-in client can still grind repeated bounded writes. The full fix is to move every counter mutation server-side (Admin SDK bypasses rules) and set `allow write: if false`, keeping the client as a sum-on-read consumer. `counterShardForgeryGuard.test.js` (static + emulator) now asserts the absolute mint `assertFails` (create and update) while a legitimate ±1 increment `assertSucceeds` — the earlier `assertSucceeds`-on-forge pin is removed. |
| N208 a community post created by the server callable had no `expiresAt`, and the get-rule RAISED on the absent field — the post was unreadable by everyone, including its own members | ✅ **now closed** | `postReadableByViewer` evaluated `resource.data.expiresAt` directly, which **raises** when the field is absent, denying the whole `get`. `functions/communities.js#createCommunityPost` (the UI's `CreatePost` → `communityService.createCommunityPost` → callable path) wrote no `expiresAt`, so a community post created through the app was unreadable by every viewer (the client always writes `expiresAt: null`, so this only bit the server path). Fixed both ends: the rule now uses `resource.data.get('expiresAt', null) == null || resource.data.expiresAt > request.time` (missing = never expires; membership/privacy gates unchanged), and `createCommunityPost` writes the canonical `expiresAt: null`. Emulator-backed N208 check (member reads a no-`expiresAt` community post; a non-member is still denied on a private community; a public post is readable). |
| N209 several admin-gated Cloud Functions changed state with no audit record, and two admin maintenance callables were deployed but unreachable from any surface | ✅ **now closed** | AGENTS.md requires every admin mutation to record through `functions/admin.js#writeAudit`. A source scan of every `assertAdmin`/`checkIsAdmin`-gated callable that also writes state found five without a trail: `deleteUserData` (`functions/user.js` — the most destructive operation the platform performs, self-service or admin, was unattributable), `generateFriendRecommendations` (writes another user's recommendations), `createBulkNotificationJob` (broadcasts to an audience), `generateAudioFingerprint` + `updateViralScore` (`functions/video.js` — operator re-run levers), and `backfillPrivacyBoundary` (`functions/privacyMigration.js` — a data migration). All now call `writeAudit(...)` with the caller's uid and a `system`/`video`/`user`/`notification` target type. Separately, `FUNCTIONS.BACKFILL_POST_PRIVACY` (`backfillPostPrivacy`) was a dead contract key — deployed, admin-gated, but nothing called it, so the legacy-`authorIsPrivate` sweep could not be run at all; it is now wired into `AdminSystemHealthScreen` (a *Maintenance Operations* panel) through a new `callableService.backfillPostPrivacy()` helper. Two new hard-failing guards freeze the boundary: `functionsInventoryGuard.test.js` (no dead `FUNCTIONS` key, no ad-hoc `httpsCallable(..., 'name')` literal that bypasses the map, and a baseline of the 16 legitimately client-unreferenced callables — operator tools and superseded duplicates — that may only shrink) and `adminAuditGuard.test.js` (no admin-gated callable writes state without an audit record; read-only admin lists are exempt). Both run in the `guards` CI job. |
| N210 `daily_upload_counters` trusted the `userId` FIELD but never bound the document ID to the caller | ✅ **now closed** | The rule was `allow read, delete: if isOwner(resource.data.userId)` / `allow create, update: if isOwner(request.resource.data.userId)` — it authorised on the payload field only. The shard id is `upload_{userId}_{YYYY-MM-DD}_shard_{n}` (`videoService._checkDailyUploadLimitSharded`), and `shardId` is client-chosen, so any signed-in user could create `daily_upload_counters/upload_{victim}_...` carrying `userId: victim`, resetting or inflating the victim's daily video-upload counter and defeating the per-tier upload limit (a pre-seeded `count` also makes the legitimate owner's own increment exceed the limit and fail). Fixed by pinning the id to the caller (`shardId.matches('upload_' + uid() + '_[0-9]{4}-[0-9]{2}-[0-9]{2}_shard_[0-9]+')`), mirroring `unread_counters`. Emulator-backed N210 check in `firestoreRulesCompile.test.js` (owner shard admitted; a shard under another id, and an arbitrary id carrying another `userId`, both denied) + a static guard. |
| N211 `functions/saml.js` claimed to reject encrypted assertions but did not, and its attribute parser could not read the standard nested `<AttributeValue>` shape | ✅ **now closed** | The module header states "SAML Response with encrypted assertions … is rejected explicitly rather than silently", but no code rejected them: an `<EncryptedAssertion>` decoded to ciphertext, the issuer/audience regexes found nothing, and the request failed with a *misleading* `issuer mismatch`. Added an explicit `failed-precondition` rejection before the issuer check. Separately, `extractAttr` only matched the flat `<Attribute Name="x">value</Attribute>` form; a real IdP sends `<Attribute Name="x"><AttributeValue>value</AttributeValue></Attribute>`, so `displayName`/`role` silently fell back to defaults. The regex now tolerates the nested wrapper. New `privacyPropagationAndSaml.test.js` exercises the pure helpers (including a genuine RSA-SHA256 verify with a generated key pair), pins the auth gate + issuer/audience + unsigned/encrypted rejection, and pins the propagation callable's owner-scoping/bounded paging/audit. |
| N212 the withdrawal settlement accepted a caller-supplied coin→USD rate, and the economy summary re-derived the rate by hand | ✅ **now closed** | `settleWithdrawal` took an optional `deps.coinsPerDollar` and computed `coinsToUsd(amount) * (COINS_PER_DOLLAR / coinsPerDollar)`; both callers happened to pass the canonical value, so the override was inert — but it was a second source of truth for a real-money payout (any future caller could settle at a different rate than the creator was shown). Removed the override; settlement reads the shared config only (`functionsContract.test.js` now asserts no `coinsPerDollar` and no `: 200` literal). `admin.getEconomySummary` also divided `circulatingCoins / COINS_PER_DOLLAR` by hand instead of using `coinsToUsd`, re-implementing the rounding policy; routed through the helper and added a functions-side no-raw-division guard in `sharedConfigSync.test.js`. Canonical model recorded in `docs/engineering/money-model.md`. |
| N213 search returned blocked users' content — moderation was not enforced in `searchService` (the feed filtered blocks; search did not) | ✅ **now closed** | AGENTS.md §7.17 requires moderation to be enforced "throughout feeds, profiles, comments, **search**, notifications and messaging", and `feedService._getBlockedUsersCached` filters blocked authors from the home feed. `searchService` had **no** block filter at all: a user the viewer blocked (or who blocked the viewer) still surfaced in people, post and video results (Algolia, vector and the Firestore fallback), including the sponsored slot. Search is the natural discovery surface for a blocked harasser, so this is a real safety gap. Fixed: `_getBlockedUserIds(viewerId)` reads **both** directions of the block through the graph spine (`listConnections(viewerId, 'blocked')` + the new `'blocked_by'` kind) and caches the union for 60s; `_filterBlockedHits` (pre-fusion) and `_filterBlockedItems` (on the built list) drop blocked authors; the cache key is now **per-viewer** (`v: this._resolveViewerId(options)`) so one viewer's filtered page cannot leak to another; the sponsored slot is dropped when its author is blocked. `socialGraphService.LIST_SPECS` gained `blocked_by` (`blocks` where `blockedId == uid`, ordered by `blockerId` — the shape the rule proves, and the `(blockedId, blockerId)` composite index already exists). New `searchBlockFiltering.test.js` (in the `guards` CI job) pins the merge/cache, the filters, the per-type author resolution and the source-level wiring; `socialGraphService.test.js` gained a `blocked_by` listEdges case. |
| N214 `useSearch` read `response.hasNextPage` / `response.cursor`, but `searchService.search` returns `hasMore` / `nextCursor` — search pagination was dead | ✅ **now closed** | The service response is `{ items, total, page, totalPages, hasMore, nextCursor, … }`, but both the initial search and `loadMore` in `src/hooks/useSearch.js` read `response.hasNextPage` / `response.cursor` — keys that never exist. So `setHasMore(false)` and `setCursor(null)` ran on **every** successful search: `SearchScreen`'s load-more (which renders on `hasMore`) never appeared, and a manual `loadMore` always sent `cursor: null` (page 1 again). A silently-dead feature, the exact class AGENTS.md calls out ("a clickable-looking element that does nothing is a defect"). Fixed to read `response.hasMore` / `response.nextCursor`. New `searchPaginationWiring.test.js` (in the `guards` CI job) drives the real hook against a mocked service: `hasMore`/`cursor` now track the response and `loadMore` re-queries with the returned cursor and appends page 2. |
| N215 `searchService.search` returned an EMPTY page for every `page > 0`, and `hasMore` was always `false` — the service asked the provider for page N, then sliced `finalHits[page*hitsPerPage…]` out of a fusion list that only held that one page | ✅ **now closed** | `_executeSearch` called `_algoliaSearch(query, …, page, hitsPerPage, …)` (and the Firestore fallback likewise) so `resultsByType` held exactly one page, then did `const start = page * hitsPerPage; finalHits.slice(start, start + hitsPerPage)` — for page 1 that slice starts at index 20 of a 20-element array, so `items` was `[]`. `total` was the page-local length (20) and `hasMore = page + 1 < totalPages` was `1 < 1` → false, so even the now-correct `useSearch` wiring (N214) could never advance past page 1. Reproduced with a fake Algolia client (page 1 → `[]`, `hasMore:false`). Fixed by fetching a **window** of pages in one provider query (`SEARCH_CONFIG.PAGINATION.FETCH_PAGES`, default 5 → `hitsPerPage * 5` hits, one `multipleQueries` call, no N+1) and slicing the requested page out of the fused window; `total` now reports the provider's real `nbHits` when Algolia answered (else the window size — never fabricated), and `hasMore` is `window overflows || provider total > consumed || window full`. New `searchPaginationService.test.js` (in the `guards` CI job) pins page-1 content, `hasMore`/`total`/`totalPages`/`nextCursor`, the short-result-set case, and that the provider is asked for a window (not one page). |
| N216 comment "load more" was dead — `CommentsDrawer.handleLoadMore` sent `startAfter: postState.nextCursor` and read `result.hasMore`/`result.nextCursor`, but `commentService.getCommentsByPost` never applied a cursor and never returned `nextCursor`; its cache-hit path also dropped the pagination fields | ✅ **now closed** | `getCommentsByPost` built its query from `where`/`orderBy`/`limit` only — it never read `options.startAfter` (the name `CommentsDrawer` passes) or `options.cursor`, and its return object had no `nextCursor`, so the drawer's `endReached` load-more re-fetched page 1 on every call and (deduped by id in `appendComments`) appended nothing — an infinite-scroll that silently never scrolls. The cache-hit early return also returned only `{comments}` (no `hasMore`/`nextCursor`), so a warm cache disabled load-more entirely. `getReplies` already had the correct `options.startAfter || options.cursor` + `startAfter(cursorSnap)` handling; `getCommentsByPost` now mirrors it (page size capped by `MAX_FETCH_LIMIT`), computes `hasMore = snapshot.size === pageSize` / `nextCursor = last doc id`, and stores **and** returns both fields on the fresh and cached paths. New `commentPaginationWiring.test.js` (in the `guards` CI job) drives the real method against a stubbed Firestore: page 1 returns `hasMore`/`nextCursor`, a cursor query applies `startAfter` and returns page 2, and a cache hit still carries the pagination fields; `integrationWiring.test.js` gains a source-level pin alongside the existing `getReplies` one. |
| N217 `communityService.listCommunities` pushed a bare **number** (`(page - 1) * COMMUNITIES_PER_PAGE`) into the Firestore `query()` constraint list — every "Load More" (page > 1) threw "Invalid QueryConstraint" and the screen toasted "Failed to load communities"; `hasMore` also counted *filtered* rows | ✅ **now closed** | `constraints.push(startAfter)` where `startAfter` was an integer, not a `startAfter()` QueryConstraint: `query()` rejects it, so `CommunityDirectoryScreen`'s Load More always failed. Replaced with real doc-id cursor pagination (`options.startAfterId` → point-read the cursor doc → `startAfter(cursorSnap)`; a `page > 1` offset fallback keeps callers that pass only `page` working). `hasMore` now derives from the **raw page size** (`querySnapshot.docs.length === COMMUNITIES_PER_PAGE`), not the post-`searchQuery`-filtered length (a local filter could shrink the page below 20 and falsely report the end of the list). Returns `nextCursor`; the screen keeps the cursor in a ref and passes it on Load More. |
| N218 `eventService.listEvents` accepted `page` but never applied it, and ignored the screen's `status` filter — "Load More" re-fetched page 1 forever and the Upcoming/Live tabs showed every status | ✅ **now closed** | `listEvents` hardcoded `where('status','in',['published','open','live'])` and never read `options.page` or `options.status`, so `EventDiscoveryScreen`'s `options.status = ['published','open']` / `['live']` (and its page-based Load More) were dead. Now honors `options.status` (falls back to the discovery set), applies real cursor pagination (`startAfterId` + `page` offset fallback), and returns `nextCursor`; the screen passes the cursor from a ref. Three missing composite indexes were added (`(status, createdAt)`, `(status, type, stats.attendeeCount)`, `(status, type, createdAt)`) so the Popular/Recently-Added sorts with a type filter do not fail `FAILED_PRECONDITION`; `firestoreIndexCoverage.test.js` confirms coverage. New `directoryPaginationService.test.js` (in the `guards` CI job) drives both real list methods against a stubbed Firestore whose `query()` rejects a non-constraint (proving the bare-number bug is gone) and asserts the cursor query returns page 2. |
| N219 `messagesService.getUserConversations` fed its string `nextCursor` (a conversation id) straight into `startAfter()` — which requires a `DocumentSnapshot` — so every "load more" threw, and the cursor pointed at the last doc of the 2× over-fetch window (not the last doc returned); the store also re-sent that stale cursor on any non-refresh reload | ✅ **now closed** | `getUserConversations` built `q = query(q, startAfter(options.startAfter))` where `options.startAfter` is the opaque **id** the store passes back — the modular SDK's `startAfter()` rejects a string, so paginating conversations always failed; and `nextCursor` was `snap.docs[last].id` of the `limit * 2` fetch, so a page-2 request skipped the 20 conversations the caller never saw. `messagingStore.loadConversations` compounded it by sending `state.conversationsNextCursor` on **any** non-refresh reload (e.g. after creating a conversation), appending an old page instead of refreshing. Fixed: the service point-reads the cursor doc and applies `startAfter(cursorSnap)`; `hasMore = snap.docs.length > pageSize` and `nextCursor` = the last **returned** conversation id (never the over-fetch tail); the store continues from a cursor only when the caller explicitly passes `options.startAfter`. New `conversationPaginationService.test.js` (in the `guards` CI job) drives the real method against a stubbed Firestore whose `query()` rejects a non-constraint, asserting page 1 returns 20 with `nextCursor='c19'` and page 2 resolves the string cursor and returns the tail. |
| N220 `SavedScreen` mapped a `likes` field onto every saved card (`likes: p.likeCount \|\| 0` for posts, a hardcoded `likes: 0` for videos) that **nothing in the screen renders** — a dead field that invented an engagement number | ✅ **now closed** | Neither mapping block is read anywhere in `SavedScreen` (`item.likes` has no consumer), so the post branch coerced a missing `likeCount` to `0` and the video branch hardcoded `0` — a fabricated engagement value on a screen that shows no engagement at all. Removed both dead assignments. `noFabricatedData.test.js` gains a pin (`SavedScreen` must not contain `likes: p.likeCount || 0,` or `likes: 0,`). |
| N221 A **block did not stop a DM**. `sendMessage`'s privacy catch treated `messaging/blocked` the same as `messaging/privacy` and filed a **message request** (`sendMessageRequest`) — and the `firestore.rules` conversation-create rule accepts a conversation off any `pending` `message_requests` doc (`dmRequestFrom`), which has no block check — so a blocked user still landed in their blocker's request inbox and could re-open the thread. `sendMessageRequest` / `startDirectConversation` had no block guard either. | ✅ **now closed** | Two-layer fix. **Client** (`messagesService`): the `messaging/blocked` case is no longer downgraded to a request (`sendMessage` only offers a request for `messaging/privacy`); `sendMessageRequest` now returns `{success:false, blocked:true}` when either party has blocked the other (via the existing `isBlocked` graph-spine check); `startDirectConversation` maps that to `{status:'blocked'}`; `NewConversationScreen` shows "You cannot message this user." for both 1:1 entry points. **Rules** (`firestore.rules`): a new `blockEdgeBetween(a,b)` helper (`exists()` on both `blocks/a_b` and `blocks/b_a` — safe for an absent edge, unlike a point read) is AND-ed into the `conversations/{id}` create rule for 2-participant docs, so no `pending` request can override a block; groups are unaffected. Verified by new `startDirectConversation.test.js` block-guard tests (5) and a new emulator-backed `firestoreRulesCompile.test.js` case (blocked party denied, blocker denied, group allowed). |
| N222 **Mute was a dead control.** `userService.muteUser` writes a `mutes` edge and `ProfileOptionsMenu` exposes mute/unmute, but no feed surface read it: `feedService` filtered only `blocks` (via `_getBlockedUsersCached`), so muting a user left every one of their posts in the home feed. | ✅ **now closed** | `feedService._getHiddenAuthorsCached` now delegates to the graph spine's shared `getHiddenAuthorIds(userId)` (see N225), which unions **blocked + blocked_by + muted** in one bounded read; all six feed builders hide the union. `noFabricatedData.test.js` pins the delegation and bans a direct `blocks`/`mutes` query in the feed; `feedService.test.js` gains a delegation test + a fail-open test (a graph read error hides nobody, never throws). |
| N223 **The feed filtered only one direction of a block.** `feedService._getHiddenAuthorsCached` read `blocked` (users the viewer blocks) but not `blocked_by` (users who block the viewer), so a viewer who had been **blocked by** someone kept seeing that person's posts in every feed — the blocker's content leaked straight back to the party they blocked. `searchService` already read both directions (`blocked` + `blocked_by`); the feed did not. | ✅ **now closed** | The spine's `getHiddenAuthorIds` (N225) includes `blocked_by`, so the feed's hidden-author union covers both block directions, matching search's two-direction model. Both `blocks` composite indexes already exist (`(blockerId,blockedId)` and `(blockedId,blockerId)`), so the reversed read is index-backed. `noFabricatedData.test.js` pins all three spine reads. |
| N224 **The Sparks video feed had no moderation filter.** `videoService.getVideoFeed`'s direct `videos` (and `posts` fallback) query filtered only `isDeleted` — no block/mute — so a blocked, blocked-by or muted creator's videos still surfaced in Sparks (the primary path goes through `feedService`, which filters; this fallback did not). | ✅ **now closed** | New `videoService._getHiddenAuthors(userId)` delegates to the spine's `getHiddenAuthorIds` (bounded, fails open on error) and the fallback filters those author ids. `noFabricatedData.test.js` gains a pin asserting the delegation + the filter call. |
| N225 **Friend recommendations ignored blocks/mutes.** `socialGraphService.getSuggestions` returned the server callable's `user_recommendations` and its friends-of-friends fallback with **no** safety filter, so a user the viewer had blocked (or who had blocked the viewer, or been muted) could be suggested back. | ✅ **now closed** | New `socialGraphService.getHiddenAuthorIds(userId)` unions `blocked` + `blocked_by` + `muted` (bounded, fails open on error); `getSuggestions` drops any suggested id in that set (and the viewer themselves) on **both** the callable and fallback paths. `socialGraphService.test.js` gains a union test, a fail-open test, and a blocked-candidate exclusion test. |
| N226 **A block hid content in only one direction on the comment and home-feed surfaces.** `CommentsDrawer` loaded its hidden set from `userService.getBlockedUsers` (outgoing `blocked` only), so a user who **blocked the viewer** still had their comments rendered to the viewer; `HomeScreen`'s own optimistic feed filter read the same outgoing-only list. A block is a mutual denial — the reverse direction must be hidden too (the service feed and search already read both). | ✅ **now closed** | New `userService.getBlockedUserIds(uid)` returns the union of `blocked` + `blocked_by` through the spine (`listEdges`, ids only). `CommentsDrawer` and `HomeScreen` now use it; `getBlockedUsers` remains only for the manage-my-blocks screen. New `rankingBlockFiltering.test.js` (guards job) drives the real service against a stubbed Firestore + spine; `noFabricatedData.test.js` pins it. |
| N240 **Follower/following/friends lists showed blocked users.** `socialGraphService.listConnections` (followers/following) and `listMutualConnections` (friends) hydrated a viewer-projected profile for a blocked/blocked-by/muted user — masking hides the bio but the row and the account still appear in the viewer's own `ConnectionsScreen` lists (and the friends list in `NewConversationScreen`). | ✅ **now closed** | Both now resolve `getHiddenAuthorIds(viewerId)` and drop hidden ids (and the viewer's own id) before hydration; with no `viewerId` the raw list is returned. `socialGraphService.test.js` adds five cases (incl. the safety-management lists staying intact); `noFabricatedData.test.js` pins it. |
| N241 **Live discovery showed blocked hosts.** `liveService.getActiveLiveStreams` listed every `live_streams` doc with `status == 'live'` and no block filter, so a blocked/blocked-by/muted host's stream still surfaced in `LiveScreen` (the live *comments* were filtered under N232, but the stream list itself was not). | ✅ **now closed** | `getActiveLiveStreams` drops hidden hosts when a `viewerId` is supplied (same rule as the video feed); `LiveScreen` passes `user?.uid`. `noFabricatedData.test.js` pins it. |
| N242 **The Sparks grid/list feed showed blocked creators.** `VideosScreen` (the `/videos` grid and list tabs, distinct from the immersive `ReelsScreen`) called `videoService.getVideoFeed(null, …)` for both the first page and "load more". `getVideoFeed` maps a null viewer to the sentinel `'anonymous_user'`, so `_getHiddenAuthors('anonymous_user')` read the block/mute graph for a uid that has none and returned an empty set — the N224 fallback filter was inert and a blocked/blocked-by/muted creator's Sparks still surfaced in the grid. | ✅ **now closed** | Both `VideosScreen` calls now pass `user?.uid ?? null` (and the loader callbacks list `user?.uid`), so the hidden-author set is resolved for the real viewer. `noFabricatedData.test.js` pins the two call sites, the `useAuth` wiring and the absence of the null-viewer call. |
| N243 **Event discovery stranded later pages when a hidden organizer was on a full page.** `eventService.listEvents` filtered blocked/blocked-by/muted organizers out of the fetched page and then computed `hasMore = events.length === EVENTS_PER_PAGE`. A hidden organizer on an otherwise-full page shortened `events` below `EVENTS_PER_PAGE`, so `hasMore` became `false` and `EventDiscoveryScreen` stopped paginating — every later page was unreachable (the same class as the N217 `hasMore`-counts-filtered-rows bug in `listCommunities`). | ✅ **now closed** | `hasMore` now derives from the **raw** page (`querySnapshot.docs.length === EVENTS_PER_PAGE`), matching `listCommunities`. `noFabricatedData.test.js` pins it. |
| N244 **A block/mute did not hide already-delivered notifications.** N231 stopped `sendNotification` from *writing* a notification across a block, but the notification **read** paths never consulted the graph: `getUserNotifications` (the `getUserNotifications` callable) and the realtime `subscribeToUserNotifications` subscription both returned every stored row, so a notification delivered before the block (or from a muted user) still rendered in the Alerts inbox and streamed into the screen. | ✅ **now closed** | `notificationsService` gains `_getHiddenSenders(userId)` (spine `getHiddenAuthorIds` = blocked + blocked-by + muted, 60 s cache) and `_filterHiddenSenders` (system notifications with no `senderId` always survive). Both read paths apply the filter (the callable path filters before caching; the subscription filters the `added`/`modified` change batch). `notificationBlockFiltering.test.js` (guards job) drives the real service against a stubbed spine. **Known limitation (LOW):** the sharded unread counter (`getNotificationStats` / `subscribeToNotificationCount`) is server-side and cannot be filtered per-sender on the client, so the nav badge may briefly over-count pre-block notifications until they are cleared; no new such rows can be created after N231. |
| N245 **A block did not remove the existing direct thread from the inbox.** N221 made a block terminal for *creating* a DM (client guard + the `blockEdgeBetween` rules helper), but `messagesService.getUserConversations` returned every conversation whose `participants` array-contains the viewer, so a thread opened before the block kept rendering in both inboxes — the block looked like a no-op. | ✅ **now closed** | `getUserConversations` now resolves the blocked-pair set (spine `blocked` + `blocked_by` edges, both directions, bounded, fails open) and drops any `type: 'direct'` conversation whose other participant is in it; **mutes are deliberately NOT filtered** (a muted thread stays in the inbox, just quiet) and group/channel threads are unaffected. `blockUser`/`unblockUser` now call `_invalidateUserConversationsCache` for both parties so the change is immediate instead of waiting out the cache TTL. `messagingInboxBlockFiltering.test.js` (guards job) drives the real service: blocked-out, blocked-by, mute-kept, fail-open and cache-invalidation cases. |
| N246 **A block left pending follow/friend requests actionable, and approving one re-minted the blocked edge.** `followUser`/`sendFriendRequest` refuse to *file* a request across a block (N221-class), but a request filed before the block stayed in the owner's Network tab (`getFollowRequests`/`getFriendRequests` returned every pending row) and `approveFollowRequest`/`acceptFriendRequest` created the `follows` edge regardless — so an owner could resurrect the very edge the block removed, and a blocked party could keep an actionable "approve" affordance on the blocker's screen. | ✅ **now closed** | `userService._blockedPairIds` (spine `blocked` + `blocked_by`, bounded, fails open) filters both request read paths; `_isBlockedPair` gates both consent writes, which close the request (`status: 'blocked'`) and skip the counters/notification/XP side effects instead of minting the edge; a graph read error propagates out of the transaction, so the write aborts (fails closed) without writing a destructive terminal status on a transient blip. `requestInboxBlockFiltering.test.js` (guards job) drives the real service: follow-request hidden, blocked-by hidden, unrelated kept, friend-request filtered, fail-open read, and both consent writes refusing across a block. |
| N247 **`getMutualConnections` leaked blocked parties back into the "mutual friends" surface.** N240 filtered `listConnections`/`listMutualConnections`, but `getMutualConnections` — the reader the profile "Mutual friends" row uses (`profileStore`, `ConnectionsScreen`) — returned the callable's raw `mutualFriends` (or the unfiltered fallback intersection) with no safety filter, so a blocked/blocked-by/muted user reappeared there. | ✅ **now closed** | `getMutualConnections` now resolves `getHiddenAuthorIds(viewerId)` and filters both the callable result and the local fallback (the callable itself runs as admin and does not exclude blocks). `socialGraphService.test.js` (guards job) pins a blocked mutual being dropped while an unrelated mutual survives. |
| N248 **The `userService.searchUsers` fallback ignored blocks.** The primary path (`searchService.searchUsers`) filters blocked pairs, but when the Algolia-backed service throws, `userService.searchUsers` fell back to a raw `users` collection query with **no** block filter — so a blocked user reappeared in the mention autocomplete and the New-conversation/Group people pickers whenever Algolia was unavailable. | ✅ **now closed** | The fallback now resolves the viewer's blocked-pair set (same helper the request inboxes use, renamed `_blockedPairIds` since it is no longer request-specific) and drops those users; the viewer id comes from `options.userId` or the live session. `requestInboxBlockFiltering.test.js` (guards job) forces the fallback (searchService stub throws) and pins blocked-user exclusion + the no-edge pass-through. |
| N249 **`feedService.getFollowSuggestions` was a second, unfiltered suggestion engine.** It ran its own friends-of-friends + popular-creator scan (`isCreator == true` ordered by `followerCount`) with no block/mute filter, so a blocked or muted creator could be suggested. It had no caller, while the canonical, safety-filtered `socialGraphService.getSuggestions` (N225) is the one the app uses. | ✅ **now closed** | `getFollowSuggestions` now delegates to `socialGraphService.getSuggestions` (blocked/blocked-by/muted excluded) and keeps its fail-closed error contract; the now-dead `_getUserService` helper was removed. `feedService.test.js` (guards job) pins delegation + the fail-closed path. |
| N250 **Event Discovery's search box was a dead control.** `EventDiscoveryScreen` collected a `searchQuery`, debounced it, and rendered `Results for "…"` / "Try adjusting your search" copy — but never passed it to the service. `eventService.listEvents` had no `searchQuery` option, so typing filtered nothing (every search showed the full discovery feed). The only method that could search, `searchEvents`, was never called. | ✅ **now closed** | `listEvents` gains a `searchQuery` option: a non-empty search fetches one bounded window (`MAX_EVENT_READ` = 200, since Firestore cannot substring-match) and filters on title/description/location, returning `hasMore: false` (a filtered page cannot serve a stable cursor); `EventDiscoveryScreen` passes the trimmed query and re-loads on it. The dead `searchEvents` method was removed. `directoryPaginationService.test.js` (guards job) pins the title/description match, the no-match empty result and the single-window `hasMore`. |
| N251 **Event organizer controls and attendee feedback were dead service methods with no UI.** `eventService` exposed `deleteEvent` (the only path that sets `status: 'cancelled'`, a state `EventDetailScreen` already renders), `checkInAttendee`, `promoteFromWaitlist`, `submitFeedback` and `getEventFeedback` (the reader for the `event_feedback` collection + its index) — but **none** had a caller. The organizer's "Event Management" card offered only Edit and Copy Link, so an event could never be cancelled, an attendee never checked in, the waitlist never promoted, and the aggregate `stats.feedbackScore` (written by `functions/events.js#submitEventFeedback`) never rendered. | ✅ **now closed** | `EventDetailScreen` gains an organizer **Cancel Event** button (confirm → `deleteEvent`), a per-attendee **Check in** action and a **Promote next attendee** action (both organizer-only, server-authoritative callables), a **star-rating** control for registered attendees (`submitFeedback`, pre-filled from `getEventFeedback` so re-rating shows the current value), and a **Rating** stat tile reading `stats.feedbackScore`. `getUpcomingEvents` / `getCommunityEvents` — dead, unbounded-by-query-shape readers with no caller — were removed. `directoryPaginationService.test.js` pins the new methods delegating to the correct callables. |
| N252 **The messaging "Archived" folder was unreachable — `archivedBy` had no writer.** `MessagingScreen` maps `category: c.archivedBy?.includes(uid) ? 'archived' : …` and exposes an **Archived** filter tab, but `messagesService.archiveConversation` / `unarchiveConversation` — the only writers of `archivedBy` — had no caller anywhere. Every conversation therefore mapped to `personal`/`group`, the Archived tab matched nothing, and there was no way to archive a thread. (The sibling `hiddenFor` field *is* live — written by the request-accept path — so only the archive pair was dead.) | ✅ **now closed** | `ConversationSettingsScreen` gains an **Archive / Unarchive Conversation** control wired to `archiveConversation`/`unarchiveConversation` (state seeded from `conversation.archivedBy`); the Archived tab now receives conversations. `conversationPaginationService.test.js` (guards job) pins the `archivedBy` union/remove writes and the screen wiring. |
| N253 **Push tokens were never deregistered on sign-out — a logged-out device kept receiving the account's notifications.** `notificationsService.removePushToken` (deletes `push_tokens/{uid}/devices/{deviceId}` and calls FCM `deleteToken`) had no caller, so `signOut` left this device's registration live: after logging out (or handing the phone to someone else) the device still received the previous account's push notifications. Registration (`requestPushPermission` → `_savePushToken`) and refresh existed; only teardown was missing. | ✅ **now closed** | `AuthContext.signOut` now calls `getNotificationsService().removePushToken(uid)` before `authService.signOut()` (so the owner-gated rules still authorise the delete), fire-and-forget so it never blocks logout. `notificationsWiringGuard.test.js` (guards job) pins the call and its ordering relative to `signOut()`. |
| N254 **The chat message menu offered Reply / Forward / Edit / Pin, but every one of those handlers was undefined — tapping them did nothing.** `MessageBubble` renders `onReply?.()`, `onForward?.()`, `onEdit?.()`, `onSave?.()` unconditionally, and `messagesService` has real `replyToMessage`/`forwardMessage`/`editMessage`/`pinMessage` implementations — but `ChatScreen` passed only `onReaction`/`onDelete`/`onSave`/`onReport`. So reply, forward, edit and (for groups) pin were silent no-ops, and `getPinnedMessages` fed a panel nothing could fill. | ✅ **now closed** | `ChatScreen` gains `handleReply` (sets a `replyTo` compose banner, attaches canonical `replyTo` metadata on send), `handleEdit` (`editMessage` + optimistic local update), `openForwardPicker`/`handleForwardTo` (`forwardMessage` into a picked conversation) and `handleTogglePin` (`pinMessage`/`unpinMessage`, admin-gated server-side); `MessageBubble` gains a Pin/Unpin menu item. `reelsAndMessagesWiring.test.js` (guards job) pins the handler wiring and the service calls. |
| N255 **The messaging inbox filter tabs (Groups / Personal / Channels) matched nothing, and a search-slider button opened no panel.** `MessagingScreen` mapped each conversation's `category` from `c.category`, which no writer ever sets — `messagesService.createConversation` stores the kind in `type` (`'direct' | 'group' | 'channel'`). Every conversation therefore fell through to `personal`, so the Groups and Channels tabs were permanently empty and a group/channel thread was mislabelled. Separately the header's filter-slider button toggled `showSearchFilters`, a value that only styled the button itself — no filter panel ever rendered — and the empty-state copy always read "No conversations yet" even when a filter or search was active with threads present. | ✅ **now closed** | The inbox derives `category` from `type` (`group`→`groups`, `channel`→`channels`, else `personal`, `archivedBy` still wins for Archived); the dead filter-slider button and its `showSearchFilters` state/`SlidersHorizontal` import are removed (the category pills are the real control); and the empty state now distinguishes an empty inbox, a no-match search and an empty filter view. `reelsAndMessagesWiring.test.js` (guards job) pins the mapping, the removed control and the empty-state copy. |
| N256 **The Alerts "System" filter tab could never match a notification.** `NotificationsScreen` buckets notifications by a derived `category`, but it only ever produced `Messages`/`Friends`/`Coins`/`All`; the System tab filters on `category === 'System'` (the other tabs each have a `type`-based fallback, System did not), so the tab matched nothing even though the service emits platform-generated types (`system_alert`, `maintenance`, `policy_update`, `security_alert`, `verification`, `new_feature`, …) with `senderId: 'system'`. | ✅ **now closed** | Platform-generated types now map to the `System` category via a `SYSTEM_NOTIFICATION_TYPES` set, so the tab receives them. `notificationsWiringGuard.test.js` (guards job) pins the mapping. |
| N257 **The signup phone-verification screen kept two write-only `useState` values (`recaptchaError`, `recaptchaReady`).** `SignupStep2VerifyContact` set `recaptchaError` on a reCAPTCHA failure and `recaptchaReady` on success, but neither value was ever read: the visible error + Retry affordance is rendered by the child `InvisibleRecaptcha`, and the parent's only real signal is the `recaptchaVerifier` object. `recaptchaReady` had even been listed as a `useMemo` dependency it was not used inside. `uiStateWiringGuard` carried a stale file-level exemption for exactly this pair. | ✅ **now closed** | Both write-only values and their setters are removed (`handleRecaptchaError`/`handleRecaptchaReady` now only manage `recaptchaVerifier`); the stale `WRITE_ONLY_EXEMPT` allowlist is deleted from `uiStateWiringGuard.test.js`, so the write-only-state scan now covers the file with no exemptions. |
| N258 **Dead derived values / refs that the linter does not flag.** `LoginScreen` computed a `useMemo` `isFormValid` (switching on `method`) but its buttons gate on a *different* pair (`canSubmitEmail`/`canSubmitPhone`), so the memo was dead; `PostCard/AudioCard` declared `const animationId = useRef(null)` while its animation loop used a local `rafId` (the ref was never assigned or read). Both are silently dead — ESLint does not flag an unreferenced local derived from a hook call the same way it flags an unused plain local. | ✅ **now closed** | Both declarations are removed. `uiStateWiringGuard.test.js` gains a conservative scan that fails when any `const x = useMemo/useCallback/useRef(...)` is never referenced (spread usage counts), so the class cannot regress. |
| N259 **Dead toggle controls: a `useState` boolean read only inside its own setter — the button flips a flag nothing renders.** `CreateStory`'s camera "Settings" button and the mobile chevron both toggled `toolsExpanded`, but no drawer read it (the only tool list is `hidden lg:flex`, i.e. desktop-only, so small screens had no way to open the creative tools at all); the desktop column also always rendered the tools regardless of the flag. `TextTool`'s colour swatch button toggled `showColorPicker` while the always-visible native `<input type="color">` beside it is the real control. This class passes both existing `uiStateWiringGuard` scans (the setter *is* reachable, and the value *is* read — inside its own setter), so it went unflagged. | ✅ **now closed** | `CreateStory`: the desktop tools drawer and the footer hint are now gated on `toolsExpanded`, and a new mobile (`lg:hidden`) creative-tools drawer renders the same `CREATIVE_TOOLS` list behind the toggle — so both controls now open/close real UI. `TextTool`: the dead swatch button and its `showColorPicker` state are removed (the native colour input remains). `uiStateWiringGuard.test.js` gains a 4th scan (strip every `setter(...)` call, then require the value identifier to survive) verified to fail on a reintroduced dead toggle. |
| N260 **Secret communities were world-readable, and the "obvious" fix silently re-opened the leak for list queries.** `firestore.rules` declared `match /communities/{communityId} { allow read: if true; }`, so any signed-in (or anonymous) client could point-read a `privacy: 'secret'` community's document — its name, description, rules and full `members` privilege map (roles/permissions). The community list/detail surfaces treat `secret` as members-only (`CommunityDetailScreen` gates posts on `privacy === 'public' \|\| members[uid]`), so the rule contradicted the product. The natural correction — `resource.data.get('privacy', 'public') != 'secret'` — is itself a bug: in a **list** rule the `.get(field, default)` form no longer proves a single permission outcome for the whole potential result set, so an unfiltered directory query returned the secret rows anyway (the Firestore emulator reproduced this; a direct `resource.data.privacy != 'secret'` correctly denies the whole list). The directory also offered a "Secret" filter chip whose query could only ever be denied. | ✅ **now closed** | The community document rule now reads `resource.data.privacy` **directly** (`!= 'secret' \|\| uid() in resource.data.members \|\| isAdmin()`), and the `{subcollection}` read follows the parent via a new `communityPrivacyOpen()` helper (so join-request/pending-member docs are members-only too). `listCommunities`/`searchCommunities`/`getRecommendedCommunities` constrain `privacy in ['public','private']` (the "all" branch), so the query proves a non-secret result set; the dead "Secret" directory chip and its unreachable `filter === 'secret'` page are removed; `CommunityDetailScreen` renders an explicit members-only state on `permission-denied`; the dead `getCommunityWithAccess` (which encoded a divergent privacy model — it hid *private* communities that are meant to be world-readable) is removed. `communityPrivacyRules.test.js` (static + emulator-backed adversarial) pins it. |
| N261 **Story replies were world-readable — a private replier → owner graph exposed to any client.** `story_replies/{id}` (written by `storyService._logStoryReply`) records `userId` (the replier) and `toUserId` (the story owner), yet the rule was `allow read: if true`. Any client could `getDoc`/list the collection and enumerate who replied to whom (plus each reply's `storyId`). No client ever reads the collection — it is a write-only reply log — so the read served only leakage. | ✅ **now closed** | Read is scoped to the two parties plus admins: `isSignedIn() && (resource.data.userId == uid() \|\| resource.data.toUserId == uid() \|\| isAdmin())`. `storyReplyPrivacyRules.test.js` (static + emulator-backed adversarial) pins it and is registered in the hard-failing `guards` job. |
| N262 **Message-poll votes were world-readable even though the feature declares them anonymous.** `polls/{pollId}/votes/{voteId}` keys the vote document by the voter's **uid** and stores `optionIndex`; the rule was `allow read: if true`. `messagesService.createPoll` defaults to `anonymousVotes: true`, so the rule contradicted the product — any client could list a conversation poll's voters and how each voted. (The live creator-poll path is separate and already server-only: flat `poll_votes` is `allow read, write: if false` and `posts/{id}/poll_votes` is readable only by the voter.) | ✅ **now closed** | Read is scoped to the voter plus admins: `isSignedIn() && (voteId == uid() \|\| isAdmin())`. The only reader (`messagesService.getPollResults`) had **no caller** (the message-poll UI — `createPoll`/`votePoll`/`closePoll`/`getPollResults` — was dead), so the four dead methods and their facade re-exports are removed, leaving no client reader that could regress. |
| N263 **Least-privilege reads for platform telemetry and live presence.** `system_health/{docId}` and `live_viewers/{streamId}_{viewerId}` were both `allow read: if isSignedIn()`. The only client reader of `system_health` is the admin health screen's round-trip probe, so a bare signed-in read exposed platform internals to every user; `live_viewers` names **who is watching a stream**, so a bare signed-in read exposed the live presence graph to every user (the only client read is `getLiveViewers`, a host-only viewer list). | ✅ **now closed** | `system_health` read is `isAdmin()`. `live_viewers` read is scoped to the viewer, the stream host and admins via a new `liveStreamHost(streamId)` helper (`resource.data.userId == uid() \|\| liveStreamHost(resource.data.streamId) == uid() \|\| isAdmin()`); the list query filters `streamId`, so the helper resolves a single host document. `livePresenceAndHealthPrivacyRules.test.js` (static + emulator adversarial) pins both. |
| N264 **Least-privilege reads for server bookkeeping, tenant SSO config and collaboration membership.** `system/{docId}` (coin-supply + scheduler cursors), `enterprise_tenants/{tenantId}` (SAML IdP metadata/ACS URLs) and `collaboration_projects/{projectId}` (+ its `team` roster) were all `allow read: if isSignedIn()`. None has a client reader that needs it — `system` is written/read only by Cloud Functions, `enterprise_tenants` only by `functions/saml.js`, and the collaboration dashboard lists the viewer's *own* projects — so every one leaked platform internals or membership/role (privilege) data to any signed-in user. | ✅ **now closed** | `system` and `enterprise_tenants` reads are `isAdmin()`. `collaboration_projects` and its subcollections are scoped to the owner, a team member or an admin via a new `collabProjectAccess(projectId)` helper (owner check + `exists(.../team/$(uid()))`); the dashboard's `where('ownerId','==',uid())` query proves access. `livePresenceAndHealthPrivacyRules.test.js` (static + emulator adversarial) covers all three. |

| N265 **Share records were world-readable — a private who-shared-what log.** `shares/{shareId}` (written by `firestoreService.sharePost`/repost) records `userId` (the sharer) and `postId`, but the rule was `allow read: if true`. No client ever reads the collection (the share count is a separate sharded counter), so the read only exposed who shared which post to any client. | ✅ **now closed** | Read is scoped to the sharer and admins: `isSignedIn() && (resource.data.userId == uid() || isAdmin())`. `livePresenceAndHealthPrivacyRules.test.js` (static + emulator adversarial) covers it. |
| N266 **`events/{eventId}` was readable by any signed-in client, so a `draft` (or `cancelled`/`archived`) event could be read by id even though no discovery list surfaces it.** (The former `privacy` field that also fed this row was a dead control — written, never read, never enforced — and is removed; see N304.) | ✅ **now closed** — the read is now status/relationship-gated: `isSignedIn() && (status in ['published','open','live','ended'] || organizerId == uid() || attendees[uid()] != null || isAdmin())`. The blocker the earlier deferral named — `eventService.getUserRegistrations`'s list query (`where('attendees.<uid>.status','in',…)`) cannot prove the status/organizer predicate (rules are not filters) — is resolved by serving that list server-side: new `getUserEventRegistrations` callable reads with the Admin SDK and returns a minimal projection; `eventService.getUserRegistrations()` delegates to it. `readScopePrivacyRules.test.js` proves the rule statically and adversarially on the emulator (draft: organizer + attendee succeed, stranger + anonymous fail; published: any signed-in succeeds). |

| N267 **`stories/{id}/analytics_shards` leaked one creator's private engagement to every signed-in user.** The shards hold `forwardTaps`/`backTaps`/`completions`/`comments`/`replies`; the roll-up runs server-side (`functions/stories.js`) and the creator sees the aggregates on the story document's `stats`, so no client reads the raw shards — yet the rule was `allow read: if isSignedIn()`. | ✅ **now closed** | Read is scoped to the creator and admins: `isSignedIn() && (vibeData().userId == uid() || isAdmin())`. `livePresenceAndHealthPrivacyRules.test.js` (static) pins it. |
| N268 **`collections/{id}/items` (saved posts) was readable by any signed-in user.** The parent `collections/{id}` is private to its owner, but the `items` subcollection read was a bare `isSignedIn()`, so any user could enumerate another user's saved posts by guessing/observing a collection id. | ✅ **now closed** | The `items` read now follows the parent ownership (`get(.../collections/$(collectionId)).data.userId == uid() || isAdmin()`), matching the create/update/delete clauses. `livePresenceAndHealthPrivacyRules.test.js` (static) pins it. |

| N269 **`live_gifts/{giftId}` (live-gift ledger) was readable by every signed-in user.** The doc records `senderId`, `recipientId`, `giftType` and `coinValue`, written by `sendLiveGift` (Admin SDK). No client reads it (the streamer sees `stats.coinsEarned` on the stream doc), so a signed-in read exposed the whole live-gift ledger; the sibling `gift_transactions` is already party-scoped. | ✅ **now closed** | Read is scoped to the two parties and admins (`senderId == uid() || recipientId == uid() || isAdmin()`). `livePresenceAndHealthPrivacyRules.test.js` (static) pins it. |
| N270 **`highlights` + `highlights/{id}/highlightStories` are readable by every signed-in user (LOW).** The highlight doc and its entries store `userId` + `storyId` and no story content. The read is `isSignedIn()` (not world-readable — an earlier draft of this row said `if true`; re-verified against the current rule). A cross-user read is intentional: `storyService.getHighlights(userId)` renders any profile's Highlights section. `highlightStories` leak only the reference ("user X highlighted story Y"); to render the actual story the client must read `stories/{id}`, which is independently visibility-gated (`vibeVisibleToViewer`), and `archived_stories` is private. | ℹ️ **NOT_AN_ISSUE / reviewed** — a highlight is profile content by design; the reference-only leak is acceptable and the underlying content stays gated. No change; re-classified after re-verification. |

| N271 **`reputation/{userId}` (a user's trust/reliability/contribution standing) was readable by every signed-in user.** The record is server-written only (`allow write: if isAdmin()`) and `rankingService.getUserReputation` has no caller, so a bare `isSignedIn()` read exposed every user's standing for no product reason. | ✅ **now closed** | Read is scoped to the subject and admins (`isOwner(userId) || isAdmin()`). `livePresenceAndHealthPrivacyRules.test.js` (static + emulator adversarial) pins it. |
| N272 **`post_analytics/{postId}` (aggregate per-post analytics) was world-readable.** The collection is server-written (`allow write: if false`) and has no writer today, but a public read would leak any future analytics the moment a server writer lands. The only consumer is the author-only analytics panel (`PostCard` → `firestoreService.getPostAnalytics`, gated on `isAuthor`). | ✅ **now closed** | Read is scoped to the post author and admins via `resource.data.authorId == uid()`. `livePresenceAndHealthPrivacyRules.test.js` (static + emulator adversarial) pins it. |
| N273 **`posts/{postId}/daily_stats` (per-day view stats) was world-readable — through the generic posts-subcollection wildcard.** The specific block was `allow read: if true`, but the read was in fact granted by the broader `match /{subcollection}/{docId}` rule (any signed-in user), so scoping only the specific block would not have denied the leak. `daily_stats` is server-written (`allow write: if false`) and read only by the author's analytics panel. | ✅ **now closed** | The specific block now reads author/admin (`get(.../posts/$(postId)).data.authorId == uid()`), and the wildcard excludes `daily_stats` (`subcollection != 'daily_stats'`) so it can no longer re-open the read. `livePresenceAndHealthPrivacyRules.test.js` (static + emulator adversarial) pins both. |

| N274 **`event_feedback/{feedbackId}` (each attendee's event rating + comment) was readable by every signed-in user.** The row is keyed `event_${eventId}_${uid}` and the only client reader (`EventDetailScreen`) fetched the whole list just to find the caller's own rating, so a bare `isSignedIn()` read exposed every attendee's rating/comment. The aggregate `stats.feedbackScore` on the event document is the public signal. | ✅ **now closed** | Read is scoped to the attendee and admins (`resource.data.userId == uid() || isAdmin()`); the service now does a deterministic point read (`getMyEventFeedback`) of the caller's own doc instead of the list. `livePresenceAndHealthPrivacyRules.test.js` (static) pins the rule. |

| N275 **`creator_profiles/{userId}` (official creator record: application bio/links, tier, `eligibilityStatus`, `monetizationEligible`, `policyStanding`, `approvedAt`) was readable by every signed-in user.** The only client reader is the owner's own application/status view (`creatorService.getCreatorProfile`, currently uncalled), so a bare `isSignedIn()` read exposed every creator's eligibility and policy standing. | ✅ **now closed** | Read is scoped to the owner and admins (`isOwner(userId) || isAdmin()`). `livePresenceAndHealthPrivacyRules.test.js` (static) pins it. |
| N276 **`ARVDOUL_STATUS.md` claimed "59 of 59 features complete … 100%" while citing 40 file paths that do not exist.** The whole Phase 11–20 set (items 40–59: `recommendationEngine`, `spacesOrchestrator`, `complianceGovernanceService`, `multiRegionMeshService`, `verifiableCredentialsService`, `digitalEscrowService`, `aiCoPilotDirectorService`, `predictiveAnalyticsService`, `communityGovernanceService`, `revenueSplitsService`, `liveInteractiveGamificationService`, `watchPartyService`, `contentProvenanceService`, `activityPubMeshService`, `chaosDefenseService`, `immutableAuditLedgerService`, `decentralizedStorageMeshService`, `decentralizedBountyMarketService`, `decentralizedTokenBondingCurveService`, `decentralizedModerationJuryService`), the Phase 7 gesture/lite-mode UI (`PullToRefresh.jsx`, `usePullToRefresh.js`, `LowEndModeToggle.jsx`), the Phase 9 `observabilityService.js`, the Phase 10 `shardedCounter.js`/`cacheControl.js`, all eleven `phaseN*.test.js` files, and a "Cloud Run containerized deployment build" were fabricated or never existed. | ✅ **now closed** | The document is rewritten as an evidence-based ledger: it records only verifiable paths, marks the fabricated items **REMOVED / NOT IMPLEMENTED**, and corrects the stale test counts (1,680 tests / 146 suites) and per-item claims (MFA is service-only, no cover photos by policy, Stripe is config-gated, live streaming is signalling-only, analytics funnels/cohorts/A-B absent, rankings have no writer = N175, `counter_shards` not server-authoritative = N207). `statusDocHonestyGuard.test.js` (guards job) fails if a documented path stops resolving or the 59/59 claim returns. |
| N277 **The Lite-Mode / Data-Saver capability was a dead control** — `deviceCapabilities.js` computed the tuning and `HomeScreen` consumed it, but nothing let a user turn it on and there was no pull-to-refresh, despite `ARVDOUL_STATUS.md` claiming both complete. | ✅ **now closed** | Added a real reactive Lite-Mode toggle in Settings (`settings.liteMode`, persisted via `setLiteModePreference`, reactive to `arvdoul-litemode-change`, i18n in all 7 locales) and native pull-to-refresh (`usePullToRefresh` non-passive touch hook + `PullToRefresh` indicator, wired into `HomeScreen`). `pullToRefresh.test.js` drives the real gesture path. |
| N278 **`Logger` had no remote sink** — structured JSON went only to the browser console, which no operator can read; there was no transport, batching, or flush. | ✅ **now closed** | `Logger` now exposes `setLogSink(fn, {minLevel})` + `flushLogSink()` with batched delivery of warn+ entries and an auto-installed `fetch` transport when `VITE_LOG_ENDPOINT` is set. No endpoint ⇒ no-op (nothing fabricated); transport failures never surface. `loggerSink.test.js`. |
| N279 **An event could be "wired" (a listener exists) while its payload shape drifted** — `deadEventGuard` proved only that a listener exists, not that it reads the payload the dispatcher builds, so a consumer could destructure `undefined`. | ✅ **now closed** | `eventPayloadContract.test.js` pins producer↔consumer payload shapes for the five `arvdoul:*` events that carry data and fails on any `CustomEvent` that omits `detail` without an allowlist entry. In the `guards` CI job. |
| N280 **MFA/TOTP was a lockout trap** — `enrollMFA`/`finalizeMFAEnrollment`/`verifyMFAAndSignIn` existed but nothing consumed them; `signInWithEmailPassword` formatted `auth/multi-factor-auth-required` into a generic error, so any account with a second factor could never finish signing in, and there was no enrollment UI at all. | ✅ **now closed** | `signInWithEmailPassword` returns `{ requiresMFA, resolver, hints }`; `AuthContext` passes it through and exposes `verifyMFAAndSignIn`; `LoginScreen` renders a TOTP code step; `SettingsScreen` enrolls (setup key + code) and disables factors via `getAuthService()`. `getEnrolledMFAFactors()` added. `mfaWiring.test.js` in the `guards` job. |
| N281 **Design-system state primitives used emoji as interface icons** — `ErrorState` rendered `⚠️` and `PermissionDenied` rendered `🔒`, violating the Arvdoul design language ("avoid emoji as interface icons"). | ✅ **now closed** | Both now render lucide `AlertTriangle` / `Lock` at a consistent 24px in the tokenised red chip. `sharedStatePrimitivesGuard.test.js` fails if any `src/design-system/*.jsx` reintroduces an emoji glyph. |
| N282 **PWA update flow was unreachable (and a `SKIP_WAITING` no-op)** — `sw.js` `install` called `self.skipWaiting()`, so a new worker activated immediately and `SWUpdateBanner` could never observe a waiting worker: the "Update available" prompt never appeared and the running bundle was swapped silently. The banner also posted `{type:'SKIP_WAITING'}` to a worker with no `message` handler (dead), and reloaded before activation. | ✅ **now closed** | `install` no longer self-activates; a `message` handler calls `skipWaiting()` only on `SKIP_WAITING`; the banner sets an `acceptedRef` and reloads on the resulting `controllerchange`. Cache bumped to `arvdoul-v3`. `swUpdateFlow.test.js` (guards job) pins it. |
| N283 **The composer fabricated an author identity when no session was present** — `CreatePost.publishPost` fell back to `localStorage.getItem("arvdoul_uid") || \`user_${Date.now().toString(36)}\`` and the display name `"Arvdoul Creator"` when it could not resolve a signed-in user, so a publish attempted after a raced sign-out wrote a post under a synthetic uid that matches no account (unattributable, orphaned content, and a spoofable `authorId` handed to `firestoreService.createPost`). The composer sits behind a `ProtectedRoute`, so this was a latent integrity hole rather than a normal path. | ✅ **now closed** | `publishPost` now throws `"You must be signed in to publish…"` when no real uid resolves, instead of inventing one; `noFabricatedData.test.js` gains a block pinning the absence of `arvdoul_uid`, the timestamp uid and the placeholder name. |
| N284 **`firestoreService.createPost` fabricated an author identity two ways** — when no session was present it (a) called `signInAnonymously(auth)`, minting a throwaway uid that owns the post but can never be the real author, and (b) failing that, accepted the caller-supplied `postData.authorId` verbatim (`currentUser = { uid: postData.authorId, ... }`). Either path let a post be written under an identity that is not the signed-in account. Arvdoul has no guest-authoring product flow. | ✅ **now closed** | Both fallbacks are removed; the service now throws `'User not authenticated. Please sign in to publish.'` when no real uid resolves, so the only `authorId` written is `auth.currentUser.uid`. `noFabricatedData.test.js` pins the absence of `signInAnonymously` and the caller-supplied-uid fallback. |
| N285 **Placeholder author identity was fabricated across the write + hydrate paths** — the same invented person (`"Arvdoul User"` / `User_<uid>`) and synthetic handle (`user_<uid>` / `'creator'`) appeared in `CreatePost.publishPost`, `firestoreService.createPost`/`repostPost`, `HomeScreen.hydratePost`, `commentService.createComment`/`replyToComment`, `SavedScreen` and `videoService` (saved-video snapshot). Each wrote or rendered an identity that reads as a real account but matches nobody, so posts/comments could not be attributed and the UI showed a `@user_8f3a2c1d` handle that resolves to no profile. | ✅ **now closed** | All sites now derive identity through the canonical read model: `pickDisplayName([...])` (neutral "Creator" fallback, placeholder names rejected) for the name and `deriveHandle(username) || deriveHandle(email) || ''` for the handle; the render sites (`PostCard`, `VideoComments`, `SavedScreen`, the composer preview) omit the `@handle` when it is empty instead of showing a synthetic one. `noFabricatedData.test.js` gains an "Author identity is never fabricated across write + hydrate paths" block pinning all six files. |
| N286 **Uncoerced transaction/bounty/reputation amounts could render fabricated numbers** — `CoinsScreen` transaction rows printed `{tx.amount?.toLocaleString?.() ?? tx.amount}`, so an unreadable amount rendered `+undefined`/`-undefined`; `QuestionCard` derived its bounty badge from `question?.bounty ?? 0` and its reputation band from `getReputationLevel(userReputation)` (raw prop), so a non-numeric bounty/reputation silently produced a fake 0 or a `NaN` band. | ✅ **now closed** | Both now route through the canonical `unknownNumber()` display guard: an unknown amount renders an explicit `—`, an unknown bounty renders no badge, and an unknown reputation renders `—` with no band. `noFabricatedData.test.js` gains a "no fabricated counts sweep (round 4)" block. |
| N287 **`analyticsService.getEngagementTrends` fabricated a measured 0 for every unrecorded day** — each per-day series coerced missing `engagement`/`likes`/`comments`/`shares`/`views`/`reach` to `|| 0`, so a chart drew a real-looking flat zero line where the metric was simply never recorded (inconsistent with `activeDays`/`changes`, which already use `null` = unknown). | ✅ **now closed** | The series now runs each metric through a finite-number guard and emits `null` for an unrecorded day; the chart's own `d.views || 0` scale math is unchanged (a bar of height 0 is an honest rendering of "no views that day"). `noFabricatedData.test.js` pins it. |
| N288 **Identity leaks/fabrication in the quick-access panel and video comments** — `QuickAccessPanel` built its fallback avatar as `https://ui-avatars.com/api/?name=<displayName>…`, sending the user's display name to a third party (and bypassing the canonical local SVG fallback); `VideoComments` mapped a missing commenter handle to the literal `'user'` and rendered `@{replyTo.user.username}`, so an anonymous commenter appeared as `@user`; `SavedScreen` fell back to a raw `/assets/default-profile.png` path instead of the shared avatar resolver. | ✅ **now closed** | `QuickAccessPanel` now uses `getSafeAvatarUrl` for both the initial and on-error src (no external request); `VideoComments` derives the handle with `deriveHandle` (null for placeholders) and the reply bar/placeholder gate on it; `SavedScreen` routes both post and video author avatars through `resolveAvatarUrl`. `noFabricatedData.test.js` pins all three. |
| N289 **Creator-dashboard sparklines drew fabricated zeros** — `ProfileCreatorDashboard` built its sparkline series as `dailyStats.map(d => Number(d.views) || 0)` (reach/engagement/coins alike), so a day whose metric was never recorded was plotted as a measured 0, and `formatTrend` coerced an unknown trend to `0.0%`. | ✅ **now closed** | Each series is now built with `unknownNumber`; if any point is unknown the whole series falls back to `null` and `Sparkline` renders the explicit "No trend data yet" placeholder. `formatTrend` reads the trend through `unknownNumber` (unknown → `—`). `noFabricatedData.test.js` pins both. |
| N290 **Achievement progress fabricated a measured 0% for unmeasurable criteria** — `achievementService.enrichAchievements` initialised `progress` to `0` and clamped every non-numeric criterion (isCreator/isRoyal/hostedLive/hasPassport/pollVotes/profilesVisited/giftsSent/lifetimeCoinsEarned/isPioneer) to that 0, so `AchievementsScreen` and `QuickAccessPanel` rendered "0% progress" for a locked boolean achievement — indistinguishable from a real 0-of-N. | ✅ **now closed** | Progress is now `null` unless a genuine numeric ratio exists (`ratio()` returns null for a non-finite/non-positive numerator or target); `unlocked` derives from `progress === 100`. Both surfaces render a null progress as a plain "Locked" label and skip the progress bar. `noFabricatedData.test.js` pins the service and both renderers. |
| N291 **Video-analytics chart plotted unrecorded days as 0 views** — `VideoAnalyticsScreen` built the per-day view series as `views: d.views || 0`, so a day the server never wrote drew a zero-height bar indistinguishable from a genuinely zero-view day. | ✅ **now closed** | The series maps each day through `unknownNumber` and drops unknown points; the bar height and `seriesMax` no longer coerce with `|| 0`. An all-unknown series falls back to the explicit "No daily view data yet" state. `noFabricatedData.test.js` pins it. |
| N292 **Verification modal fabricated 5% standing progress** — `ProfileVerificationModal` rendered the level-progress bar width as `Math.max(5, ((userLevel || 0) / requiredLevel) * 100)%`, so an account whose level had not loaded (`null`) showed a 5%-full bar toward the verification gate while the label read "Unranked". | ✅ **now closed** | Progress is computed as `standingProgress`: 0 when the level is unknown, otherwise the real `min(100, max(5, level/target))` fraction. `noFabricatedData.test.js` pins it. |
| N293 **Realtime post stats fabricated 0 for unwritten counters** — `firestoreService.subscribeToPostStats` mapped the post stats block with `stats.likes ?? 0` (comments/shares/gifts/views alike), so every realtime tick pushed a fabricated "0 Likes / 0 Comments" into `PostCard`'s `UPDATE_STATS` for a post whose counters had not been written. | ✅ **now closed** | The mapper now reports each counter through `unknownNumber` (unknown → null); `PostCard`'s reducer already keeps its prior value for a null (`action.payload.likes ?? state.likeCount`), so nothing fabricated is ever applied. `noFabricatedData.test.js` pins both. |
| N294 **Ranking badge totals treated a missing post counter as a measured 0** — `rankingService.getUserBadges` summed `Number(p.stats?.likes || p.likes || 0)` (views/comments alike) across the author's posts, so a post whose `stats` block was never written contributed a fabricated 0 and understated every badge total (and could show a badge "not earned" that the real total would earn). | ✅ **now closed** | Each counter is read through `unknownNumber`; if any counter is unknown the aggregate total is `null` (rendered '—'), never a fabricated partial sum. `noFabricatedData.test.js` pins it. |
| N295 **Audio posts labelled every author "Arvdoul Creator" in the OS media session** — `AudioCard`'s `_updateMediaSession` hardcoded `artist: 'Arvdoul Creator'`, so the lock-screen / media-notification card attributed every audio post to a nonexistent account. | ✅ **now closed** | `play()`/`_updateMediaSession()` take the real `authorName` (passed from `PostCard` as `post.authorName`); the artist is `pickDisplayName([authorName], { fallback: null })` and the field is omitted when the author is unknown rather than invented. `noFabricatedData.test.js` pins both. |
| N296 **Creator payout rows fabricated the amount and the settlement state** — `CreatorPayoutScreen` rendered `-${tx.amount?.toLocaleString?.() || tx.amount}` (raw/undefined for an unreadable amount) beside a hardcoded **"Completed"** badge, claiming every withdrawal — including a `pending` / `pending_review` / `failed` one — had settled. | ✅ **now closed** | The amount is read through `unknownNumber` (rendered '—' when unreadable) and the badge is derived from the ledger's own `status`/`state` (Completed / Pending / Failed, or no badge when unknown). `noFabricatedData.test.js` pins it. |
| N297 **Creator analytics engagement treated a missing post counter as a measured 0** — `analyticsService.getUserAnalytics`'s new-creator fallback summed `(s.likes \|\| 0) + (s.comments \|\| 0) + (s.shares \|\| 0)` over the top posts, so a post whose `stats` block was never written contributed a fabricated 0 and reported a measured-looking engagement that understated the real total. | ✅ **now closed** | Each counter is read through `unknownNumber`; if any is unknown the whole engagement aggregate is `null` (rendered '—'). `noFabricatedData.test.js` pins it. |
| N298 **Community stats fabricated 0 for counters the community doc never wrote** — `communityService.getCommunityStats` returned `community.stats?.memberCount \|\| 0` (and the same for postCount/eventCount/activityScore) and summed a weighted engagement with `(stats.likes \|\| 0)`, so an unwritten counter read as a measured 0. | ✅ **now closed** | Every counter is read through `unknownNumber` and the weighted `weeklyEngagement` is `null` when any post counter is unknown. `noFabricatedData.test.js` pins it. |
| N299 **Story insights and the coin leaderboard fabricated 0 for unwritten counters** — `storyService.getStoryInsights` / `subscribeToInsights` returned `story.stats?.comments \|\| 0` (and completionRate/forwardTaps/backTaps/replies/shares), and `monetizationService.getCoinLeaderboard` mapped `coins: doc.data().coins \|\| 0` with a position title derived from that 0. | ✅ **now closed** | Both read each counter through `unknownNumber`; an unknown balance stays `null` and gets no fabricated position title. `noFabricatedData.test.js` pins both. |
| N300 **Perks screen derived an XP bar from a fabricated 0 experience** — `PerksRewardsScreen` computed `Number(profile?.experience) \|\| 0` and fed it to `getLevelInfo`, so a profile that never stored `experience` rendered a measured-looking "0 XP to Level 2" bar. | ✅ **now closed** | `experience` is kept unknown when unwritten (`hasExperience`); the caption renders '—' and the bar renders a hatched unavailable track instead of a fabricated progress fill. `noFabricatedData.test.js` pins it. |

| N301 **Several collections were world-readable (`allow read: if true`), leaking private rows to unauthenticated clients.** `comments` (and its `likes`/`dislikes`/`reactions`/`history` children), `highlights`, `events` (+ subcollections), the `usernames` registry, `reaction_shards`, story reaction children, and the advanced-creator suite (`spaces`, `sounds`, `marketplace_items`, `polls`, `badges`, `user_badges`, `copyright_registry`, `previous_usernames`) all granted `read: if true`, so anyone holding only the public project config could dump them (edit history, private events, availability/reaction data) without signing in. | ✅ **now closed** | Each read is scoped to the surface that actually consumes it — signed-in for collections every reader reaches behind auth (`comments`, `usernames`, `events`, the creator suite), owner-only for `highlights` and the comment `history` subcollection, and reactor/admin for the comment `reactions` marker (no cross-user reader). `readScopePrivacyRules.test.js` (new, in the `guards` CI job) fails if any `allow read: if true;` reappears and pins each collection's scope, with emulator-backed adversarial checks where Java is available. |
| N302 **`rankingService.getUserBadges` fabricated a `0` follower/following count.** `Number(userData.followerCount \|\| userData.stats?.followers \|\| 0)` reported a measured "0 followers" (and marked `first_follower`/`influencer` genuinely unearned) whenever the sharded counter had simply not been read — contradicting the file's own `unknownNumber` contract a few lines above. | ✅ **now closed** | Both counts read through `unknownNumber(userData.followerCount ?? userData.stats?.followers)`; an unknown count stays `null` (progress → '—'). `noFabricatedData.test.js` pins it. |
| N303 **The passport, spaces banner and event-feedback paths fabricated `0` (or threw) for unread counters.** `PassportScreen` rendered `visaStamps?.filter(...).length \|\| 0` (fabricated 0 when the list was absent) and `{…length} of {…length}` (literal `undefined`) in the summary chip; `SpacesScreen` called `activeSpace.tipsTotalCoins.toLocaleString()` with no guard (throw when unread); `eventService.submitFeedback` returned `feedbackScore: result?.feedbackScore ?? 0`. | ✅ **now closed** | All three read through `unknownNumber` (unknown → '—', never a measured 0); the passport summary chip renders '—' for an absent list. `noFabricatedData.test.js` pins all three. |
| N304 **The `events` document's `privacy` field implied a private-event capability that did not exist** (extends N266). `createEvent` wrote `privacy: data.privacy || 'public'` and `updateEvent` accepted it, but no screen ever rendered a selector for it and no reader — screen, service or rule — ever consulted it, so it was a dead control that implied privacy the product does not offer. | ✅ **now closed (as a dead control)** — the `privacy` field is removed from `eventService.createEvent`, `eventService.updateEvent`'s `allowedFields` and `CreateEventScreen` (create/edit/payload), and the ruleset's `events` note is corrected. The residual read-scope limit (a signed-in stranger can point-read an event while it is `draft`) remains the *N266* status-gating item, which still needs registrations served server-side. `readScopePrivacyRules.test.js` pins that `eventService` no longer writes/reads `privacy`. |
| N305 **The auth screens advertised "End-to-end encrypted" and "Military-grade security"** in the login / forgot-password / verify-email footers.
