Neither claim is backable: the messaging path never encrypts (no key provisioning, per the N214-class E2EE finding), and the only transport guarantee is TLS. The same class the messaging E2EE guard already blocks. | ✅ **now closed** | The footers now state what the stack delivers — "Encrypted in transit (TLS)" and "Protected by Firebase Auth" (login/forgot-password) / "Verified by Firebase Auth" (verify-email) — and the stray 🔒/🛡️ emoji were dropped. `noFabricatedData.test.js` gains a pin for the three screens. |
| N306 **`videoReadPrivacyRules.test.js` asserted an anonymous client must be DENIED a **public** video, and the suite was never wired into any CI emulator step.** The shipped `videoReadableByViewer()` allows an anonymous read of a public video (the public branch carries no `isSignedIn()` gate), matching the post/community posture; the sibling `communityPrivacyRules.test.js` asserts anon CAN read a public community. Because the suite ran only when `FIRESTORE_EMULATOR_HOST` was set and no workflow step set it, the wrong assertion never executed — it surfaced the first time the suite ran against a real emulator. | ✅ **now closed** — the assertion is corrected (anon reads a public video; anon is still denied a private one, which is added explicitly) and the suite is added to the `guards` emulator step (with `notificationCounterRuleShape.test.js`). 10 emulator suites / 117 tests pass live. |
| N307 **The Settings "Show active status" toggle persisted `settings.privacy.showActiveStatus`, which no reader consumed** — presence visibility is owned by `profile.privacy.presence` (`resolveCapabilities` / `projectProfileForViewer`), and the same Privacy section already had a "Live Online Presence" scope selector. The toggle's `onChange` only called `handleUpdateProfilePrivacyScope('presence', …)`, so the persisted key was theatre and the toggle duplicated the selector below it. | ✅ **now closed (as a dead control)** — the toggle and the `showActiveStatus` key are removed (presence is controlled solely by the `presence` scope). The same sweep removed other unread persisted fields: `user_settings.privacy.{showOnline,allowMessages,allowComments}`, `user_settings.notifications.*` and `user_settings.appearance.theme` (the server reads `notificationPreferences`, never `notifications`), plus the unread output fields `showOnline`/`allowDiscovery`/`groupInvitePermission` of `messagesService.getUserPrivacySettings` and the orphan `GROUP_INVITE_PERMISSIONS`/`DEFAULT_GROUP_INVITE_SETTING` constants and the `settings.activeStatus` i18n key. `settingsWiring.test.js` pins `showActiveStatus`/`spatialAudio`/`streamQuality` as absent. |
| N308 **`EventCard` rendered five badges/pills from fields no writer ever produces and three prop-gated blocks from props no render site ever passes** — a permanently-dead branch set. The event schema (`eventService.createEvent` + `CreatePost`'s event payload) writes only `title/date/location/description/coverImage/rsvpEnabled/capacity/timezone`, yet the card read `event.coinReward`, `event.friendsGoing`, `event.scarcity.seatsLeft`, `event.stats.interested` and `event.gravity`, and gated blocks on `matchScore`, `attendanceVelocity`, `friendProximity`, `userAffinity` and `distanceKm` — none of which is passed by the sole render site (`PostCard.jsx:1171`). The `gravityLevel`/`getGravityLevel` "exploding/trending/growing" tier was therefore always `'low'`, the "Matches you" badge never showed, and `stats.interested` was the exact class of phantom field the sibling `stats.going` guard (N-EventCard) already removed — the sweep was incomplete. | ✅ **now closed (as a dead control)** — all five phantom-field reads and five dead props are removed, along with the `getGravityLevel` helper and its `gravityLevel` consumer; the glow now reflects only the two states the card actually knows (`urgent`/`isLive`). `noFabricatedData.test.js` gains a pin listing every removed identifier. |
| N309 **`LinkCard` fabricated a safety verdict, an AI summary, a view count and two fake-success controls, all from props no render site passes.** `PostCard.jsx:1172` renders `<LinkCard link tokens onAnalytics postId>`; it never passes `onFetchMetadata`/`onClassifyLink`/`onGetSafetyScore`/`onSave`/`onShare`/`onTip`/`currentUser`. So `onGetSafetyScore` defaulted to `{ score: 100 }` and the card rendered a green "Trusted domain" shield — an affirmative safety claim nothing verified; `onFetchMetadata`/`onClassifyLink` resolved to `null`/`'link'` so the AI-summary and trending branches were permanently dead; the Save button toasted a fake "Saved to bookmarks" and the tip menu a fake "Sent N coins"; and the local `engagement` counters rendered a measured view count while incrementing only client state. The card also shipped an IndexedDB metadata cache and a `console.error` with nothing to cache. | ✅ **now closed (as a dead control)** — the card renders only the publisher-supplied Open Graph fields off the link document (title falls back to the bare hostname, never an invented one), keeping the real external link, the native share sheet and the YouTube/Vimeo embed (derived from the URL). The unreachable cache, the fabricated controls and the dead props are removed. `noFabricatedData.test.js` pins every removed identifier. |
| N310 **`eventService.getEventStats` fabricated a `0` rating and a `100%` attendance.** `feedbackScore: event.stats?.feedbackScore \|\| 0` reported a measured "0.0" rating for an event that had never been rated, and `attendeePercentage: … : 100` claimed a capacity-less event was 100% full. | ✅ **now closed** — the rating reads through `unknownNumber` (unknown stays `null`) and a capacity-less event reports no percentage. `noFabricatedData.test.js` pins both. |
| N311 **`ImageCard`'s Save and Report buttons toasted a success nothing performed.** `PostCard.jsx:1166` rendered `<ImageCard images onLike postId onAnalytics>` with no `onSave`/`onReport`, so both handlers fell into fallbacks that toasted "Saved to bookmarks" / "Report submitted" while no save was written and no report was sent — on every image in the feed. | ✅ **now closed** — `onSave` is wired to `PostCard.handleSave` (the same optimistic `firestoreService.savePost`/`unsavePost` + offline-queue path the post bookmark uses) and `onReport` to a new `handleReportImage` that goes through the canonical `reportContent` callable (rate-limited, de-duplicated). The unwired fallbacks now fail honestly ("Saving/Reporting is unavailable here"). `noFabricatedData.test.js` + `integrationWiring.test.js` pin both. |
| N312 **`VideoCard` advertised four swipe gestures `PostCard` never wires.** `PostCard.jsx:1171` renders `<VideoCard src isVisible onDoubleTap postId currentUser onAnalytics>` — no `onShare`/`onOpenDetails`/`onNext`/`onPrev` — yet a horizontal or vertical swipe unconditionally flashed a "Share"/"Details"/"Next Video"/"Previous Video" hint and fired an optional-call no-op. The hint promised a capability the card did not have. | ✅ **now closed (as a dead control)** — each swipe branch is gated on its handler actually being present (and the hint only shows then), so an unwired card does nothing rather than claiming an action. `noFabricatedData.test.js` pins the gating. |
| N313 **`VideoCard`'s quality picker was a dead control** — `PostCard` renders `<VideoCard src isVisible onDoubleTap postId currentUser onAnalytics>` and never passes `qualities`, yet the controls bar always showed a Settings button that opened an empty quality menu. The card also destructured an `index` prop no render site passes. | ✅ **now closed (as a dead control)** — the quality button + menu are gated on `qualities.length > 0` (they only appear when a real ladder exists) and the unused `index` prop is removed. `noFabricatedData.test.js` pins both. |
| N314 **`liveService` reported a peak-concurrent-viewer metric no writer maintains.** `startLiveStream` seeded `stats.peakViewers: 0`, and nothing ever updated it (the sibling `totalViewers` is incremented by `functions/live.js#onLiveViewerCreate`; tips/gifts/coins by `functions/monetization.js`), yet `endLiveStream` (return + `auditLogger`), `getLiveEarnings` and `getLiveAnalytics` all surfaced `peakViewers` — an unwritten value presented as a measured peak of `0` for a stream that may have had thousands of viewers. | ✅ **now closed** — the `peakViewers` seed and all three read/audit sites are removed; the genuinely maintained counters (`viewerCount`/`totalViewers`/tips/gifts/coins) stay. `noFabricatedData.test.js` pins the absence and the retained counters. |
| N315 **`achievements/{uid}/items` and `titles/{uid}/items` were readable by any signed-in client regardless of the profile's `achievements`/`titles` privacy scope.** Both were `allow read: if isSignedIn()`, so a user who set badges/titles to `ONLY_ME`/`FOLLOWERS` still had their earned badges, titles and grant times exposed to any authenticated client — a client-masking-only control (the scope was enforced by `resolveCapabilities`/`projectProfileForViewer` on the client, not by the rules). | ✅ **now closed** — a new `profileSectionReadable(userId, section, defaultScope)` helper mirrors the client decision (owner always; otherwise the section scope must permit the viewer's relation AND a private account must grant follower/connection), and both collection + `items` reads use it. `readScopePrivacyRules.test.js` pins the static shape and adds an emulator test (owner reads, stranger denied on `ONLY_ME`, `EVERYONE` still readable) — 13/13 pass live. |
| N316 **`storyService.answerQuiz` credited the quiz coin reward inside its Firestore transaction.** The `monetization.addCoins` network callable ran in-band in the `runTransaction` callback, which the SDK may retry — so a single answer could mint the reward more than once, and a cap/network failure aborted a legitimate answer. | ✅ **now closed** — the reward is computed in the transaction and the credit runs **after** commit with a deterministic idempotency key (`quiz_${uid}_${storyId}_${quizId}`), so the credit is exactly-once even on caller retry. `transactionOrderGuard.test.js` gains a static guard failing any `runTransaction` callback that awaits `addCoins`/`spendCoins`/`transferCoins`/`callFunction`/`httpsCallable`. |
| N317 **`HomeScreen`'s rewarded-ad toast announced a fabricated `+0 Coins`.** `toast.success(\`🎉 Claimed +${result?.coinsAdded ?? 0} Coins…\`)` coerced a missing `coinsAdded` (a non-credit result) to a measured "0". | ✅ **now closed** — the toast only announces the amount when the server actually returned a number; otherwise it says "Reward credited!". |
| N318 **`AudioCard`'s smart-preload read a prop no caller supplies.** `isNearViewport` was destructured and drove both the preload effect and the `preload` attribute, but `PostCard` never passes it (it passes `isVisible`) — so metadata was never preloaded and `preload` was permanently `'none'`. | ✅ **now closed (as a dead control)** — the dead prop is removed and preload keys off the real `isVisible` signal. |
| N319 **A rogue second brand gradient was re-declared as `ARVDOUL_GRADIENT` across the post cards.** `#9333ea → #c026d3 → #06b6d4`/`#ec4899` (Tailwind purple-600/fuchsia-600/cyan-500/pink-500) was the "neon purple" stand-in for the brand gradient in `PostCard.getDesignTokens().actionBarBg`/`neonPurple`, `PostCard/VideoCard.ARVDOUL_GRADIENT`, `AudioCard`, `TextCard`, `QuestionCard`, `PollCard`, `EventCard` (`fallbackGradient`), `components/Videos/VideoCard` (scrubber) and `SplashScreen`. It was gated behind `audioNeonPrimary`/`audioNeonSecondary`/`audioNeonAccent` token reads that are **never defined**, so the fallback always fired — meaning a change to the canonical `#8B1EF3 → #4431F7 → #055BFB` brand would silently miss every post card. | ✅ **now closed** — every site now consumes `BRAND_GRADIENT` (or `tokens.color.brand.*` for the glow/alpha variants); the undefined `audioNeon*` reads are gone. `designTokenDriftGuard.test.js` gains a raw-hex guard for `#9333ea`/`#c026d3`, and `semanticColorRatchetGuard.test.js` (new, shrinking baseline) pins the semantic-palette (`#10B981`/`#059669`/`#F59E0B`/`#D97706`/`#EF4444`/`#DC2626`) consolidation; `LoadingSpinner`/`SplashScreen` migrated to `tokens.color.semantic.*`. |
| N320 **The creator dashboard's "Engagement" and "Coins Earned" cards rendered a fabricated measured `0`.** `functions/analytics.js#trackProfileView` seeds `profile_analytics.totalEngagement: 0` / `coinsEarned: 0` at creation and **no writer ever increments them**, yet `analyticsService.getUserAnalytics` read them with `typeof data.totalEngagement === 'number' ? data.totalEngagement : null` (the stored `0` passes the check), and the doc-exists branch *replaced* the real live-computed values the else branch derived — so a creator with real post engagement and a wallet balance saw "Engagement 0 / Coins Earned 0" as measured data (and the misleading comment claimed the value was "never incremented" while still reading it). The same seed also wrote per-day `dailyStats[today].engagement: 0` / `coins: 0`, which **only `views`/`reach` are ever incremented**, so the dashboard's per-day engagement/coins sparklines drew a fabricated flat `0` line. | ✅ **now closed** — both totals are derived live in every branch: engagement from the creator's recent public posts (`_computeRecentEngagement`, `unknownNumber`-summed, null when any counter is unknown) and earnings from the wallet balance on `users/{uid}` (`_readLiveCoinBalance`). The server seed no longer writes the two never-incremented totals, and the daily series now carries only `views`/`reach` (the counters `trackProfileView` actually maintains) so a day with no engagement reading is absent rather than a measured flat zero. `noFabricatedData.test.js` pins the live derivation, the absent stored reads and the trimmed daily seed. |
| N321 **Live presence (`isOnline`/`lastActive`) on the world-signed-in-readable `users/{uid}` document leaked a user's online status and activity timestamp to every authenticated client, regardless of the profile's `presence` privacy scope.** The `users/{uid}` read rule grants any signed-in client (the doc must stay broadly readable for feed/profile hydration), so a raw `getDoc` returned `isOnline`/`lastActive` even when the profile set `presence` to `ONLY_ME` — the scope was enforced only by the client projection (`resolveCapabilities`/`projectProfileForViewer`), never by the rules (the same client-masking-only class as N315). `updateLastActive` and `AuthContext` also stamped `lastActive` onto the session object. | ✅ **now closed** — presence moved to its own scope-gated `user_presence/{uid}` document whose rule mirrors `profileSectionReadable(userId, 'presence', 'FOLLOWERS')`; `userService` writes/reads presence there (create, `updateLastActive`, `getUserProfile`), `socialGraphService.hydrateProfiles` hydrates it opt-in with per-doc reads (a batch `documentId() in` read cannot prove the per-doc `get()` condition), `AuthContext` no longer fabricates a `lastActive`, and the dead `appStore.updateUserLastActive` was removed. `users/{uid}` create/update now refuse `isOnline`/`lastActive` (server-authoritative denylist + create forbidden-keys). `userPresencePrivacyRules.test.js` pins the rules statically and adds emulator checks (ONLY_ME denied to a stranger, FOLLOWERS allowed for a follower, EVERYONE allowed, client cannot seed the fields); `scripts/migrate-user-presence.mjs` is an idempotent, dry-run-by-default migration for legacy documents (emulator-verified). |
| N322 **`NEW_USER_DEFAULTS.totalEarned: 0` seeded a lifetime-earnings figure no writer maintains, so the wallet and creator surfaces rendered a fabricated measured "Lifetime Earned: 0".** No server path increments a lifetime-earnings counter (`functions/monetization.js` increments per-stream `live_streams.stats.coinsEarned`, and `gift_received` XP is separate; nothing writes `users/{uid}.totalEarned`), yet `userService.createUserProfile` seeded it from `NEW_USER_DEFAULTS` and `firestore.rules` pinned the create to `== 0`. `walletService`/`WalletScreen`/`QuickAccessPanel` then read the stored `0` as a real "you have earned nothing" figure — the N320 class. | ✅ **now closed** — `totalEarned` is removed from `NEW_USER_DEFAULTS` (canonical `src/shared/levelConfig.cjs`, synced to `functions/levelConfig.cjs`), `createUserProfile` no longer seeds it, and the `users/{uid}` create rule no longer pins it (it stays in the server-authoritative denylist, so a client still cannot set it). Absent now renders as unknown ("—"), which the existing `walletService` (`?? null`) and `WalletScreen` (`== null ? '—'`) already handle. `noFabricatedData.test.js` pins the absence in the config, the create rule, `createUserProfile` and the two readers. |
| N323 **A three-stop "brand mimic" gradient (`from-purple-500 via-pink-500 to-cyan-400` and siblings) stood in for the brand gradient across six modules.** `RouteProgressBar`, `VideoTopBar`, `GroupInfoScreen`, `MessagingScreen`, `ProfileQRCodeModal` and `QuickAccessPanel` rendered a purple→pink→cyan ramp as their primary accent — a second brand gradient that a change to the canonical `#8B1EF3 → #4431F7 → #055BFB` would silently miss (the N319 class, but as a Tailwind-class family the hex ratchet could not see). | ✅ **now closed** — every occurrence is migrated onto `BRAND_GRADIENT`; `designTokenDriftGuard.test.js` gains `ROGUE_BRAND_MIMIC` (a hard-zero guard for the 3-stop family) so it cannot reappear. |
| N324 **A user's coin balance (`users/{uid}.coins`) and derived lifetime earnings are readable by every signed-in client, because they live on the world-signed-in-readable `users/{uid}` document while `economicStatus` defaults to `ONLY_ME`.** `firestore.rules` grants `users/{uid}` `allow read: if isSignedIn()` (needed for feed/profile hydration) and field-level masking is impossible on a readable document — so a raw `getDoc(users/{uid})` (e.g. `walletService`, `analyticsService`, `rankingService`, `socialGraphService`) returns `coins`/`earnings`/`coinBalance` regardless of the owner's `economicStatus` scope. The scope is enforced only by the client projection (`projectProfileForViewer` nulls `ECONOMIC_FIELDS`), i.e. it is a client-masking-only control — the N315/N321 class, and the highest-value one because it is a financial figure. | ✅ **FIXED + VERIFIED.** The authoritative balance now lives on `users_private/{uid}` (owner/admin-readable); `users/{uid}` carries no financial figure. `functions/coinBalance.js` is the single accessor (`readCoinBalance`/`writeCoinBalance`/`availableFrom`), every money-path module (`monetization.js`, `withdrawalSettlement.js`, `video.js`, `xpAward.js`) reads/writes through it, the admin/coin-supply aggregates sum `users_private`, `privacyMigration.js` backfills `coins`/`lockedCoins`, and the client (`monetizationService.getBalance`, `walletService`, `analyticsService._readLiveCoinBalance`) reads the private doc. The cross-user `getCoinLeaderboard` (which required a public balance and had no screen caller) was removed. `coinBalanceBoundary.test.js` (new, guards job) + `profilePiiBoundary.test.js` + `sharedConfigSync.test.js` pin the boundary. Legacy pre-migration accounts keep a `fallbackToPublic` read until the sweep runs. **(Original deferral rationale, retained for context)** The correct fix is to move the authoritative balance to `users_private/{uid}` (already owner/admin-readable) and leave only a masked projection on the public doc, but every balance mutation in `functions/monetization.js` (`addCoins`/`spendCoins`/`transferCoins`/`sendGift`/`boostPost`/`purchaseCoins`/`purchaseMarketplaceItem`/`sendLiveGift`/`requestWithdrawal`), `functions/video.js` (`sendGiftToCreator`) and `functions/withdrawalSettlement.js` (~40 `t.update(userRef, { coins })` sites) reads/writes the field inside a transaction; a single missed site would silently create the field back on the public doc or read a stale balance. The Jest suite **explicitly excludes `/functions/`** (`jest.config.cjs` `testPathIgnorePatterns`), so there is no automated way to prove the migration does not corrupt balances. Executing it blind is exactly the §3.4 "superficial workaround that can make financial data incorrect" the mandate forbids. **Preparatory work:** the boundary already exists (`users_private` + `splitProfileFields` + `PRIVATE_PROFILE_FIELDS`), `walletService` already reads `coins ?? coinBalance ?? null` (so a null balance renders "—", not `0`), and `firestore.rules` already keeps `coins` in the server-authoritative denylist. **Required before closing:** (1) a `firebase-functions-test`/emulator harness for the monetization callables; (2) move the field + repoint every transaction read/write; (3) an idempotent migration (copy `coins` → `users_private`, drop from `users`); (4) a rules test proving a stranger can no longer read the balance. |

| N325 **`userService.getCoinBalance(userId)` was a dead method that returned `profile?.coins \|\| 0`, presenting an absent balance as a measured `0`.** It had **no caller anywhere** (only its own export/re-export and a `noFabricatedData` denylist string), so it was both dead code and an N320-class fabrication trap for the next reader (the live balance read is `walletService.getWalletOverview`, which returns `null` when unknown). | ✅ **now closed** — method + both exports removed; `noFabricatedData.test.js` gains a guard asserting `userService` no longer defines it. |
| N326 **A PayPal-selected withdrawal settled as a Stripe payout from the PLATFORM account.** `functions/withdrawalSettlement.js#settleWithdrawal` unconditionally called `stripe.payouts.create(...)` and took the destination from the CLIENT-supplied `withdrawalData.paymentDetails.stripeAccountId`. The payout surfaces (`CoinsScreen`, `CreatorPayoutScreen`) offered a "PayPal" method with no PayPal payout integration, so a `paypal` withdrawal settled as a Stripe payout with `stripeAccount: undefined` — paying the creator's money out of the platform account — and the stored PayPal email was never used. `requestWithdrawal` accepted any method string, so an unpayable request could be queued (locking the creator's coins behind a payout that could never be sent). The same defect class let a caller name any connected account as the destination. | ✅ **now closed** — settlement resolves the provider server-side (`resolvePayoutProvider`): `paypal` and any non-Stripe method are terminal (rejected + `rejectAndRelease` returns the locked coins), a Stripe method requires `payout_settings.status === 'ready'` and the account id comes from `payout_settings`, never the request; `requestWithdrawal` validates the method before locking; the Stripe webhook now handles `account.updated` to flip `payout_settings.status` to `ready`. The unbacked PayPal control was removed from `CoinsScreen` (now routes to the real Stripe payout screen), `CreatorPayoutScreen` (Stripe-only, gated on a real connected account) and `VideoAnalyticsScreen` (reads `status === 'ready'`). `withdrawalSettlementProvider.test.js` loads the real module against the Firestore emulator and pins PayPal rejection + coin release, no payout without a ready account, and that the payout targets the SERVER account id, never the client-supplied one. |
| N327 **`walletService.requestPayout` passed the details object where the callable expects the method string.** `monetizationService.requestWithdrawal(userId, amount, paymentMethod, paymentDetails)` received `details` (e.g. `{ payoutMethod: 'stripe_connected' }`) as `paymentMethod` and `undefined` as `paymentDetails`, so the server threw "paymentMethod and paymentDetails required" for EVERY `WalletScreen` withdrawal — the wallet payout flow was broken end-to-end. | ✅ **now closed** — `requestPayout(userId, amount, paymentMethod = 'stripe_connected', paymentDetails = {})` and `WalletScreen` passes `'stripe_connected'`. |
| N328 **`getPayoutSettings()` returned the callable envelope, not the payout record, and the screens read a field the server never writes.** The callable returns `{ success, settings }`; the service returned `result.data` verbatim, and `CreatorPayoutScreen`/`VideoAnalyticsScreen` read `settings.accountStatus` (expecting `'verified'|'active'|'enabled'`), while the server writes `status: 'onboarding'|'ready'`. So a fully-onboarded creator was shown "Not connected" forever and the connect button stayed active. | ✅ **now closed** — the service unwraps `data.settings ?? data`, the fallback returns an honest `status: 'unconfigured'`, and both screens treat `status === 'ready'` as connected. `noFabricatedData.test.js` pins the unwrap. |
| N329 **`/image-editor` and `/image-studio` were dead routes.** Both rendered the same `ThumbnailDesignerScreen` as the reachable `/thumbnail-designer`, and nothing linked to either (only `TopAppBar`/`MainLayout` special-cased their paths). | ✅ **now closed** — both aliases and their path special-cases removed; the real `/thumbnail-designer` route (reachable from the Menu and `CreateVideo`) is untouched. |

| N330 **`purchaseCoins`, `createSubscription` and `cancelSubscription` ran Stripe network calls INSIDE the retryable `createFirestoreTransaction` callback.** `createFirestoreTransaction` retries the callback up to 5x on any thrown error, and `admin.firestore().runTransaction` re-runs the callback on a write-conflict retry — so `stripe.paymentIntents.create` (purchaseCoins), `stripe.prices.create` + `stripe.subscriptions.create` (createSubscription) and `stripe.subscriptions.update` (cancelSubscription) each fired once per attempt. `purchaseCoins` re-charged the card on every retry (masked only by the Stripe `pi_${key}` idempotency key), and `createSubscription` had **no** idempotency key, so a transient Firestore contention could start several paid subscriptions and charge the card repeatedly. `createSubscription` also read `users/{uid}` (`creditSubscriptionCoins`) **after** `t.set(subscriptions/{uid})` — a read-after-write that throws every time, so subscribing would have failed outright. The `transactionOrderGuard` only banned `addCoins`/`spendCoins`/`transferCoins`/callables, so the whole Stripe class was invisible to it. | ✅ **now closed** — all Stripe calls moved BEFORE the transaction (the transaction now only records the settled charge/grant, keyed by the ledger row); `creditSubscriptionCoins` runs before the subscription-doc `t.set` so reads precede writes; `cancelSubscription` reads + cancels in Stripe first and re-reads the sub inside the transaction; `createSubscription`/`cancelSubscription` now reject a foreign idempotency key. `transactionOrderGuard.test.js` gains a hard-zero "no `stripe.*` call inside a `runTransaction` callback" assertion so the class cannot regress. |

| N331 **`payPerView` nested a transaction inside a transaction (and enqueued a push in the callback), so every pay-per-view purchase failed.** The outer `db.runTransaction` callback awaited `transferCoinsInternal(...)`, which opens its **own** `db.runTransaction`. Firestore forbids opening a transaction inside another, so the call threw every time and no purchase ever completed; the same callback also `await enqueuePush(...)`, which would fire once per SDK retry. | ✅ **now closed** — the video/price read, the entitlement check and the coin transfer now run as separate steps: `transferCoinsInternal` (its own idempotent transaction on `ppv_<uid>_<videoId>`) moves the coins first, then the entitlement is written and the push enqueued after commit; the free (`price <= 0`) path claims the entitlement in its own small transaction. `transactionOrderGuard.test.js` now also fails on `transferCoinsInternal`/nested `runTransaction` and on `enqueuePush`/`fetch`/`getUserEmail`/`algolia` inside any callback. |

| N332 **A client could write its own `users/{uid}.lockedCoins`, defeating the withdrawal hold.** `getAvailableBalance = coins - lockedCoins` (functions/monetization.js), and the hold is what stops a second withdrawal against coins already queued for payout. `lockedCoins` was absent from both `SERVER_AUTHORITATIVE_FIELDS` (`src/config/profileContracts.js`) and the `users/{uid}` rule's `touchesServerAuthoritativeFields()` denylist (and from the create denylist), so an owner could `updateDoc(users/{uid}, { lockedCoins: 0 })` — raising their own spendable balance and filing a second withdrawal against the same coins (two payouts from one balance). The only writers are server-side (`requestWithdrawal` locks, `withdrawalSettlement` releases). | ✅ **now closed** — `lockedCoins` added to `SERVER_AUTHORITATIVE_FIELDS`, to `touchesServerAuthoritativeFields()` and to the `users/{uid}` create denylist; new `serverAuthoritativeFieldsRules.test.js` (static + emulator adversarial: an owner `updateDoc({ lockedCoins: 0 })`/`({ coins: 1e6 })` is denied while a plain profile edit succeeds) added to the `guards` CI job. |

| N333 **A second brand gradient shipped in 24 sites — including the design-system `Button` primary — because the drift guard only banned the pink/rose-middle mimic.** `ROGUE_BRAND_MIMIC` matched `from-purple-600 via-pink-600 to-cyan-500` but not the structurally identical `from-purple-600 via-indigo-600 to-cyan-500` (and `from-violet-600 via-indigo-600 to-cyan-500` in `design-system/Button.jsx`). A change to the canonical `#8B1EF3 → #4431F7 → #055BFB` would silently miss all of them. | ✅ **now closed** — every brand-shaped CTA/avatar/bubble/hero/progress surface migrated onto the canonical `bg-arvdoul-gradient` utility; `ROGUE_BRAND_MIMIC_NEUTRAL` added to `designTokenDriftGuard.test.js`; semantic gradients (tiers/auras/menu categories/audio meters/story rings) left as their own palettes. |
| N334 **Keyboard focus rings rendered a different colour than the brand.** 97 focus rings across 40 files used the raw Tailwind family (`focus:ring-purple-500` / `ring-indigo-500` / `ring-blue-500` / `ring-violet-*`); Tailwind `purple-500` is `#a855f7`, not the brand violet `#8B1EF3`, so the app's focus affordance was off-brand. | ✅ **now closed** — all standardised onto `focus:ring-arvdoul-purple-500`; drift-guard assertion added (semantic emerald/amber/red rings exempt). Also added `designSystemPrimitives.test.jsx` (axe + behaviour) for the app-wide `Button`/`EmptyState`/`ErrorState`/`PermissionDenied` primitives, previously untested. |

| N335 **The screen canvas was copy-pasted with raw brand literals.** Four screens hand-rolled the identical `radial-gradient(circle at 50% 0%, rgba(139,30,243,0.1) 0%, transparent 50%), #03071B` string — the brand violet as a decimal literal and the base colour as raw hex, neither of which moves when a token changes. | ✅ **now closed** — added `brandRgba()` / `brandCanvasBackground()` to `src/design-system/tokens.js` and migrated all four; `designTokens.test.js` pins the helpers. |
| N336 **`ring-arvdoul-purple-500` (from N334) was a dead class.** The config declares a flat `arvdoul-purple` with no `-500` shade, so Tailwind emitted no CSS and 97 focus rings were invisible — the N111 failure mode, which the `tailwindUtilities` guard missed because its SHADE test skips custom colours. | ✅ **now closed** — corrected to `ring-arvdoul-purple`; added a config-aware assertion that every `*-arvdoul-*` utility resolves to a declared colour key (proved it fails on the invalid class). |

| N337 **The Tailwind `bg-arvdoul-gradient` utility was unpinned from the token.** The config re-declares the brand gradient as a literal (Tailwind cannot import the ESM token), and nothing asserted the two agreed, so changing the token would leave every migrated CTA rendering the old gradient. | ✅ **now closed** — `designTokenDriftGuard` now asserts the config literal is byte-identical to `tokens.color.brand.gradient`. |
| N338 **`themePalettes.js` declared the brand violet in decimal form.** The drift guard requires the palettes to derive from the tokens, yet their glow/card shadows used `rgba(139,30,243,..)` — a literal that never moves with the token. | ✅ **now closed** — derive from `brandRgba()`. Also added `brandVioletRgbaRatchetGuard` (baseline `scripts/brandVioletRgbaBaseline.json`) so the remaining decimal occurrences can only shrink. |
| N339 **TopAppBar / FollowButton hand-wrote brand-violet glows in decimal.** JS string contexts (not Tailwind arbitrary values), so safely derivable. | ✅ **now closed** — migrated to `brandRgba()`; ratchet baseline 73 → 67. |
| N340 **Two deterministic-avatar generators carried independent palettes.** `avatarUtils.generateDefaultAvatarSvg` and `userService.generateDefaultAvatar` each had their own list with **different secondary shades**, so the same user rendered a different fallback avatar on the feed vs the profile. | ✅ **now closed** — `avatarUtils` owns the canonical `AVATAR_PALETTES`; `userService` imports it; `avatarPaletteConsistency.test.js` fails on a second list or the old shades; semantic-hex baseline shrinks. |
| N341 **`BottomNav` hand-wrote brand-violet radial/linear gradients in decimal** (JS string contexts inside `style={{}}`). | ✅ **now closed** — migrated to `brandRgba()`; ratchet baseline lowered. |
| N342 **Eleven more JS-string brand-violet literals survived** the N339/N341 sweep: the six auth/onboarding screens' `--glow-a`/`--rim-1` style vars, `ImageEditor`, `editorConstants.ARVDOUL_SHADOW`, `OfflineIndicator`, `SearchScreen`, and the `EqualizerModule` canvas gradient (which also hard-coded the brand **indigo**/`blue` decimals). | ✅ **now closed** — all derive from `brandRgba()` (and a new generic `brandHexRgba(token, alpha)` for the canvas gradient, so the indigo/blue stops track their tokens too); `designTokens.test.js` pins `brandHexRgba`; ratchet baseline 73 → 38 (the residue is Tailwind arbitrary-value classes, which must stay literal). |
| N343 **`ProfileProgression` fabricated a "0 XP" bar from a missing XP value.** `experience` defaulted to `0`, so `getLevelInfo(experience || 0)` rendered "0 XP earned / 100 XP to Level N" for a profile that stores a `level` but no `experience` — a fabricated measured zero, despite the component doc claiming it "never fabricates progression". | ✅ **now closed** — `experience` defaults to `null`; the XP curve is consulted only when `hasExperience`; the XP bar renders only when `xpInfo` resolves; `noFabricatedData.test.js` pins all three. |
| N344 **A caller could replay another user's idempotency result.** `requestWithdrawal` and `watchAd` read `idempotency_ledger/{key}` and returned `ledgerSnap.data().result` without checking the row's owner (three sites), unlike `addCoins` which does. A leaked/guessed key returned a foreign settlement result. | ✅ **now closed** — every replay path asserts `ledgerSnap.data().userId === caller` (permission-denied otherwise); `moneyLedgerOwnershipGuard.test.js`. |

| N369 **`message_dedupe` was `allow read, write: if isSignedIn()`, so any signed-in client could suppress another user's message and enumerate every recent send.** The dedupe key is `sha256(senderId:conversationId:type:content:mediaHash)` and its pre-image is entirely public, so an attacker could compute a victim's next key and pre-create the record: the victim's `_checkDuplicate` then saw a hit and threw `messaging/duplicate` — a targeted denial of a specific message. The open read also let a client `list` the collection and enumerate recent send keys, and the rule comment actively overclaimed that the key "cannot be forged for another user's message" (only *non-guessability* was assumed; the value is public, so a legitimate client creates the very record an attacker can pre-create). | ✅ **now closed** — the rule binds each record to its sender: `allow create, update: if isSignedIn() && request.resource.data.senderId == uid()` and `allow read: if isSignedIn() && (resource == null || resource.data.senderId == uid())` (a missing doc stays readable so the ordinary "no duplicate yet" check is unaffected; `resource == null` avoids the `resource.data` throw). `messagesService.sendMessage` stamps `senderId: currentUser.uid` on the dedupe write and `_checkDuplicate` treats a `permission-denied` read as "no duplicate of mine" so a foreign record can never block a send. `messageDedupeGuard.test.js` (new, guards job) statically pins the rule shape + client stamp and, under the emulator, proves alice's own record round-trips while mallory cannot mint a record naming alice nor read hers and an anonymous client is denied entirely. |
| N370 **Story shard counters accepted an unbounded client-written value — a forged view/reaction count.** `stories/{storyId}/view_shards|reaction_shards|analytics_shards` bounded the KEY SET (`hasOnly([...])`) but not the VALUE, so any signed-in client could `setDoc` a shard with `count: 999999`; the sum-on-read (`_getShardedViewCount`/`_getShardedReactionCounts`) then reported a forged count. Same class as N207. | ✅ **now closed** — the shard rules now pin a bounded relative change using the same technique as N207 (`fieldDeltaBounded`/`fieldCreateBounded`: `request.resource.data.<f>` is the resulting value and `resource.data.<f>` the pre-write value; a missing pre-write field reads as the map's `get(field, null)`): create bounds the whole initial value, update bounds the delta, both to `±100`. A one-shot `count: 999999` mint (create or update) is denied while a legitimate `increment(±1)` succeeds. `storyShardForgeryGuard.test.js` (new, guards job) statically pins the bounded shape and, under the emulator, proves the mint fails for view/reaction/analytics shards while a legitimate increment succeeds and an anonymous writer is denied. The same value-bound was applied to `daily_upload_counters` (id-bound since N210, but the `count` value was unbounded — a fresh shard could be pre-set to `999999`, defeating the per-tier upload limit); its create now requires `count` in `[1,100]` and its update a bounded `+0..+100` step, asserted in `firestoreRulesCompile.test.js`. |


| N371 **`watchAd` had no daily cap, paid zero for the 15s claim its own UI offers, and recorded the unclamped duration.** `functions/monetization.js#watchAd` is a coin mint. (a) It was guarded only by a per-minute rate limit (`checkRateLimit(uid,'watchAd',20,60000)`), so an account could claim up to ~20 ads/minute all day — the `MAX_ADS_PER_USER_PER_DAY` budget was a client-only display constant that no server path read. (b) `reward = Math.max(AD_REWARD_PER_30S, Math.floor(watchedSeconds/30)*AD_REWARD_PER_30S)` paid **0 coins** for any claim under 30s, yet `SponsoredPostCard` claims at 15s and both surfaces render "+N coins per 30s" — a real user watched an ad and earned nothing. (c) the stored `ad_impressions` row recorded the raw client `watchDurationSeconds`, not the clamped value actually paid for. | ✅ **now closed** — `MAX_ADS_PER_USER_PER_DAY` moved into the canonical `src/shared/levelConfig.cjs` (synced to `functions/`), `monetizationService` derives its copy from it, and `watchAd` consumes the budget server-side via `enforceDailyLimit(uid, 1, 'ad', MAX_ADS_PER_USER_PER_DAY)` (the existing `fraud_limits` daily counter). The reward is now `Math.max(1, Math.floor(watchedSeconds/30)) * AD_REWARD_PER_30S`, so a sub-30s claim pays exactly the advertised one unit; the impression stores `watchedSeconds`. New `adRewardBoundGuard.test.js` (guards job) pins the daily-limit call, the reward floor, the clamped-duration write, the canonical-constant sync and the client derivation. |
| N372 **A paid post boost charged real coins but changed nothing in the feed, and an author could self-grant a free boost.** `functions/monetization.js#boostPost` debits `BOOST_COST_PER_DAY * days` coins, sets `posts/{id}.boostData.isBoosted = true` and writes `boosted_posts/{postId}` — but (a) no feed/ranking path ever read `boosted_posts` or `boostData` (the only consumer was a `PostCard` "Boosted" badge), so a user paid coins for a promotion that never surfaced their post; (b) `boosted_posts` had **no rule at all** (deny-all catch-all) and no index, so even a wired reader would have got `[]`; (c) the `posts` author-update rule allowed moving any key except the moderation list, so an author could set `boostData` themselves for free (the create rule likewise let a client seed it). | ✅ **now closed** — the viewer feed now reads active promotions (`feedService._getBoostedPosts`: bounded, `where('expiresAt','>',now)` + `orderBy('expiresAt','asc')` + `limit`, per-post `get` so a private/blocked/missing post is dropped rather than leaking) and tags them `isBoosted`, which `_scoreAndRankPostsOptimized` consumes as `finalScore *= BOOST_RANKING_FACTOR` (1.5) — so the paid promotion actually ranks. Added the `boosted_posts` rule (signed-in read, `write: if false` — server-only), the `(expiresAt, __name__)` composite index, and a TTL `expireAt` on the row. Locked `boostData` server-owned in `firestore.rules` (create requires `boostData == null`; the author-update carve-out now forbids `boostData`), removed the client-supplied `boostData` seed from `firestoreService` post creation, and let an **expired** boost be re-purchased (the old `boostData?.isBoosted` check blocked the post forever). New `postBoostWiringGuard.test.js` (guards job) pins all three layers. |

Any gift missing from the literal (e.g. `star`, `heart`, `galaxy`) advertised 5 coins, and the whole table silently drifts if a price changes. | ✅ **now closed** — `getGiftValue` now reads `GIFT_VALUES[giftType]` (imported from the shared config) and returns `null` for an unknown type; the toast appends the amount only when it is known, and the analytics value is `null` rather than a fabricated 5. New `giftPriceSingleSourceGuard.test.js` (guards job) scans every `src` module for a gift-price literal (`type: price` for each `GIFT_VALUES` entry, comments stripped) and fails if one reappears outside the canonical config / derived `videoData.js` catalog. |
| N374 **N372's boost hardening was only statically asserted — no adversarial (emulator) proof.** The boost rules (`posts` create `boostData == null`, the author-update carve-out, `boosted_posts` server-only write) were pinned by a source-text guard but never exercised against a live Firestore. | ✅ **now closed** — new `postBoostAuthorityRules.test.js` runs on the emulator in the `guards` job: an author seeding `boostData` on create and self-granting it on update are both refused, a plain author edit still succeeds, a client `setDoc` on `boosted_posts` is refused, a signed-in viewer can read the active-promotion signal, and an unauthenticated read is denied. 4 tests pass against the emulator (13+ suites / 92+ tests of rules checks green). |
| N375 **`gift_transactions` read rule keyed on fields no writer sets, so a gift party could never read their own row.** `functions/monetization.js#sendGift` writes `senderId`/`receiverId`, but the rule gated the read on `resource.data.fromUserId == uid() || resource.data.toUserId == uid()` — names that appear nowhere in any writer. Both expressions are always false, so the owner read was denied for every party (the `scheduled_messages` `senderId`-vs-`userId` and `video_reports` `reporterId`-vs-`reportedBy` bug class). | ✅ **now closed** — the rule keys on `senderId`/`receiverId` (plus `isAdmin()`). New `partyReadFieldNameGuard.test.js` (guards job) extracts the party field each writer sets (brace-matching parser over `functions`+`src`), extracts every `resource.data.<f> == uid()` field in a curated set of party-scoped read rules, and fails when a rule references a field no writer sets; it also runs an emulator check that both gift parties can read their row while a stranger and an unauthenticated caller are denied. A broad one-off scan of every `resource.data.X == uid()` rule against its writers confirmed `gift_transactions` was the only genuine mismatch (`sounds`/`story_replies`/`orders`/`marketplace_items`/`gifts`/`saved_sounds`/report collections all align). |
| N376 **The composer's Monetization panel was four dead paid controls.** `CreatePost` offered "Boost", "Subscriber Only", "Pay‑Per‑View" and "Tip Jar". The Boost type ran `spendCoins(uid, budget, 'post_boost')` after publish, but the stored `boost` field was never persisted by `firestoreService` and never read anywhere, and no `boostData`/`boosted_posts` row was written — coins debited, no promotion (financial integrity + fabricated success). The other three were persisted (`monetization`/`ppvPrice`/`tipJarAmounts`/`subscriptionTier`) but never read by any feed, post surface, rule or function, so a "Subscriber Only" post was world-readable and a "Tip Jar" collected nothing. The only real paid post feature is the post-publish `boostPost` callable (N372). | ✅ **now closed** — removed the whole panel: `MONETIZATION_TYPES`, `MonetizationPanel`, `BOOST_TIERS`, `state.monetization`/`state.boost`/`ppvPrice`/`tipJarAmounts`/`subscriptionTier`, the `SET_*` reducer cases, the `boost`/`monetization` payload fields, the post-publish `spendCoins('post_boost')` block, the orphaned `userCoins`/`userCoinsError` balance read, the dead `services.monetization` wiring/import, and the "Monetization" composer tab + preview tile. The boost daily rate is now single-sourced: `BOOST_DAILY_RATE_COINS` in `src/shared/levelConfig.cjs` (synced to `functions/levelConfig.cjs`), read by `functions/monetization.js#boostPost` (with a `functions.config().app.boost_daily_rate` override) and by `PostOptionsDrawer`'s BoostModal preview; the dead `monetizationService.BOOST_COST_PER_DAY` literal was removed. New `composerPaidControlsGuard.test.js` (guards job) pins all of the above; `postVisibilityEnforcement.test.js` slice boundary updated. |
| N377 **The composer offered scheduling, but no writer ever published a scheduled post — every scheduled post was silently lost.** `firestoreService.createPost` has always written `status: 'scheduled'` + a future `scheduledTime` when the user schedules a post, and `postReadableByViewer`/`postListableByViewer` correctly hide `status != 'published'` — but **nothing anywhere** (no Cloud Function, no scheduled job, no client path, no rule) ever flipped `scheduled` to `published`. The status transition existed only as a dead constant, so a user who pressed "📅 Schedule Post" got a success toast and a post that was never visible to anyone, including themselves. The composer compounded the fabrication with a "Recurrence" control (daily/weekly/monthly) that persisted a `recurrence` field no writer, reader, rule or function consumed. | ✅ **now closed** — new `functions/scheduledPosts.js#publishScheduledPosts` (pubsub every 5 min, merged in `index.js`) is the single writer that publishes a due post: bounded/index-backed (`status == 'scheduled'` + `scheduledTime <= now`, `orderBy('scheduledTime','asc')`, `limit(50)`), skips soft-deleted/moderation-hidden posts, flips `status` to `published` + stamps `publishedAt`, and fires an **idempotent** author notification (deterministic doc id via `create()`, so a retried run never double-notifies or double-counts the unread shard). Added the `posts` `(status, scheduledTime)` composite index. The client no longer injects a scheduled post into the device-local feed cache or the `arvdoul:new_post_published` event (it is not live yet) and toasts "Post scheduled" instead of "Post published instantly". Removed the fabricated `recurrence` state/reducer/payload/UI. New `scheduledPostsGuard.test.js` (guards job) pins the module+merge, the query shape, the index, the client gate and the recurrence removal. |
| N378 **The composer's "Co‑Authors" panel and comment/gift toggles collected values nothing ever read.** `CoAuthorPanel` let an author add co-authors with a revenue `percentage`, and `state.settings` carried `enableComments`/`enableGifts`/`enableSharing`/`isNSFW` — none of which `firestoreService.createPost` persisted (it only ever wrote `enableComments: postData.enableComments !== false` from the *top-level* payload, which the composer never set) and none of which any feed, post surface, rule or function read. A "co-author" got no attribution, no revenue split and no notification; the toggles did nothing. (Same dead-control class as N376.) | ✅ **now closed** — removed `CoAuthorPanel`, `state.coAuthors`/`collaborationInvites`/`settings` and their reducers/payload fields; `PostPreview`'s dead `coAuthors[0]?.name` author-name read is gone. The `Author‑only`/`%`/invite UI no longer implies a revenue-split or attribution model the backend does not have. Guarded by `scheduledPostsGuard.test.js`. |

The `expiresAt`/`SET_EXPIRES_AT` selector had no UI dispatch site at all (an unreachable reducer case). (Same dead-control class as N376/N378.) | ✅ **now closed** — removed `TaggingPanel`, `searchUsers` wiring, `state.taggedPeople`/`taggedBrands`/`taggedProducts`/`topics`/`feeling`/`activity`/`customList`/`expiresAt` and their reducers/payload fields; the composer's "📍 Tags & Location" tab is now "📍 Location" (the real `LocationPanel`, which persists `location` and is rendered by `PostCard`). `scheduledPostsGuard.test.js` extended to pin the removal. |
| N380 **Three composer reducer branches were unreachable, and the composer error banner had no dismiss.** `SET_AUDIO_FILE`, `SET_VIDEO_FILE` and `SET_IS_DRAFT_LOADED` were `case` branches with no `dispatch` anywhere (the audio/video editors write through `SET_TYPE_DATA`/`SET_MEDIA_ITEMS`), and `CLEAR_ERROR` was never dispatched because the inline error banner (`ReviewSummary`) rendered `state.error` with no way to dismiss it. | ✅ **now closed** — removed the three dead cases; wired the error banner's dismiss button to `CLEAR_ERROR` (`ReviewSummary` now takes `dispatch`). `scheduledPostsGuard.test.js` gained a generic guard that fails if any composer reducer `case "X"` has no matching `dispatch({ type: "X" })`. |
| N381 **The composer's Post Predictions panel hid the server's explanation for every number.** `predictPostPerformance` returns `factors` — one human-readable sentence per scoring rule that produced `reach`/`engagement`/`viralScore` — but `InsightsPanel` rendered only the four figures, so a heuristic estimate looked like a measured forecast with no basis shown. | ✅ **now closed** — the panel now lists `insights.factors` and states the values are draft-derived heuristics, not a guarantee. (No new backend: the server already returned the array.) |
| N382 **`sendNotification` accepted any `type` from a signed-in client, so a caller could mint a trusted system or financial notice into another user's inbox.** The callable wrote whatever `notificationData` it was handed; a client could raise `security_alert`, `system_alert`, `payment_received`, `withdrawal_approved`, `verification`, `level_up` or `coins_earned` toward any recipient — indistinguishable from a real server-issued record, and the recipient has no way to tell. (The "no fabricated security/standing claims" rule, applied to the inbox.) Three dead client helpers (`createGiftNotification`, `createCoinRewardNotification`, `createRoyaltyPromotionNotification`) also routed through this hole; none had a caller. | ✅ **now closed** — `sendNotification` now validates `type` against two allowlists: a client may raise only social types toward *other* users (`like`, `comment`, `reply`, `mention`, `follow`, `follow_request`, `follow_accepted`, `friend_request`, `friend_accepted`, `message`, `group_message`, `group_join_request`, `event_update`, `story_collaboration`) and only `welcome`/`system` toward itself; everything else is `permission-denied`. The receiver's `gift_received` in-app notification moved server-side into `sendGift` via the new trusted `functions/notificationWriter.js` (the only place the gift can be proved); the dead client helpers and `_afterGiftSent` were removed. `notificationTypeAllowlistGuard.test.js` pins both directions (every type `src` sends is allowed; no trusted type is client-sendable). |
| N383 **`PostAnalyticsScreen`'s "Virality Score" was a fabricated bucket label, and its post timestamp was a placeholder word.** The panel rendered `views > 1000 ? 'High' : views > 100 ? 'Medium' : 'Normal'` — a virality *claim* computed from the raw view count with no virality model behind it, and "Normal" for a post whose views were simply unmeasured. The summary also printed `Created: Recent` when `createdAt` was absent (the only writer sets `serverTimestamp()`, so this was a placeholder in place of an honest "—"). | ✅ **now closed** — the panel renders the server's real `trendingScore` (written by `functions/feed.js#processScoreUpdates` on every public published post) as a number, or "Unavailable" when the score has not been computed; the label is "Trending Score", not "Virality Score". An absent `createdAt` renders "—". `noFabricatedData.test.js` pins both (no "Virality Score", no `> 1000 ? 'High'` bucket). |
| N384 **`QuestionCard`'s reputation badge used a private threshold table that diverged from the canonical 0-100 reputation scale.** The helper returned `Legend`/`Master`/`Expert`/`Skilled` at `reputation >= 5000/1000/500/100`, but reputation is a 0-100 standing score (`readStandingScore` clamps it to 0-100; `REPUTATION_BANDS` are 0/20/40/60/75/90), so the badge could never fire below 100 and mislabelled a 100 as `Skilled`. An absent reputation was also coerced to `?? 0`, which would render `Unestablished` for an unmeasured score. | ✅ **now closed** — `getReputationLevel` now delegates to the canonical `getReputationBand` from `levelSystemService.js`; the badge uses the gold accent token (the canonical bands carry a `color` *name*, not a hex, so the old `${color}20` produced an invalid colour). An absent reputation no longer coerces to 0. `noFabricatedData.test.js` pins all three. |

| N386 **Video Editor "Recent Projects" stored a display string (`updatedAtText: 'Just now'`).** Because the label was persisted verbatim, a project saved days ago still read "Just now" after a reload — a fabricated freshness claim. | ✅ **now closed** — the snapshot stores a real `updatedAt` ISO timestamp and `EditorHeader` derives the relative label (`formatSavedAt`); an absent/unparseable timestamp renders "—". `noFabricatedData.test.js` pins it. |

| N414 **Identity claims the platform could not verify: “guaranteed unique username” + “100% unique global identity code”.** `EditProfileScreen`'s Auto-Generate tooltip claimed a “guaranteed unique username via userService” and set availability to `available` without re-checking — but `generateUniqueUsername` falls back to a `Date.now()+random` suffixed handle when every candidate is taken, which is NOT re-verified against the `usernames` index, so a taken handle could render as free. `ProfileQRCodeModal` labelled the profile URL QR “100% unique global identity code” and `ProfileQRScannerModal`'s docblock called the manual fallback “100% reliable”; `userService`'s random-suffix branch was commented “Guaranteed fallback”. | ✅ **now closed** — the generate handler now runs `checkUsernameAvailability` on the produced handle (showing `checking`/`taken`/`error` instead of a blanket “available”), the tooltip reads “Suggest an available username”, the QR copy reads “Scannable link to your profile”, and the comments state the honest behaviour. `noFabricatedData.test.js` pins all three. |

| N416 **Fabricated person/era labels on list, event, sound and passport surfaces.** `SoundsScreen` printed “Unknown artist” for a missing artist (inventing a credited person); `PostCard` reposts printed “Original author” instead of resolving the real author; `EventDetailScreen` rendered “Attendee”/“attendee” as a name/alt/aria-label for a nameless roster row; `passportService`/`PassportScreen` minted a `CITIZEN` handle, a `Genesis Era`/`Epoch I` issuance date, and `Perpetual`/`Registered` status words the platform never measured. | ✅ **now closed** — sounds render `—`, reposts and the event roster resolve names through `resolveActorName` (real uid prefix, never a fabricated word), and the passport shows `—` when a value is absent. `noFabricatedData.test.js` (N416) pins all four. |
| N417 **Generic person-word fallbacks (`Member`/`Creator`) fabricated a person, and the composer's "Best time" invented a timestamp from no dataset.** Fourteen named-user surfaces (notifications, admin user list, comments drawer, rankings, polls, stories, connections, community members, blocked accounts, question answers, top bar, menu, profile store) resolved a missing display name to the literal word `Member` (or `Creator` for non-owners) — a fabricated identity that reads as a real account. Separately, `predictPostPerformance` returned a `bestTime` of `"Tuesday 18:00"`/`"Saturday 10:00"` with no measured posting-time dataset, rendered as "Best time: …". | ✅ **now closed** — every surface resolves an absent name through `resolveActorName` (a real uid prefix, never a generic word), and the prediction returns a `suggestedWindow` (`Weekday`/`Weekend`) labeled as a scheduling convention, never an invented timestamp. `noFabricatedData.test.js` (N417) pins all of it. |

| N420 **The level-perk table and the royal roadmap advertised financial rates the server never honours, and the video-editor canvas asserted a hardcoded resolution.** `LEVEL_PERKS` (shared `levelConfig.cjs`) sold a “Gift & Tip Boost — higher daily gift and tip earning multiplier” (no gift/tip multiplier exists — `functions/monetization.js` `sendGift` moves `giftValue` 1:1), a “5% Coin Store Bonus” (`purchaseCoins` credits exactly `pkg.coins`, no bonus), a “10% Cashout Fee Rebate” (there is no platform withdrawal fee at all — `withdrawalSettlement.js` pays `coinsToUsd(amount)` with no fee deduction), and an “Ascendant … 2x XP multiplier” (`functions/xpAward.js` applies `rule.xp` with no level multiplier). Separately `PreviewCanvas` hardcoded a `4K` status badge for every project and `MediaDrawer` labelled its (empty) stock catalogue “4K Videos”. | ✅ **now closed** — the perk table and royal copy now state only honours-able presentation/standing benefits (Gift & Tip Identity, Coin Store Access, Priority Cashout Review, title + standing), the canvas badge reports the project's selected `resolutionLabel` (hidden when unknown), and the stock drawer reads “Stock Videos”. `noFabricatedData.test.js` (N420) pins all of it. |
