| N430 **Royal standing was detected by a string compare against an object, so every granted royal title was invisible — and the royal roadmap advertised a crown/halo cosmetic the platform never renders.** `setActiveTitle` (`functions/levelSystem.js`) stores `activeTitle` as an object (`{ id, name, icon, domain }`), but `ProfileHeroSection` and `EditProfileScreen` compared `profile.activeTitle === 'duke' / 'king'` — always false, so a legitimate Duke/King never saw the Royal Family badge or the heraldic editor section. `ProfileHeroSection`'s fallback `profile.activeTitle`/`titles` string compare shared the defect. The `ProfileSystemRoadmapModal` Duke/King privilege lists also promised a “Royal Crown Profile Badge with ambient violet profile aura”, a “Golden Monarch Crown & Radiant Halo Frame on avatar across all screens” and “Royal Standing recorded in your citizenship standing” — the aura is a level-unlocked crest ring (not royal), there is no separate golden halo/crown cosmetic, and the standing lives in the passport, not a “citizenship standing” surface. | ✅ **now closed** — `profileReadModel` gained `resolveActiveTitleId`/`resolveRoyalFlag` (object *and* string shapes, plus a denormalized `titles` array) and both surfaces consume them; the roadmap now lists only the rendered benefits (Royal Family badge, title on profile, standing in the passport). `profileReadModel.test.js` and `noFabricatedData.test.js` (N430) pin it. |
| N431 **`users/{uid}.followerCount`/`followingCount` are seeded `0` at signup and never written again, so every server reader that trusted the raw field saw a fabricated zero.** Every follow/unfollow mutation routes the increment to a sharded `counter_shards` document (client `CountersManager`, inside `userService.followUser`/`unfollowUser`/`approveFollowRequest`/`blockUser`); no writer ever mirrors the shard sum back onto the user document. The consequences were not cosmetic: (1) the fan-out threshold in `functions/feed.js` and `functions/stories.js` (`followerCount > 10000`) **never tripped**, so a celebrity's post/story was fanned out to *every* follower — the exact unbounded write storm the pull model exists to prevent; (2) `functions/search.js` shipped `followerCount: 0` to the Algolia user index for every user, so people-search rendered a fabricated “0 followers”; (3) `socialGraphService.hydrateProfiles`/`getSummary`, `profileReadModel`, `rankingService` and `monetizationService.getUserPopularityPosition` all read the stale raw field (only `userService.getUserProfile` applied the shard overlay). A client could also *seed* an arbitrary `followerCount` at signup and write it via profile update, minting standing it did not have. | ✅ **now closed** — new `functions/userCounters.js` is the authoritative reader: `readUserCounter(userId, field)` sums the shards in one batched `db.getAll` (legacy raw field only when no shard exists) and is now used by both fan-out decisions (feed.js treats an unresolvable count as “large”, so it fails toward the pull model rather than a write storm); the scheduled `aggregateUserCounters` mirror writes the shard sum back onto `users/{uid}` (only when it changed, so the Algolia `onWrite` index does not churn) so every remaining raw reader reads a real number. The counts are now server-authoritative: `firestore.rules` `touchesServerAuthoritativeFields` + the create rule pin them to `0`, and `SERVER_AUTHORITATIVE_FIELDS` mirrors it. New `followerCounterSourceGuard.test.js` (guards job) pins the module+merge, the byte-identical server/client shard hash + layout, the batched read, the mirror shape, both fan-out reads, the fail-safe default, the rules denylist/create pin and the contract parity. |
| N432 **14 of 21 achievements and 8 of 15 titles are unearnable: `evaluateAchievements` / `claimTitle` score criteria against `users/{uid}` metric fields that no writer ever populates, so the evaluators read a permanent `0` and deny every qualifying user.** The evaluator reads `commentsCount`, `pollVotesCount`, `profilesVisitedCount`, `giftsSentCount`, `hostedLive`, `likesCount`, `friendsCount`, `lifetimeCoinsEarned`, `hasPassport`/`passportCreated`, `isPioneer`, and (titles) `contributionScore`/`reputationScore`/`influenceScore`/`creationsCount`/`isFounder` — none of which any client or server path ever writes. Affected achievements: `viral_moment`, `creator_status`, `first_live_stream`, `civic_voter`, `helpful_citizen`, `nation_explorer`, `social_architect`, `first_patron`, `coin_magnate`, `passport_holder`, `state_noble`, `founding_pioneer` (plus `minPosts` achievements blocked by the never-written `postsCount`). Affected titles: `visionary`, `helper`, `mentor`, `community_leader`, `founder`, `pioneer`, and the royal `duke`/`king` (their `minAchievements`/`reputation`/`contribution`/`influence` dimensions are all permanently 0, so royal eligibility can never be met either). This is the inverse of “fabricated standing”: a fabricated *zero* that denies a real accomplishment. | ✅ **now closed for the metrics that have a real, cheap source** — new `functions/userMetrics.js#computeUserMetrics` aggregates the collections the platform actually writes, using bounded `.count()` aggregation reads (no collection scan): `comments.userId` → `commentsCount`, `poll_votes.userId` → `pollVotesCount`, `profile_views.viewerId` → `profilesVisitedCount`, `gift_transactions.senderId` → `giftsSentCount`, `live_streams.userId` → `hostedLive` (existence), `posts.authorId` → `postsCount` (count) and → `likesCount` (a `.aggregate({ sum })` over `posts.stats.likes`, now a real number thanks to N433). `evaluateAchievements` overlays the result on the stored doc (`const stats = { ...userData, ...computed }`) and scores that; a metric that cannot be resolved stays absent (fail-closed — unmet, never a fabricated pass), and any aggregation error leaves the metric `undefined` rather than 0. `achievementMetricSourceGuard.test.js` (guards job) pins the module, the overlay, the real writer field names, the bounded aggregation shape and the fail-closed behaviour. **Still BLOCKED (documented, not silently “fixed”):** `friendsCount` (a mutual-follow intersection, not one collection), `lifetimeCoinsEarned` (needs a product definition of “earned” vs. purchased coins), `hasPassport` (no passport is persisted anywhere), `isPioneer`/`isFounder` (need a genesis-cutoff constant that does not exist), and the whole reputation/contribution/influence score model (no formula is defined anywhere) — the titles gated on those (`visionary` `minCreations`, `helper`/`mentor`/`community_leader` `minContribution`/`minReputation`/`minInfluence`) remain unreachable until that formula is specified. |
| N433 **`posts/{postId}.stats.likes`/`comments`/`shares`/`saves` are seeded `0` at create and never written again, so server ranking and search read a fabricated zero.** Every like/save/share/comment mutation routes its increment to a sharded `counter_shards` document (client `CountersManager`, inside `firestoreService.toggleLike`/`toggleSave`/`sharePost` and `commentService.createComment`/`deleteComment`); no writer mirrors the shard sum onto the post. The consequences: (1) `functions/feed.js#processScoreUpdates` read `post.likeCount`/`commentCount`/`shareCount`/`viewCount` — top-level fields that **no writer populates at all** — so the engagement term was a constant `0` and `personalizationScore`/`trendingScore` collapsed to a pure recency ranking (every post ranked identically regardless of popularity); (2) `functions/search.js#indexPost` shipped the stale `stats.likes`/`stats.comments` (both `0`) to the Algolia post index, so content search sorted by a fabricated engagement score. Unlike N431 (which had a raw field at least seeded truthfully-ish), here the feed reader used field names that do not exist anywhere — the ranking signal was dead. | ✅ **now closed** — new `functions/postCounters.js` mirrors the video counter model (`aggregateVideoViews`/`aggregateVideoLikes`): `readPostCounterShards` sums a post's shards in one batched `db.getAll` (no scan) and the scheduled `aggregatePostCounters` (cursor-paged, bounded per run) writes the sums back onto `posts/{postId}.stats.{likes,comments,shares,saves}`, only when a value changed (so the Algolia `onWrite` index does not churn). `feed.js#processScoreUpdates` now reads `post.stats.*` (the real fields) instead of the never-written top-level ones. The shard hash + doc-id layout stays byte-identical to `src/utils/CountersManager.js`. New `postCounterSourceGuard.test.js` (guards job) pins the merge, the hash/layout parity, the batched read, the mirror shape and the feed reader. The client already overlays the live shard sum via `countersManager.apply`, so the UI was never wrong — only the server ranking/search. |
| N434 **`communities/{communityId}.stats.eventCount` is seeded `0` when the community is created and never written again, so the community detail screen's "Events" card always rendered a fabricated `0`.** `functions/communities.js#createCommunity` seeds `stats: { memberCount: 1, postCount: 0, eventCount: 0, activityScore: 100 }`; `postCount` is incremented by `createCommunityPost` and `memberCount` by the join/leave callables, but **no path ever updates `eventCount`**. `CommunityDetailScreen.jsx` renders it as the "Events" stat and `communityService.getCommunityStats` reads it (`unknownNumber(community.stats?.eventCount)`), so every community showed "Events 0" regardless of how many events its members had created — the same counter-with-no-writer class as N431 (followers) and N433 (post engagement). Events do carry a `communityId` (client `eventService.createEvent`), so the count is derivable, but the community document is server-write-only (the rules reject client writes), so the derived counter must be maintained by a server trigger. | ✅ **now closed** — new `functions/eventCounters.js#onEventWrite` (firestore `events/{eventId}` `onWrite`) applies a **net delta** to the owning community's `stats.eventCount`: `-1` if the event counted before, `+1` if it counts after (a status-only or community move is handled by the same delta, and a registration/check-in write — which never changes status/communityId — cancels to zero so no community is read or written). `COUNTED_EVENT_STATUSES = ['published','open','live','ended']` so a `draft`/`cancelled` event does not count and cancelling/deleting decrements. It reads the affected communities in one batched `db.getAll` and applies the deltas in a single `db.batch`, and **only counts an event toward a community the organizer is a member of** (forgery guard: a user cannot point an event at a foreign community to inflate its counter). New `communityEventCountSourceGuard.test.js` (guards job) pins the merge, the trigger shape, the net-delta logic, the status set, the membership guard and the real UI consumer. |
| N435 **`videos/{id}.stats.comments` is seeded `0` and never written: video comments increment a phantom `posts/{videoId}` counter nobody reads.** The video comment UI (`VideoComments`) submits a comment with the *video id* as the target, so `commentService.createComment` runs `countersManager.increment({ docPath: \`posts/${videoId}\`, field: 'comments' })` — creating a `counter_shards` entry for a `posts/{videoId}` document that does not exist (a video is not a post), which no reader ever consults. `videos/{id}.stats.comments` therefore stayed a fabricated `0`, and it feeds two real readers: `updateVideoRankingScores` (the `comments * 2` term of the video ranking score) and the video card / `videoService` comment count. Same counter-with-no-writer class as N431/N433/N434. | ✅ **now closed** — a new scheduled `functions/video.js#aggregateVideoComments` derives `stats.comments` from the real `comments` collection with a bounded `.count()` (`.where('postId','==',videoId).where('isDeleted','==',false)`, no collection scan), mirroring `aggregateVideoViews`/`aggregateVideoLikes`, and only writes when the value changed. The client no longer increments the phantom post counter for a video comment (`VideoComments` passes `targetType: 'video'`; `createComment` skips the `posts/*` increment for it). New `videoCommentCounterSourceGuard.test.js` (guards job) pins the mirror, the ranking read, the client guard and the UI tagging. |
| N436 **`videos/{id}.stats.saves` is seeded `0` and never written, yet the video card and reels screen render it as a real save count.** A viewer's save state lives in `users/{uid}/saved_videos/{videoId}` (`videoService.saveVideo`/`unsaveVideo`), and the `videos` update rule excludes `stats`, so no client could maintain the aggregate — `videos/{id}.stats.saves` stayed a permanent fabricated `0`. `VideoCard` renders `formatViewCount(video.saves)` and `ReelsScreen` renders `stats.saves.toLocaleString()`, both as if measured. Same counter-with-no-writer class as N431/N433/N434/N435. | ✅ **now closed** — a new server-authoritative `toggleVideoSave` callable (rate-limited, transactional) is the only writer of `stats.saves`: it keys on the `videos/{id}/saved_by/{uid}` record (a save exists iff that doc exists) and increments/decrements the aggregate, staying idempotent (a no-op when the state already matches). The client `saveVideo`/`unsaveVideo` call it through the canonical `FUNCTIONS.TOGGLE_VIDEO_SAVE` binding (best-effort, so the private snapshot write still succeeds if the counter call fails). The new `saved_by` subcollection is server-internal (`allow read, write: if false` — a read would be a save-graph enumeration oracle). New `videoSaveCounterSourceGuard.test.js` (guards job) pins the callable, the FUNCTIONS map, the client wiring, the display consumers and the rules. |
| N437 **`PostAnalyticsScreen` presented the seeded `posts/{id}.stats.views` as the real view total.** Post views are counted as a sharded `counter_shards` document (`functions/analytics.js#recordPostView` → `incrementShardedCounter('posts/{id}','views')`) — no writer ever touches `posts/{id}.stats.views`, so it stays the create-time `0`. The screen read `stat(postDoc.stats?.views ?? postDoc.viewsCount ?? postDoc.views)` and rendered it as "Views", so every post's analytics showed a measured `0` views (and a `0%` engagement rate derived from it). The true total is only reachable by summing the shards. | ✅ **now closed** — the screen resolves views through `CountersManager.get({ docPath: 'posts/{id}', field: 'views', fallback: stats.views })` (the same shard layout `recordPostView` writes; falls back to a legacy `stats.views` only when no shard exists yet). `postCounterSourceGuard.test.js` (guards job) now pins the sharded read and forbids presenting the raw seeded field as the total. |
| N438 **`users/{uid}.postCount` had no server writer and was not server-authoritative: the owner's profile showed a fabricated "0 Posts", and any client could mint the count.** `users/{uid}` is seeded `postCount: 0` at signup, but the only writer was a best-effort *client* increment inside `firestoreService.repostPost` — so a normal post never moved the count (only a repost did), while `ProfileMetricsGrid` ("Posts"), `QuickAccessPanel`, `AuthContext` and `socialGraphService.hydrateProfiles` rendered it as a real total. Unlike `followerCount`/`followingCount` (N431) it was absent from `SERVER_AUTHORITATIVE_FIELDS` and the rules denylist, so any signed-in owner could `updateDoc` it to an arbitrary number. Same counter-with-no-writer class as N431/N433/N434/N435/N436. | ✅ **now closed** — `postCount` is added to `SERVER_AUTHORITATIVE_FIELDS` (`src/config/profileContracts.js`) and to `touchesServerAuthoritativeFields()` in `firestore.rules` (the signup `create` still pins it to `0`), and a new `functions/userCounters.js#onPostWrite` document trigger is the only writer: it applies a net delta per author (`isDeleted === false` before → `-1`, after → `+1`, zero-delta updates skipped) via `FieldValue.increment`, touching only users that still exist. The cursor-paged `reconcileUserPostCounts` job (bounded `count()` over `posts where authorId == uid`, no scan) corrects legacy drift and the old repost-only bump. The client `updateUserPostCount` writer (and its repost call site) was deleted. New `postAuthorCountSourceGuard.test.js` (guards job) pins the trigger, the reconcile job, the rules/contract parity and the absence of the client writer. |
| N439 **Comment reaction counts are written to shards but no read path sums them — every comment rendered a fabricated "0 likes".** `commentService.likeComment`/`dislikeComment`/`removeLikeDislike` increment a sharded `counter_shards` counter for `comments/{id}` (fields `likes`/`dislikes`) and never write the numeric fields on the comment document (they stay at the create-time `0`). No reader resolved the shard sum, so `CommentsDrawer` (`formatCount(comment.likes)` at ~line 953 and the "Best" sort at ~line 265) and `VideoComments` (`comment.likes ?? '—'` at ~line 464 and the sort at ~line 244) showed `0` likes on every comment, and the realtime subscription (`subscribeToPostComments`) replaced an optimistic like count with that seeded `0`. `likesBy` is not a substitute: it is capped at `REACTION_ARRAY_CAP` (200), so it undercounts. Same shard-written/raw-read class as N431/N433/N437. | ✅ **now closed** — new `CountersManager.applyAll` overlays the shard sum onto a batch of items in bounded `getAll` chunks (falling back to the raw field only when no shard exists, like `apply`), and `commentService._overlayReactionCounts` applies it on every read path: `getCommentsByPost`, `getComment`, `getReplies`, and the `subscribeToPostComments` snapshot callback (before it builds the nested tree, so an optimistic like count is not clobbered). New `commentReactionCounterSourceGuard.test.js` (guards job) pins the overlay call sites + the batched read, and behaviourally tests the sum/fallback/batching. **Also fixed the sibling facet:** the drawer's reply expander reads a derived `comment.repliesCount` that no read path populated (the count lives in the sharded `replies` counter), so a comment with pre-existing replies showed no expander until the viewer replied themselves — the overlay now aliases the `replies` shard onto `repliesCount` (never onto the `replies` child array), and `getReplies.total` reads the shard sum instead of the page size. |
| N440 **`firestoreService.getPost`/`getPostsByUser` overlaid a subset of the sharded post counters, so the post-detail view rendered a fabricated `0` comment count.** Post engagement lives in `counter_shards` (docPath `posts/{id}`; `commentService.createComment`/`deleteComment` and `firestoreService.toggleLike`/`toggleSave`/`sharePost` increment it, and `posts/{id}.stats.{likes,comments,shares,saves}` stay at the create-time `0`). The reader overlay listed `['likes','saves','shares','gifts','giftValue']` — **`comments` was omitted** — so `PostDetails`/`PostCard`/`PostAnalyticsScreen` (all of which read through `postService.getPost`) showed `0` comments while the feed (which overlays the full set) showed the real count. `getPostsByUser` had the same omission and also issued one `countersManager.apply` per post (N+1 shard reads). Same shard-written/raw-read class as N431/N433/N437/N439. | ✅ **now closed** — both `getPost` and `getPostsByUser` overlay the full set `['likes','comments','saves','shares','gifts','giftValue']`, and `getPostsByUser` now uses the batched `countersManager.applyAll` (one `getAll` per field-chunk) instead of a per-post `apply`. `postCounterSourceGuard.test.js` (guards job) now fails if any `posts/` overlay omits a sharded field. |
| N441 **`functions/postCounters.js` never mirrored the `views` shard, so the feed's view-weighted engagement term was a permanent 0.** `recordPostView` (`functions/analytics.js`) increments a sharded `counter_shards` counter for `posts/{id}` with field `views`, but `POST_COUNTER_FIELDS` listed only `['likes','comments','shares','saves']`, so `aggregatePostCounters` never wrote `posts/{id}.stats.views` and `functions/feed.js#processScoreUpdates` read `stats.views || 0` as a constant 0 — the `views * 0.1` term contributed nothing to `trendingScore`/`personalizationScore` (only ~2% of a real post's engagement). The client overlay has the same field list, so `CountersManager.apply` also left `stats.views` at the seeded 0. Same shard-written/never-mirrored class as N433. | ✅ **now closed** — `views` is added to `POST_COUNTER_FIELDS` (the shard hash + doc-id layout is byte-identical between the client and server, so the mirror sums the same documents `recordPostView` writes), and `postCounterSourceGuard.test.js` (guards job) now parses every `incrementShardedCounter('posts/{id}', '<field>')` in `analytics.js` and fails if a sharded post field is missing from `POST_COUNTER_FIELDS`. |
| N442 **`personalizeFeed` (`functions/stories.js`) ranked stories on `stats.likes`/`stats.shares`, which no writer populates — reactions and shares were a constant 0 in the score.** `aggregateStoryStats` writes the per-emoji `stats.reactions` map (summed from `reaction_shards`), `stats.comments`, `stats.replies` and `stats.views`, but never `stats.likes`/`stats.shares`. The ranking expression `(stats.likes||0)*1.5 + (stats.comments||0)*2.5 + (stats.shares||0)*4 + (stats.views||0)*0.2` therefore ignored reactions entirely (a story with many reactions scored the same as one with none). Same written-field/read-field mismatch class as N433/N441. | ✅ **now closed** — ranking sums the reactions map (`Object.values(stats.reactions)`), weights comments/replies, and `storyRankingFieldSourceGuard.test.js` (guards job) fails if `personalizeFeed` reads the dead `stats.likes` field or stops reading `stats.reactions`. |
| N443 **A block hid the direct thread from the inbox but the thread stayed openable by a direct link.** N245 made `getUserConversations` drop a `direct` thread between a blocked pair, but `messagesService.getConversation` — the single thread read entry point that `getMessages`, `subscribeToConversation`, `reactToMessage`, `deleteMessage` and `editMessage` all route through — had no block gate. Opening `/chat/:conversationId` (a deep link, a back/forward navigation, or the still-mounted screen after a block) therefore returned the full thread, and the block looked like a no-op again. | ✅ **now closed** — `getConversation` gates a `direct` conversation between a blocked pair (either direction) on both the fresh read and the cache-first path via `_isDirectBlocked` (one cached `getRelationship`); groups/channels are never gated and the check fails open on a graph error. New `messagingThreadBlockGate.test.js` (guards job) drives the real service: blocked-out, blocked-by, unblocked, group/channel-kept, cache-path and fail-open. |
| N444 **The home feed ranked on top-level engagement fields (`likeCount`/`commentCount`/`shareCount`/`viewCount`) that no writer sets, so every post scored identically and the feed collapsed to pure recency.** Post engagement is a sharded `counter_shards` counter (`CountersManager`; `firestoreService.toggleLike`/`toggleSave`/`sharePost`, `commentService.createComment`), and `posts/{id}.stats.*` is seeded `0` and only mirrored later. `feedService._calculateFallbackScore` read the dead top-level aliases, so the engagement term was a constant 0. | ✅ **now closed** — `_fetchOptimizedSources` overlays the shard sums onto every fetched post (`_overlayFeedCounters` → `countersManager.applyAll`, one bounded `getAll` per field-chunk) before scoring, and `_calculateFallbackScore` reads `post.stats.*` (top-level aliases kept only as a legacy fallback). `feedRankingFieldSourceGuard.test.js` (guards job) pins it. |
| N445 **A dead watch-time ranking term (`post.avgWatchTime`, weight `WATCH_TIME_WEIGHT`) multiplied every post's score by 1.0.** No writer sets `avgWatchTime` on a post (the video path writes `stats.totalWatchTime`, not an average), so the `if (p.avgWatchTime !== undefined)` branch never ran. | ✅ **now closed** — the dead branch and the `WATCH_TIME_WEIGHT` constant are removed; `feedRankingFieldSourceGuard.test.js` fails if either returns. |
| N446 **`videoService.getVideoAnalytics` was dead code reading `stats.averageWatchTime` and a `videos/{id}/stats` subcollection no writer populates.** The method (and its facade export) had no caller, and the video stats schema uses `avgWatchTime` + a `stats` map, not a `stats` subcollection. | ✅ **now closed** — the dead method + facade export removed; the upload path's `stats` seed now writes the canonical `avgWatchTime` field (was `averageWatchTime`). |
| N447 **A committed-but-unacknowledged money action could be charged twice because its server idempotency key was derived from `Date.now()`.** `videoService.sendTip` sent `idempotencyKey: tip_${uid}_${videoId}_${Date.now()}` and `ProfileTipModal` built `tip_${uid}_${recipient}_${Date.now()}` — the key changed on every attempt, so a client/SDK retry of the same intent defeated the ledger dedupe and debited again. `purchaseMarketplaceItem` had the same class (a `Date.now()` fallback key). | ✅ **now closed** — `sendTip` accepts and forwards a caller-supplied key (`videoService` sends one stable `newIdempotencyKey()` per tip intent; the server falls back to `crypto.randomUUID()` only for direct API callers). `ProfileTipModal` sends one stable key per send and is re-entrancy-guarded. `purchaseMarketplaceItem` prefers a caller key/`orderId` and falls back to `uuidv4()`, never `Date.now()`. `moneyPathIdempotencyKeyGuard.test.js` (guards job) pins all of it. |
| N448 **The comment read paths did not apply the block/mute graph, so a blocked or muted author's comments still rendered under a post.** Every other read surface (feed, notifications, search, ranking, stories, live, videos) resolves `socialGraphService.getHiddenAuthorIds`; the comment thread did not — `getCommentsByPost` (and `PostCard`'s preview, which reads through it) returned every comment, `getReplies` returned every reply, and `subscribeToPostComments` re-emitted them live (the component-level filter only knew about blocks, never mutes). | ✅ **now closed** — all three read paths resolve the viewer (`options.viewerId` or the live session) and drop hidden authors (`getCommentsByPost` on both the Firestore and local-cache merge paths; `getReplies`; the subscription snapshot); `CommentsDrawer`/`VideoComments` pass `viewerId`. `commentBlockFiltering.test.js` (guards job) drives the real service and pins all three surfaces. |
| N449 **Two home-feed read paths returned the raw `posts/{id}.stats.*` map, so the `latest` tab and the degraded/error fallback rendered fabricated zeros for likes/comments/shares.** N444 overlaid the shard sums in `_fetchOptimizedSources` (the ranked path only). `feedService._getLatestFeed` (the `feedType === 'latest'` tab) and `_getFallbackSimpleFeed` (the timeout/error fallback that `getSmartFeed` serves as a last resort) each build their own page and returned it without overlaying, so `PostCard` read `post.stats.likes ?? null` → the seeded `0`. Same shard-written/raw-read class as N437/N439/N440. | ✅ **now closed** — both paths call `_overlayFeedCounters` (the same batched `countersManager.applyAll` the ranked path uses) before returning. `feedRankingFieldSourceGuard.test.js` (guards job) fails if either path stops overlaying. |
| N450 **The server-side feed rankers fell back to the unwritten top-level `likes`/`comments`/`shares` aliases when `personalizationScore` was unset.** `functions/feed.js#getPersonalizedFeed` and `#getPersonalizedFeedML` computed `engagement = (p.likes||0) + (p.comments||0)*2 + (p.shares||0)*3` in the `personalizationScore == null` branch — the same dead top-level aliases N433/N444 removed from the score writer (`processScoreUpdates` reads `post.stats.*`), so a freshly-created post that the score job had not yet visited ranked with a constant 0 engagement. | ✅ **now closed** — both fallbacks read the canonical `p.stats` map (`const s = p.stats || {}`). `feedRankingFieldSourceGuard.test.js` (guards job) pins it. |
| N451 **Community `stats.postCount` / `stats.activityScore` had an increment writer but no decrement writer, so both drifted upward forever.** `functions/communities.js#createCommunity` seeds `postCount: 0` / `activityScore: 100` and `createCommunityPost` incremented them (+1 / +10), but nothing decremented them — deleting a community post (author delete via `firestoreService.deletePost`, batch delete, or admin removal), unpublishing it, or moving it to another community left the counters pointing at content that no longer exists. `CommunityDetailScreen` renders `stats.postCount` as the "Posts" card and `communityService.getCommunityStats` reads both, so the drift was user-visible. The counter-with-no-(decrement)-writer class of N431/N433/N434. | ✅ **now closed** — the counters now have a single server writer: `functions/communityPostCounters.js#onCommunityPostWrite`, a net-delta document trigger on `posts/{postId}` (counts a live, published community post, decrements on delete/unpublish/move, cancels to zero on a like/comment/view update), with a non-member forgery guard and a cursor-paged `reconcileCommunityPostCounts` job for pre-trigger drift. `createCommunityPost` no longer writes the counters (single-writer, no double count). `communityPostCountSourceGuard.test.js` (guards job) pins all of it. |
| N452 **The messaging inbox online dot read a `presenceOnline` field no writer ever set (and a dead Realtime-Database `status/` path).** `users/{uid}` carries no `isOnline`/`lastActive` (presence moved to the scope-gated `user_presence/{uid}` at N321), so every row dot rendered "offline" forever, and the header painted a hardcoded green dot claiming the *viewer* was online. | ✅ **now closed** — `socialGraphService.getPresenceMap(ids)` is the single sanctioned presence reader (per-doc, scope-gated, denied/failed reads omitted so a failure renders offline, never a fabricated online). `getUserConversations` hydrates only 1:1 counterparts and sets `presenceOnline` from it; the screen's row dot is direct-only and the header's hardcoded dot is removed. `inboxPreviewPresenceWiring.test.js` (guards job) pins it. |
| N453 **Every inbox row showed "No messages yet" because the preview read `c.lastMessage`, a field no writer sets.** `sendMessage` writes `last_messages/{conversationId}` (rules: participant-only), but the inbox never read it. | ✅ **now closed** — `messagesService._fetchLastMessages(ids)` reads `last_messages` in bounded `documentId() in` chunks and `getUserConversations` attaches the real `lastMessage` (null when there genuinely is none). The screen's dead `hasAttachment`/`hasPhoto`/`photoPreview` props were replaced by a real `attachmentType` badge derived from the stored message `type`. `inboxPreviewPresenceWiring.test.js` (guards job) pins it. |
| N454 **Every inbox unread badge rendered 0 because the screen read `c.unreadCounts[uid]`, a field no writer sets.** The server (`functions/messaging.js#incrementUnreadForMessage`) and `markConversationAsRead` maintain sharded `unread_counters/unread_{cid}_{uid}/shards/{n}` docs instead. | ✅ **now closed** — `messagesService._fetchUnreadCounts(userId, ids)` sums the shards (one bounded `getDocs` per conversation, limit = shard count) and `getUserConversations` attaches the real `unreadCount` (absent → `null`, so no badge rather than a fabricated 0); the row badge and the roll-up read it. `inboxPreviewPresenceWiring.test.js` (guards job) pins it. |
| N455 **The Vibes grid rendered fabricated creator identity.** Story docs carry no `authorName`/`authorPhoto` (the writer never sets them), so `storiesArr[0]?.authorName || g.userId` showed the raw uid as the display name, the stock placeholder as the avatar, and `verified: false` / `category: 'Following'` were asserted for every creator regardless of reality. | ✅ **now closed** — `StoriesScreen` hydrates the real creator profiles in one batched `socialGraphService.hydrateProfiles` + `getEdgeSet` read and derives the honest name (`resolveActorName`), handle (`pickHandle`/`deriveHandle`), avatar (`getSafeAvatarUrl`), `verified` (real flag) and category (Friends/Following/Discover from the real edge). `vibesCreatorIdentityWiring.test.js` (guards job) pins it. |
| N456 **The Vibes grid offered two dead category tabs — Close Friends and Live — and their matching badges.** `isCloseFriend`/`isLive` were hardcoded `false` and no story was ever written with a `close_friends`/`live` audience, so both tabs matched nothing and the LIVE / green-star badges never rendered truthfully. | ✅ **now closed** — both tabs (and the `isCloseFriend`/`isLive` fields, LIVE badges, `tab.isLive` pulse and `Star`/`Radio`/`MapPin` imports) are removed until the backing model exists. `vibesCreatorIdentityWiring.test.js` (guards job) pins it. |
| N457 **The Vibes grid's multi-story dots fabricated "3" for a single-item group and its media badge keyed on a `badgeType` no writer emits.** The dots rendered `Array.from({ length: story.itemsCount || 3 })`, so a creator with one story showed three dots; the badge branches read `'photo'`/`'music'`/`'location'`/`'live'`/`'star'` while the story `type` is `image`/`video`/`audio`/`text`/`event`, so the badge icon never matched the real media. | ✅ **now closed** — the dots render only when `itemsCount > 1` (capped at 8), and the media badge reflects the real story `type`. `vibesCreatorIdentityWiring.test.js` (guards job) pins it. |
| N458 **The 1:1 chat header's presence was a dead control reading the writer-less Realtime-Database `status/` path.** `messagesService.setUserOnline` had zero callers anywhere, so `subscribeToUserPresence` (which `ChatScreen` used for its "Online / Last seen" line) always read an empty `status/{uid}` node — the header could never render real presence. The `messagingStore` `presence`/`presenceSubscription` slice and `setPresence` action had no reader or caller either (dead state). Real presence lives in the scope-gated `user_presence/{uid}` (N321). | ✅ **now closed** — `ChatScreen` reads the counterpart through `socialGraphService.getPresenceMap([uid])` (the single sanctioned, scope-gated reader) and renders the honest `isOnline`/`lastActive` (a denied/failed read leaves it blank, never a fabricated "Online"). The writer-less `setUserOnline`/`subscribeToUserPresence` methods, the `PRESENCE`/`LAST_SEEN` RTDB path constants, the service facade exports, the `presence_update` event branch and the dead `messagingStore` presence slice were all removed (typing stays — it has a real writer). `chatPresenceWiring.test.js` + `subscriptionLifecycleGuard.test.js` (guards job) pin it. |
| N459 **A creator's raw story view/reaction totals (`stories/{id}/view_shards`, `/reaction_shards`) were readable by every signed-in user.** The shard sums are rolled up server-side and the only client reader is the creator's own insights panel (`storyService.getStoryInsights`/`subscribeToInsights`, which gate on `story.userId == uid`), yet the read rule was a bare `allow read: if isSignedIn();` — so any authenticated client could point-read one creator's private story view and reaction counts. The sibling `analytics_shards` was already scoped to the creator; these two were missed. | ✅ **now closed** — both reads are scoped to `isSignedIn() && (vibeData().userId == uid() || isAdmin())`, matching `analytics_shards`. Clients still write their own view/reaction increments. `livePresenceAndHealthPrivacyRules.test.js` (guards job) pins the boundary. |
| N460 **`badges`/`user_badges` were world-readable (`allow read: if isSignedIn()`) though admin-written with no client reader.** The badge catalogue and the per-user assignment registry (`user_badges/{id}`) are written only by admins, and the Achievements screen reads the viewer's OWN earned badges from `achievements/{uid}/items` (`achievementService.getUserAchievements`) — no surface reads `badges` or `user_badges`. The signed-in read therefore exposed the full badge-assignment registry (who holds what) to any authenticated client. | ✅ **now closed** — both reads are scoped to `isAdmin()`. `readScopePrivacyRules.test.js` documents the boundary. |
| N461 **`comments/{id}/likes/{uid}` and `/dislikes/{uid}` marker docs were readable by every signed-in user.** The marker exists only as the liker's own "have I already liked this?" existence check (`commentService.likeComment`/`dislikeComment` point-read their own uid), so the signed-in read let any client enumerate who liked/disliked which comment (and, for a comment on a private/followers-only Vibe, the reactor identities). The tallies live on the comment doc. | ✅ **now closed** — both reads are scoped to `isSignedIn() && (likerId == uid() || isAdmin())`, matching the sibling `reactions` marker boundary (N301). |
| N462 **`copyright_registry/{workId}` was readable by every signed-in user though no client reads it.** The registry maps a work to its registrant, and there is no `registerWork`/copyright reader anywhere in `src` (the only hit is a report-reason label). The signed-in read was a needless world-readable work->registrant map. | ✅ **now closed** — the read is scoped to `isSignedIn() && (registeredBy == uid() || userId == uid() || isAdmin())`. `readScopePrivacyRules.test.js` pins the boundary. |
| N463 **`trending_topics/{topicId}` was readable by every signed-in user though no client reads it.** The discovery rails read `trending/{type}` via `rankingService.getTrending`; `trending_topics` is a server-computed topic rollup with no client reader, so the signed-in read was a needless exposure. | ✅ **now closed** — the read is scoped to `isAdmin()`. |
| N464 **`AuthContext.createUserProfile` passed `emailVerified: user.emailVerified \|\| true` — a constant `true`.** Every profile was stamped email-verified at creation regardless of the real Firebase value (and the `\|\| true` made a `false` unrepresentable). | ✅ **now closed** — carries the real value (`Boolean(user.emailVerified)`). |
| N465 **`marketplaceService.listNewProduct` seeded `rating: 0`, `reviewsCount: 0`, `salesCount: 0`.** No writer ever sets a rating or review count (there is no review pipeline), so every listing presented an unwritten `0` as a measured rating/review count; the Marketplace screen already renders "No reviews yet" when `rating` is absent. | ✅ **now closed** — the three seeds are removed; `salesCount` (which the purchase callable does increment) is created lazily by that writer and shown only when positive. |
| N466 **`videos.stats.avgWatchTime` was seeded `0` on the client and the server but never written.** The server writes `stats.totalWatchTime`, not an average; a seeded `0` presented an unwritten value as a measured average. | ✅ **now closed** — `avgWatchTime` is removed from the create seeds in `videoService.createVideo`, `functions/video.js` (`createVideo`, `createRemix`, `startLive`). |
| N467 **`pollService.getPolls` swallowed every failure and returned `[]`.** A Firestore error rendered as an empty-but-successful list ("No polls yet") and made the screen's own `catch { toast.error('Failed to load polls') }` unreachable. | ✅ **now closed** — the service rethrows after logging, so the error path and the empty state are distinct. |
| N468 **`FilterTool` collected a set of props it never read.** `previewImage`, `thumbnailStyle` (on `FilterThumbnail`), and the legacy `brightness`/`contrast`/`saturation`/`setBrightness`/`setContrast`/`setSaturation`/`adjustments`/`setAdjustment` set (plus a no-op `useEffect` on them) were destructured and documented in propTypes but read nowhere — the sole caller (`ImageEditor`) passes only `filter`/`setFilter`/`filterIntensity`/`setFilterIntensity`. | ✅ **now closed** — the dead props, their propTypes and the no-op effect are removed. |
| N469 **`src/components/Videos/VideoOverlay.jsx` was a dead component.** It was imported by no module (only re-exported from the `Videos` barrel, which nothing consumed), so it was unreachable dead weight. | ✅ **now closed** — the component and its barrel export are deleted. |
| N470 **The mutual-friends card rendered an unresolved/failed read as a measured "No mutual friends".** `profileStore.loadProfile` caught a `getMutualFriends` failure and left `mutualFriends: []` with no way to tell it apart from a resolved empty list, so the social-context card claimed "No mutual friends" / "Connect through common creators" when the read had actually failed or not yet completed. | ✅ **now closed** — a `mutualFriendsResolved` flag is set only after a successful read (and `true` for the owner view, where no mutual set exists); `ProfileSocialContext` renders the friends card only when `capabilities.canViewFriends === true && mutualFriendsResolved === true`. `profileReadModel.test.js` pins it. |
| N471 **`storyService._insertSponsoredStories` read a `users/{uid}.lastAdImpression` field no writer ever sets, and called `.toDate()` on it unguarded.** The `users/{uid}` write is denied by the rules and no server path mirrors the field, so the read always returned `undefined` — a wasted per-fetch read, a dead server-side frequency cap, and an unguarded `.toDate()` that would throw if the field ever appeared as a non-Timestamp. | ✅ **now closed** — the dead read is removed; the local (`localStorage`) frequency cap is the single, honest control. |
| N472 **`usernames/{name}` and `previous_usernames/{name}` granted `allow read: if isSignedIn()` — which also grants `list`.** Every client path is a point read (`getDoc` for share/QR resolution, the availability check), so the listable grant let any signed-in client enumerate the entire claimed-handle registry (and the old-handle rename history) over the REST API. | ✅ **now closed** — both blocks now grant `allow get: if isSignedIn();` and `allow list: if false;`. `readScopePrivacyRules.test.js` pins it. |
| N473 **`OfflineQueue.getPending` sorted `createdAt` (an ISO string) by subtraction, yielding `NaN`.** The comparator was a no-op, so the offline conflict UI's "newest first" ordering never held. | ✅ **now closed** — the comparator parses to `Date` (`new Date(b.createdAt) - new Date(a.createdAt)`); `noFabricatedData.test.js` pins it. |
| N474 **`messagesService.deleteMessage` called `msg.createdAt.toDate()` unguarded.** A stored value is usually a Timestamp, but a legacy/offline-written doc could hold an ISO string, throwing a `TypeError` that aborted the "delete for everyone" transaction mid-way (leaving `last_messages` stale). | ✅ **now closed** — the conversion is guarded (`msg.createdAt?.toDate ? … : new Date(msg.createdAt)`). |
| N475 **`storyService.createStory` seeded `stats.viewerCount: 0` and `stats.likes: 0`, neither of which any writer maintains.** Story reactions live in `reaction_shards` (rolled into `stats.reactions`) and viewers are the owner-only `viewers` list, so a seeded `0` presented an unmeasured metric as a measured zero (the insights panel already renders an absent counter as unknown). | ✅ **now closed** — both seeds are removed; only server-aggregated counters (`views`/`comments`/`shares`/`replies`/`reactions`) are seeded. `noFabricatedData.test.js` pins it. |

---

## 9. Next actions (ordered)

1. **Classify the remaining security-adjacent services** (§2.2b) as REAL CLIENT / UX-ONLY / FALSE SECURITY / DUPLICATE / DEAD, and remove the theatre with guards — **done ✅** (§10, §10.1).
2. **Consolidate the profile read model** (N018): make screens/passport consume the capability object only; never recompute privacy — **done ✅** (`src/services/profileReadModel.js` owns the derivation and the single `projectProfileForViewer` masking; the five surfaces consume it; `userService.getUserProfile` delegates too; `profileReadModel.test.js` and `userServicePrivacyProjection.test.js` freeze it).
3. **CI cost guard** (§83): fail on new `onSnapshot`/`getDocs` without `limit`, on new realtime listeners, and on new runtime dependencies — **done ✅** (§5, `firebaseCostGuard.test.js`, hard-failing `guards` CI job).
4. **Architecture guard** (§84): fail if `src/screens/**` or `src/components/**` import `firebase/firestore` directly, or if a lower layer imports UI — **done ✅**; all previous violations migrated through services and the allowlist is now empty.
5. **Reconcile the CSP** (§7.1): either apply the strict policy or delete `CSPService` and document the real policy — **done ✅** (`CSPService` deleted; the real policy is the `index.html` meta CSP).
6. **Monetization pagination** (V2-01) — **done ✅** (`getTransactionHistory`/`getCoinLeaderboard` cursor pages + `CoinsScreen` *Load more*; `monetizationPagination.test.js`).
7. **Comment debt** (§52): forbidden "TODO integrate / production would / handled later" comment patterns and decorative emoji/change-history prefixes removed from production source — **done ✅** (one corrupted line at `storyService.js` repaired).
8. **Close the residual privacy/capability divergence** (N007/N017/V2-09): `getUserProfile` now resolves only relation flags and delegates to the engine + projection; `resolveRelationshipState` has explicit precedence; `canViewProfileSection` fails closed — **done ✅** (`userServicePrivacyProjection.test.js`, `profileCapabilityEngine.test.js`, `profileReadModel.test.js`; all in the hard-failing `guards` CI job).
9. **Bound every unbounded Firestore read** (N034): hand-audited each `getDocs` in `src/services`; the genuine full-collection scans (feedService fallback branches, commentService default/unbounded fallback, community/event/live/collaboration/achievement/title/wallet/firestoreService per-user lists) now carry a hard ceiling; the cost baseline only shrank — **done ✅** (§5 audit note, `firebaseCostGuard.test.js`).
10. **Consolidate duplicate profile reads**: `reputationService` read `users/{uid}` a second time and carried its own score-clamp copy; it now reads through `userService.getUserProfile` and uses the canonical `profileReadModel.readStandingScore` (same helper `passportService` now shares) — **done ✅** (`profileReadModel.test.js`).
11. **Make every privacy scope real**: `profileInfo` was a dead control (settable, never enforced, and `AboutScreen` rendered the card with no projection). `resolveCapabilities` now derives `canViewIdentity`, the projection masks the identity card, and `AboutScreen` consumes it — **done ✅** (N035; `profileReadModel.test.js`, `userServicePrivacyProjection.test.js`).
12. **Stop the location leak**: full-precision GPS coordinates were written to the world-readable `users/{uid}` document and `locationPrivacy: 'hidden'` only blanked the display string. The contract now rounds coordinates (~1 km), clears them for `hidden`/`country_only`, and keeps only the country for `country_only` — **done ✅** (N036; `profileSystemHardening.test.js`).
13. **Make the `creatorInfo` scope real** (N038): the creator-suite fields were writable but rendered nowhere, so the business contact was world-readable with no control. `resolveCapabilities` now derives `canViewCreatorInfo`, the projection masks `CREATOR_INFO_FIELDS`, `AboutScreen` renders the suite, and both editors expose the scope — **done ✅** (`profileReadModel.test.js`, `profileCapabilityEngine.test.js`).
14. **Gate every money field, not just `coins`** (N039): the projection spread left `earnings`/`totalEarned`/`coinBalance`/… on the object when economic status was denied. All `ECONOMIC_FIELDS` are now nulled — **done ✅** (`profileReadModel.test.js`).
15. **Collapse the passport onto the canonical read path**: `passportService` read `users/{uid}` directly and applied capabilities only as decoration. It now reads through `userService.getUserProfile` and applies `projectProfileForViewer` once — **done ✅** (`profileReadModel.test.js`).
16. **Make post view analytics real** (N073): the PostCard author panel's "Views" row read a counter nothing wrote — the client write path (`recordPostView`) was denied by the posts `{subcollection}` rule and had no call site, and the parallel `analyticsService.post_analytics` subsystem was client-write-denied and unwired. Counting is now a server-authoritative `recordPostView` callable, the view subcollections are server-write-only, and `PostCard` records the impression — **done ✅** (`integrationWiring.test.js`).
17. **Wire the `onAnalytics` prop** (N074): the PostCard sub-cards and `PostOptionsDrawer` emitted interaction events into a handler that was a no-op (`() => {}`) or absent, and `VideoCard` beaconed to a nonexistent `/api/analytics/batch` endpoint. The emits now flow to a real sink (`analyticsService.trackAnalyticsEvent`/`trackAnalyticsBatch` → `metricsService`), both drawers receive a stable handler, and the phantom beacon is gone — **done ✅** (`integrationWiring.test.js`, `analyticsInteractionSink.test.js`).
18. **Sync AudioCard state on external pause** (N075): the `<audio>` `onPause` was a `() => {}` no-op, so an OS-media-control / watchdog / visibility pause left `isPlaying` true and the button stuck on "Pause". `handlePause` now calls `GlobalAudioManager.syncStateFromElement` — **done ✅** (`integrationWiring.test.js`).
19. **Remove dead props** (N076): the three delegating connections screens passed a `currentUser` prop `ConnectionsScreen` never read (a store-mirror identity, the N002 shape) and `IntroScreen` passed a `size` prop `ThemeToggle` never read; both dropped, plus the unused `getNextTheme`/`PropTypes` in `ThemeToggle` — **done ✅** (`noFabricatedData.test.js`, `integrationWiring.test.js`).
20. **Make post-embedded polls votable** (N077): `PostCard` never passed `onVote`, the writer keyed on `option.id` over string options, and the composer's `closesAt`/`rewardCoins` were dropped by a reducer that had no such cases — **done ✅** (`integrationWiring.test.js`).
21. **Give post-embedded Q&A a real backend** (N078): `QuestionCard` was a complete answer UI with no `answers` service, no handlers and no rules; the composer stored `question` as a string the card could not read. Answers now live on `posts/{id}.question.answers`, mutated only by the `functions/questions.js` callables, with `PostCard` wiring every handler and the viewer's own votes resolved on load — **done ✅** (`integrationWiring.test.js`, `xpWiring.test.js`).
22. **Fix the notifications dead-wiring cluster** (N088): `subscribeToUserNotifications` emits a change batch `{ type:'update', added, modified, subscriptionId }` but the screen only accepted a bare array, so every Firestore notification was dropped; the screen's icon/gift/Live logic also matched legacy aliases (`post_like`/`coin_gift`/`live_stream`) while the service emits `like`/`gift_received`/`live_started`; and `handleMarkRead` only flipped local state so the server unread counter never cleared — **done ✅** (`notificationsWiringGuard.test.js`).
23. **Fail closed on like/unlike writes** (N089): `firestoreService.likePost`/`unlikePost` swallowed a failed transaction and returned `{ success:true, localFallback:true }`, so a like that never persisted (permission error, quota, rate limit) rendered as saved — the caller had no signal to roll back the optimistic heart. The inner swallow-catch and the rate-limit soft-pass are gone; a failure now returns `success:false` and `PostCard` rolls back — **done ✅** (`failOpenReadGuard.test.js`). The device-local like registry is now uid-scoped (`readLocalLikes`/`setLocalLike`) so a like on a shared device no longer bleeds into the next account.
24. **Fail closed on feed reads** (N090): `feedService._getLatestFeed`, `videoService.getVideoFeed` and `storyService.getHighlights` returned `{ success:true, feed:[] }` from a catch, so an outage was cached as "no posts" and stopped pagination. All three now surface `success:false` with the error — **done ✅** (`failOpenReadGuard.test.js`).
25. **Delete the dead highlights carousel** (N091): `NotificationsScreen` rendered a "Highlights" carousel from `notifications.filter((n) => n.highlight)`, but no service ever sets a `highlight` field, so the section (and its `/profile/highlights` link) was unreachable UI. Removed; the real highlights remain owned by `HighlightsScreen` via `storyService.getHighlights` — **done ✅** (`highlightsWiringGuard.test.js`).
26. **Trending hashtags were fabricated counts** (N092): with no Algolia index configured, `searchService.getFacetValues('posts','hashtags')` returned a hardcoded list (`arvdoul:120`, `trending:98`, `music:64`, …) that `TrendingSection` rendered as "*N* posts". The numbers were constants no query produced. The fallback now aggregates the real `hashtags` array off a bounded page of public, published posts and returns the counts that sample actually contains (empty when there is nothing) — **done ✅** (`noFabricatedData.test.js`).

## 10. §11 shadow-system classification ledger

Every candidate named by the directive, classified. **REAL CLIENT CONTROL** = the
browser is genuinely the enforcement point; **UX-ONLY** = useful client-side
ergonomics that must never be trusted as a control; **FALSE SECURITY** = advertises
a control it cannot hold (removed); **DEAD** = no reachable caller.

| Service | Classification | Evidence / action |
|---|---|---|
| `WAFService` | FALSE SECURITY (removed ✅) | client regex; attacker skips the client |
| `CSRFService` | FALSE SECURITY (removed ✅) | Firebase Auth uses bearer ID tokens, no ambient cookie to forge |
| `DDoSProtectionService` | FALSE SECURITY (removed ✅) | client token bucket cannot scrub a network flood |
| `sessionSecurityService` | FALSE SECURITY (removed ✅) | IP/geo/impossible-travel are server-only signals |
| `CSPService` | FALSE SECURITY (removed ✅) | CSP builder never applied; real policy is the `index.html` meta tag |
| `SecureHeadersService` | FALSE SECURITY (removed ✅) | header map never sent; real headers in `firebase.json` |
| `challengeService` | FALSE SECURITY (removed ✅) | client proof-of-work; real bot control is App Check |
| `botProtectionService` | FALSE SECURITY (removed ✅) | client entropy scoring the attacker's automation controls |
| `userIntegrityService` | FALSE SECURITY (removed ✅) | client trust/strike/sybil decisions, no server enforcement |
| `sanitizationService` | DEAD (removed ✅) | React escapes output; no `dangerouslySetInnerHTML` consumer; no caller |
| `fieldEncryptionService` | DEAD (removed ✅) | real WebCrypto, but no caller; key lifecycle undefined; PII belongs in `users_private` server-side |
| `apiSecurityGatewayService` | FALSE SECURITY (removed ✅) | client-generated, client-stored API keys that the same client validates; a client cannot be the authority over its own key. Server-side issuance required. Dead `api_keys` rule removed. |
| `searchAbuseService` | FALSE SECURITY (removed ✅) | client rate limiting + client "CAPTCHA" on search; an attacker skips the client. Server `rateLimit` owns this. |
| `fraudDetectionService` | WRONG TIER (removed ✅) | detection logic is genuine but must run server-side with server-held signals; the inert client copy was removed |
| `manipulatedMediaService` | WRONG TIER (removed ✅) | detector belongs on upload (server), not in the viewer's browser; the inert client copy was removed |

WRONG-TIER logic that must run server-side belongs in `functions/` (where it is
enforced). The inert client copies were removed; if the server-side detector is
built later it lives there, not in `src/services`.

### 10.1 Client-side infra-control services — all removed ✅

These were imported only by their own tests and held no real control. **Every
service in this table has been deleted** (see §2.2); the enforced paths are
`firestore.rules`, `functions/rateLimit.js`, Firebase App Check and Cloud
Logging/Monitoring.

| Service | Classification | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| `textModerationService`, `imageModerationService`, `videoModerationService`, `audioModerationService`, `liveModerationService` | WRONG TIER (removed ✅) | detectors ran in the browser and were unenforced; the enforced path is `functions/moderation.js` `moderatePost` + `reportContent` |
| `childSafetyService`, `copyrightDetectionService`, `extremismDetectionService`, `selfHarmDetectionService`, `scamDetectionService`, `phishingDetectionService`, `misinformationService`, `safeSearchService`, `searchIndexingService` | WRONG TIER (removed ✅) | classification belongs on the server; a client can bypass its own detector |
| `multiRegionMeshService`, `activeActiveService`, `chaosDefenseService`, `disasterRecoveryService` | DEAD (removed ✅) | client-side failover/chaos simulation cannot route real infrastructure; Google Cloud owns regional failover |
| `immutableAuditLedgerService`, `verifiableCredentialsService` | DEAD (removed ✅) | client-computed hash chain / credential signature is not tamper-evident against the client that computes it |
| `observabilityService`, `alertingService`, `tracingService`, `logAggregationService`, `incidentService`, `costMonitoringService`, `costOptimizationService` | DEAD (removed ✅) | operated only in the browser; real telemetry is Cloud Logging + Cloud Monitoring |
| `AggregationCacheService`, `CacheInvalidationService`, `RedisCacheManager`(stub part), `TTLOptimizationService`, `realIntegration` | DEAD (removed ✅) | in-memory client caches with no caller; Redis is not reachable from the browser |
| `digitalEscrowService`, `revenueSplitsService`, `decentralized*Service`, `activityPubMeshService`, `watchPartyService`, `spacesOrchestrator`, `liveInteractiveGamificationService`, `communityGovernanceService`, `contentProvenanceService`, `recommendationEngine`, `predictiveAnalyticsService`, `viralPredictionService`, `aiCoPilotDirectorService`, `vendorManagementService`, `complianceGovernanceService` | DEAD (removed ✅) | no caller; logic depends on data the client does not hold |
| `samlService` | DEAD / WRONG TIER (removed ✅) | SAML assertion validation is an IdP/server concern; the enforced path is `functions/saml.js` |
| `validationService` | DEAD (removed ✅) | duplicated `profileContracts` / `firestore.rules`; never a substitute for rules |

Removal policy: FALSE SECURITY and DEAD services are deleted, guarded by
`src/__tests__/securityServices.test.js` (theatre ban) and
`src/__tests__/serviceReachability.test.js` (no unreachable service). WRONG-TIER
logic moves behind a Cloud Function — it does not stay as an inert client copy.

## 11. Verification commands

```
NODE_OPTIONS=--experimental-vm-modules npm test        # 117 suites / 1361 tests, green
NODE_OPTIONS=--experimental-vm-modules npm test -- --coverage   # coverage thresholds pass
npm run build                                          # vite build succeeds (363 JS chunks, 15 MB dist, 4.2 s)
npm run lint                                           # 0 errors (warnings expected)
node scripts/sync-shared-config.mjs                    # shared config copy in sync
node -e "require('./scripts/firebaseCostAnalyzer.cjs').analyze('.')"  # cost snapshot (see §5)
```

The honest test count is 933, not the 1127 reported before the dead-service
removal: ~238 of the old tests loaded services the app never runs, and their
coverage of live modules (`metricsService`, `IdempotencyKey`, `soundService`) has
been replaced with real tests (`metricsService.test.js`, `idempotencyKey.test.js`,
extended `soundService.test.js`).

### 11.1 §58 performance evidence (measured, not claimed)

Measured on this branch with `vite build`:

| Signal | Value |
|---|---|
| Production chunks | 363 JS files |
| Largest chunks | `ImageEditor` 1010 KB, `index.esm` 560 KB, `dist` 416 KB, `index` 394 KB |
| `dist/` total | 15 MB |
| Build time | 4.2 s |
| Client listeners | 31 sites / 15 files (frozen) |
| Unbounded `getDocs` | 115 sites / 19 files (frozen; userService is 0 after the graph-spine work) |

> The `dist/` total and chunk count rose versus the earlier 307-chunk/8.8 MB note
> because the working tree has grown; the numbers above are the ones measured
> now. Chunk-level code-splitting debt (the 1 MB `ImageEditor` chunk) is tracked,
> not hidden.

Vite flagged three `INEFFECTIVE_DYNAMIC_IMPORT` warnings where a module is both
statically and dynamically imported (`src/i18n/index.js`, `src/utils/OfflineQueue.js`,
`src/services/levelSystemService.js`). The dynamic import in those cases does not
create a separate chunk — the module is already in the entry graph. This is a
known, harmless pattern (the dynamic call sites are for code paths that must not
await at module-eval time); it is recorded here rather than asserted as an
optimisation, because no measurement shows it costs anything.
